# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented here.
The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Changed
- **The Content Visual Designer no longer grades its templates against invented adoption, engagement and satisfaction figures — every read now runs against your own prior baseline** (anti-fabrication sweep, third design persona; both dual-located copies of [`design/design-content-visual-designer.md`](design/design-content-visual-designer.md); no count change, still **85 agents / 19 skills**). Six of the eight Success Metrics asserted unsourced round numbers as results — a "70-80%" self-service adoption target, "80%+" template reuse, a "50% reduction" in turnaround, "2-3x higher engagement" for templated content, a "30-50% reduction" in custom requests, and a "4.5+/5" satisfaction rating — the same borrowed-benchmark defect the ad-creative, brand-identity, social, analytics, email, sales and paid sweeps already removed. All six are now own-baseline reads with a rubric intro and no figure asserted: **Self-Service Adoption** and **Template Reuse** are measured from the library's own usage against the team's pre-template baseline (the arbitrary thresholds dropped); **Content Production Velocity** and **Designer Time Savings** are timed before/after against this team's own baseline with the delta treated as shared with concurrent tooling and staffing changes; **Content Engagement Lift** is read against the brand's own comparable non-templated assets with the selection confound named and causal credit routed to `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`; and **Template User Satisfaction** becomes a survey read against your own prior wave that surfaces specific complaints rather than averaging them into a score. The two genuinely **controllable** bars — **Brand Consistency Score** (guideline compliance) and **Design System Completeness** (template coverage) — keep their targets, since the agent owns them through template clarity and enforcement, matching the controllable-process-bar rule the sweep has held throughout. Lint 2/2, both copies byte-identical, all six fabricated figures now appear only in negation context, 0 broken links. **Sweep status:** design continues one persona per run — `design-presentation-designer` and `design-ui-landing-page-specialist` still to scan, then the project-management nuanced case.
- **The Messaging Architect no longer grades its own work against invented benchmarks: five Success Metrics that asserted round-number results as targets are re-based on your own prior data** (both dual-located copies of [`product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md`](product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md); no count change, still **85 agents / 19 skills**). The 2026-09-11 anti-fabrication sweep of product marketing missed this persona, and a full read of its Success Metrics found five bullets stating unsourced figures as outcomes the messaging would produce: *Prospect Perception Shift* (`40%+ improvement`), *Customer Testimonial Effectiveness* (`25%+ higher engagement`), *Website Conversion Impact* (`20-35%`), *Email Campaign Performance* (`15-25%`), and *Sales Messaging Effectiveness* (`15-20% higher win rates`). All five retired to the repo's established baseline/trend/powered-comparison pattern with **no figure asserted** — pre-post reads against your own pre-exposure baseline (small or unpowered studies labeled and never rounded to a shift), powered before/after or A/B tests routed to `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`, causal conversion credit routed to `paid-media-attribution-analyst`, and the self-selection confound named on the win-rate gap (message-users may already run better-qualified deals, so an uncontrolled gap over-credits the message). The controllable and completion bars were kept verbatim (`90%+` comprehension, `80%+`/`70%+` usage measured via call review, `85%+` consistency, the `100%`/zero-defect gates), exactly as the other swept agents kept theirs. Both copies byte-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grep to zero; 0 broken links.

### Added
- **The Messaging Architect's consistency audit now reaches the one surface it could never read side by side — the live sales call** (both dual-located copies of [`product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md`](product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md); no count change, still **85 agents / 19 skills**). The surface-against-surface audit reconciles *written* surfaces (website, decks, email, ads, content) by reading them side by side — which structurally excludes what a rep actually *says* on a discovery call or demo, the highest-stakes surface and the one that reverts hardest to the pre-repositioning story because it lives in reps' heads, not a CMS. Weeks after every written surface agrees, the field can still be selling the old category out loud, and the side-by-side audit can't see it. Added one paragraph: audit the spoken surface by *sampling* recent call/demo recordings against the same four axes, distinct from the `Message Usage in Sales` metric (which asks only whether a pillar *appears*, not whether the *current* narrative has replaced the previous one), from `pmm-launch-manager` (the launch-scoped adoption-past-novelty read), and from `sales-enablement-content-creator` (the training and talk-track assets). Grep-verified the rest of the lead was already owned before shipping only the unowned sliver. Ideas-only from the `narrative/` tree of [aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills](https://github.com/aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills) (Apache-2.0); boundary lines ours, no text reused. Both copies byte-identical; lint 2/2; 0 broken links.
- **The Product Launch Manager now owns the ladder before GA: private preview, public beta and general availability are three different promises, and each one caps what marketing may claim and what sales may sell** (both dual-located copies of [`product-marketing/pmm-launch-manager.md`](product-marketing/pmm-launch-manager.md); no count change, still **85 agents / 19 skills**). `general availability`, `public preview`, `private preview`, `early-access` and `release stage` all grepped to **zero** across the 85 agents; the launch manager assumed every launch was finished. Added Rule 10 (name the stage before sizing the launch; a beta dressed as GA is a contract dispute, not a missed metric) and a *Before GA* section: stage definitions cited to Google Cloud's launch stages and Microsoft's Preview Supplemental Terms; a stage-caps-the-tier table (what may be said, where, what sales may put in writing); a sales-does-not-sell-the-roadmap seam to the deal, proposal and solutions agents; an early-access program designed for one stated purpose (learn, prove or build references), selected on ICP fit, sized in waves, with participation terms and a separate, optional reference question; the explicit GA opt-out decision; graduation as an evidence gate, not a date; and a review date so a preview can't turn into a permanent beta. New deliverable (Release-Stage Plan & Early-Access Charter) and two metrics you control (Stage Integrity, Early-Access Loop Closure). Ideas from `early-access-designer` in [aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills](https://github.com/aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills) (Apache-2.0), rewritten for a sales-led B2B context; no text reused.
- **The Pipeline Analyst now reads coverage as a clock, not a constant: the multiple you can still act on falls as the quarter runs, and a flat monthly inflow target no longer gets misread as being behind or ahead** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-pipeline-analyst.md`](sales/sales-pipeline-analyst.md); no count change, still **85 agents / 19 skills**). The agent already derived coverage from win rate and cycle and segmented it — but it treated coverage as one static planning number: `point of no return` grepped to **zero** and seasonality was absent entirely. Added two sub-blocks to the Pipeline Coverage Analysis deliverable:
  - **Coverage decays within the period — the point of no return.** A segment's point of no return is period length minus its median sales cycle; pipeline created after it cannot close in-period, so from there the number that matters is late-stage coverage of the *remaining* quota, not total coverage of the original target. Read coverage weekly and run the gap analysis early while generation is still a live lever; past the point of no return only acceleration or an honest re-forecast remains. It is a per-segment property — a short-cycle SMB motion recovers a mid-period gap the same math says a long-cycle enterprise motion cannot.
  - **Seasonality: index inflow targets to your own history.** A flat month-by-month creation target reads "behind" every slow month and "ahead" every strong one even when nothing is wrong; build a seasonal index from the team's own closed-won and creation history and shape the monthly targets to it, anchored to your and your buyers' fiscal calendars — and re-derive a segment's coverage target when its win rate moves, since the required multiple is only ever 1 ÷ that segment's own conversion.
  Ideas-only from [mbfinotti/sales-skills](https://github.com/mbfinotti/sales-skills)' `sales-pipeline-coverage-modeling` (MIT, licence verified via the GitHub API at read time 2026-09-25; no text adapted); its circulating figures (the "~20% closes in-period" and "4 turns nominal ≈ 1 turn credible" numbers) were deliberately not imported, holding the agent's own measure-your-own rule. Lint 2/2; both copies byte-identical; 0 broken links.
- **The boundary around AI answers now reads the same from all three seats: the SEO and Product Marketing agents now point back to the PPC Strategist, so a reader landing on either learns where the paid line is** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md) and [`product-marketing/pmm-agent-readiness-strategist.md`](product-marketing/pmm-agent-readiness-strategist.md); no count change, still **85 agents / 19 skills**). The [2026-09-25 PPC add](paid-media/paid-media-ppc-strategist.md) drew the three-way seam — getting *cited* in an AI answer is organic AEO (`seo-ai-search-optimizer`), being *transacted with* by a buying agent is `pmm-agent-readiness-strategist`, and the ads that run *alongside* an answer are the PPC Strategist's — but only the paid side stated it, so the boundary was invisible to anyone reading the other two first (grep-verified: neither agent mentioned `paid-media`, `advertiser`, `sponsored` or `media budget`). Added one in-voice sentence to each: `seo-ai-search-optimizer`'s **"What this does not buy"** section now says entity work does not make you *purchasable* into the answer — no advertiser buys a slot inside the model's response, so an "AI answer placement" pitch arriving on a media budget is this organic work under a paid invoice, while the paid units alongside the answer are `paid-media-ppc-strategist`'s desk; and `pmm-agent-readiness-strategist`'s **Rule 10** now carries the paid-side seam mirroring its existing direction-of-travel distinctions — a human reading a marked sponsored unit next to an answer is being advertised to, the opposite direction of travel from a buyer's agent transacting with us, so neither a paid placement nor an organic citation is that role's readiness work. Lint 4/4; both agents' copies byte-identical; 0 broken links.
- **The PPC Strategist now covers the AI answer surface — including the placement your account is already buying and cannot see** (both dual-located copies of [`paid-media/paid-media-ppc-strategist.md`](paid-media/paid-media-ppc-strategist.md); no count change, still **85 agents / 19 skills**). `ChatGPT`, `Perplexity`, `Copilot` and `AI Overviews` appeared nowhere in any of the seven paid-media agents, and `conversational ad`, `AI search ad` and `sponsored answer` returned **zero** repo-wide — the repo covered getting *cited* in AI answers (organic, `seo-ai-search-optimizer`) and being *transacted with* by buying agents (`pmm-agent-readiness-strategist`), but not buying media on the surface. Added Critical Rule 12, a new **"The AI Answer Surface"** section, an **AI Answer Surface Buy Sheet** deliverable, and five benchmark-free Success Metrics. The substance:
  - **Three different things get called "AI ads" and only one is a buy decision** — AI-powered campaign types (already governed by Rules 9–10); placements inside Google's AI experiences, which Google's own help states you cannot opt out of, cannot target, and cannot see segmented reporting for; and separate opt-in assistant platforms (ChatGPT Ads, Copilot). Plus the fourth thing that is not advertising at all: getting into the answer itself, which both OpenAI and Microsoft state advertisers cannot influence — so "we'll get you into the AI answer" on a media budget is AEO under a paid invoice, or nothing.
  - **No segment means no claim.** Because Google publishes no AI Overviews placement breakout, any CTR/CPA figure asserted about it is unfalsifiable in the platform that sold it — the agent now refuses the claim and watches aggregate drift on head commercial terms instead, routing causality to `paid-media-attribution-analyst`.
  - **The B2B reachability gate.** OpenAI states ads serve to Free and Go plans and that *"Plus, Pro, Business, Enterprise, and Edu accounts will not have ads"* — so a buyer on a company-provisioned seat is structurally unreachable, and the audience you *can* buy is the inverse of an enterprise ICP. Decided before budget, not in a footnote.
  - **The control surface this file is built on is missing.** No keywords, no match types, no search-term report, no negatives — context hints are *"not exact-match keywords or targeting rules"* — so a hard campaign-total budget replaces the negative-keyword loop as the waste cap.
  - **Target-account lists do not fit; suppression does.** Inclusion audiences require at least 25,000 matched users (a B2B list will not clear it) while exclusions work at small sizes — so suppress customers and open opportunities on day one.
  - **Your robots.txt can silently disqualify a paid channel.** OpenAI requires OAI-AdsBot to reach the landing page and says *"do not rely on a manual bypass"* — a deliberate AI-crawler block made by another team, in another quarter, now blocks ad review, with `seo-technical-auditor` owning the fix and the site's crawler-policy owner the decision.
  - **No benchmark, in either direction** — OpenAI states it has none, so circulating "conversational ads outperform search by X%" figures are cited as unaudited vendor claims or not at all. Campaign-type eligibility for **AI Mode** specifically is flagged as contested and deliberately not asserted.
  Grounded entirely in primary advertiser documentation from Google, OpenAI and Microsoft (read 2026-09-25, quoted with URLs); collection surveyed for structure only, ideas-only with no text adapted, from [mbfinotti/advertising-skills](https://github.com/mbfinotti/advertising-skills) (MIT). Lint 2/2; both copies byte-identical; 0 broken links.
- **The Pipeline Analyst now closes the books on the funnel: a stage where deals silently vanish — never contacted, sitting in a queue nobody owns, advanced in an inbox — is now detectable instead of noticed by accident** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-pipeline-analyst.md`](sales/sales-pipeline-analyst.md); no count change, still **85 agents / 19 skills**). The agent already counted advances, losses and disqualifications per stage, but nothing reconciled them — so records that left through *none* of the counted outcomes were invisible, and a healthy disqualification, a real leak and a data-capture gap all read the same. Added Critical Rule 10, a **"Pipeline Conservation"** diagnostic section, and a benchmark-free **Funnel Conservation** metric: reconcile each transition as `entered = advanced + lost + disqualified + still-open + residual`; classify every residual three ways (real leak / healthy disqualification / data-capture gap) before calling it a leak; treat any manual inbox/spreadsheet step as a suspected leak site until instrumented; and size a leak in *recoverable* dollars, never the gross value of the stalled deals (the trap latent in "revenue at risk = sum of stalled deals"). Draws the seam to `analytics-performance-analyst`'s Movement Accounting explicitly — this is the funnel-*flow* twin of that agent's customer/revenue-*stock* reconciliation, kept separate and never merged. The circulating speed-to-lead multipliers ("5 min = 100x", "21x", "391%/60-second") are flagged as uncited vendor claims, with HBR 2011 *The Short Life of Online Sales Leads* named as the real, non-multiplier evidence — measure your own curve. Ideas-only from [mbfinotti/revops-skills](https://github.com/mbfinotti/revops-skills)' `revenue-leakage` (MIT, licence re-verified via the GitHub API at read time; no text adapted); Bowtie lens credited to Winning by Design, "Paper Process" leak to MEDDPICC. Lint 2/2; both copies byte-identical; 0 broken links.
- **Affiliate payout execution now has an owner: the money leaving your company to affiliates is reconciled and paid by the Financial Tracker, not lost in the seam between it and Partnerships** (both dual-located copies of [`partnerships/partner-ecosystem-marketer.md`](partnerships/partner-ecosystem-marketer.md) and [`client-ops/ops-financial-tracker.md`](client-ops/ops-financial-tracker.md); no count change, still **85 agents / 19 skills**). `partner-ecosystem-marketer` Rule 11 already owned affiliate program *design* — commission model, clawback policy, attribution window, fraud controls, disclosure — but the later *execution* step had no owner: `payout run` grepped to **zero**, `reconcil` in that agent meant only attribution-method reconciliation, and `chargeback` appeared only as clawback-policy design, never a payment run. So reconciling and de-duplicating payouts against the CRM before money moves, multi-currency conversion and withholding-tax, and rate/tier verification could each fall through the seam — the failure mode where a program pays the wrong affiliate, double-pays a referral, or mishandles tax because nobody was named to run the payment. Fixed with two reciprocal sentences (not a new agent): Rule 11(c) now hands the qualified, settled, fraud-screened payout list to `ops-financial-tracker` for the payables mechanics, and Rule 3 of `ops-financial-tracker` accepts that run as an extension of its vendor-payment discipline — while the terms that decide *who is owed what* stay with partnerships. Concept surfaced from [mbfinotti/partnerships-skills](https://github.com/mbfinotti/partnerships-skills)' `affiliate-payout-audit` (MIT, re-verified at read time; ideas only, no text adapted). Lint 4/4; both agents' copies byte-identical; 0 broken links.
- **New agent: the Channel Program Architect. Eighty-four agents marketed *through* partners; nobody designed the program they run inside** (new [`partnerships/partner-channel-program-architect.md`](partnerships/partner-channel-program-architect.md), dual-located at `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/partnerships-ops/agents/`; roster **84 → 85 agents**, Partnerships 1 → 2, skills stay 19 and disciplines 17). `channel conflict`, `rules of engagement`, `partner recruitment`, `partner onboarding`, `partner portal`, `SPIF`, `systems integrator`, `OEM` and `white label` all returned **zero** across the roster before writing; [`partner-ecosystem-marketer`](partnerships/partner-ecosystem-marketer.md) owns marketing *with and through* partners, and the program those partners operate inside had no owner. The new agent's rules:
  - **A readiness gate that is allowed to answer "not yet."** Direct motion not repeatable, ACV too low to fund human-heavy partner work, gross margin that cannot fund partner margin, no named executive sponsor, no owner with committed capacity — name the disqualifier, name the lighter motion that still fits, and stop.
  - **Compensation neutrality outranks every other decision.** If a direct rep earns less on a partner-touched deal, the rules of engagement will be worked around; the decision goes into comp-plan language or the structural boundaries carry the load instead.
  - **A claim that lives only in a partner portal is not a claim** — registrations in the CRM system of record, with the protection window sized from the actual median sales cycle and the tie-break and rejection grounds published in advance.
  - **Conflict is governed before it is manifest**, using the vertical/horizontal/multichannel typology from the *Marketing Channels* literature and the latent → perceived → felt → manifest progression, with a named adjudicator who is not the direct sales line alone.
  - **Partner CAC compared to direct per dollar of net revenue, never per customer**, with the accounting convention stated and never mixed.
  - **The partner's P&L modelled, not only ours**, and margin stacking treated as an economic effect (Spengler's double marginalization, 1950; the two-part tariff fix) rather than partner greed.
  - **Attributed is not incremental** — the eBay brand-keyword field experiments (Blake, Nosko & Tadelis, *Econometrica*, 2015) as the standing warning about partner-attached revenue on accounts you already sell to.
  - **Untraceable channel benchmarks refused**, with each figure's evidence class graded and `[NEEDS INPUT: …]` in place of a plausible number.
  - **The ramp designed backwards from the partner's first registered deal**, and a certification that gates nothing called a badge.
  - **Margin is this agent's instrument; MDF is not** — margin never funds a campaign, MDF never funds margin, and discount floors stay inside the pricing approval matrix.

  Ideas credited in-file to [mbfinotti/partnerships-skills](https://github.com/mbfinotti/partnerships-skills) (MIT) and [gtmagents/gtm-agents](https://github.com/gtmagents/gtm-agents) (Apache-2.0); written from scratch in this repo's voice. No channel benchmark is asserted. Counts updated on every surface: README badge and prose, `AGENTS_INDEX.md`, `llms.txt`, `CITATION.cff`, both manifests, the suite router, the CATALYST orchestrator and strategy tables, and the GitHub About description.
- **The Deal Strategist now plans a displacement backward from the notice window, not the contract expiry — and knows where the contract-clock facts come from** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-deal-strategist.md`](sales/sales-deal-strategist.md); no count change, still **84 agents / 19 skills**). The mutual-action-plan section already told the strategist to plan backward from a "contract expiry," but a raw expiry misleads: an incumbent's notice period and auto-renewal mechanism close the real window earlier, so a plan anchored on the expiry can run backward from a date the buyer has already lost the right to act on. Added one grep-verified-absent, in-voice paragraph naming the four qualification fields ([`pmm-competitive-intelligence`](product-marketing/pmm-competitive-intelligence.md)'s Rule 12 — term end, notice period and auto-renewal mechanism, early-termination terms, signature authority) as inputs the strategist **consumes rather than derives** — gathered per account in that agent's **Displacement Readiness Read** — while what those terms legally entitle the buyer to do stays [`ops-legal-compliance`](client-ops/ops-legal-compliance.md)'s to say. Completes the reciprocal half of the seam the competitive agent's Rule 12 already named ("the deal is not yours to run … is `sales-deal-strategist`'s"). Lint 2/2, both copies diff-identical, all cross-refs resolve, 0 broken links.
- **New agent: the Marketing AI Operations Lead. Your team uses AI in more places than anyone has listed, and now someone owns that list** (new [`analytics/analytics-marketing-ai-ops-lead.md`](analytics/analytics-marketing-ai-ops-lead.md), dual-located in `marketing-analytics`; 83 → 84 agents).
  - **An AI use register kept per use case, not per tool**, with the tool and model, the data that goes in, where the output goes, its autonomy level, its human gate, its owner and its last eval.
  - **Legalize, don't ban.** A shadow-AI amnesty and intake with four outcomes (sanctioned / approved / restricted / replaced) and a declared response window.
  - **"Can I paste this in?" answered in writing.** Four data classes crossed with tool tiers built from each vendor's verified, dated terms.
  - **An autonomy ladder** (assist → draft → bounded-auto → autonomous) that each workflow earns on evidence, and a list of things AI never generates: prices, contract terms, assurances, customer quotes, performance claims, roadmap dates.
  - **Eval sets re-run whenever the model, prompt or vendor changes**, with a scheduled canary where the model can't be pinned.
  - **Default-on platform AI inventoried**: auto-generated ad assets, auto-applied recommendations, ESP and CRM AI features.
  - **Kill switches and an AI incident log** that fixes the gate, not just the output. **An AI-literacy record by role** for EU AI Act Art. 4 as amended in July 2026.

  Ideas credited to NEON-Rutger/B2B-revops-skills, matteotitta/genesys-skills and wshobson/agents (all MIT). No benchmark is asserted.
- **New agent: the Public Sector Marketing Strategist. Eighty-two agents, and nobody owned marketing to government buyers** (new [`product-marketing/pmm-public-sector-strategist.md`](product-marketing/pmm-public-sector-strategist.md), dual-located at `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/product-marketing-ops/agents/`; roster **82 → 83 agents**, Product Marketing 9 → 10, skills stay 19 and disciplines 17). `public sector`, `FedRAMP`, `government` and `solutions marketing` all returned zero across the roster. The new agent's rules:
  - **Readiness before visibility.** No government page, campaign or booth until three things exist: a written buying path, an accurate security status, and a current accessibility conformance report.
  - **Authorization status quoted exactly from the FedRAMP Marketplace or GovRAMP listing, with the date read.** The FedRAMP designation names are being revised in 2026, so labels are never hard-coded. *FedRAMP compliant* and *FedRAMP-ready* are never written.
  - **The VPAT-based accessibility conformance report treated as a claim.**
  - **Federal gift limits ($20 per occasion, $50 per year, 5 CFR 2635.204(a)) tracked per official across the whole company,** because gifts from different employees of one company count together.
  - **No implied government endorsement** (5 CFR 2635.702(c); GSAR 552.203-71).
  - **Marketing effort in the pre-solicitation window** (FAR 15.201(a)), with a **solicitation freeze list** once the contracting officer becomes the focal point (15.201(f)).
  - **Campaigns planned against the buyer's fiscal year.**
  - **A how-to-buy page that lists only contract vehicles you are actually on.**

  Every fact is cited to a primary source read 2026-09-19. No benchmark is asserted.
- **New agent: the Customer Education Strategist — eighty-one agents, and nobody owned the course that teaches customers to use the product** (new [`content/content-customer-education-strategist.md`](content/content-customer-education-strategist.md), dual-located at `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/content-marketing/agents/`; roster **81 → 82 agents**, Content Marketing 8 → 9, skills stay 19 and disciplines 17). The academy, the onboarding curriculum and the certification program had no owner anywhere in the roster — `customer education`, `academy`, `curriculum`, `learner` and `learning management` all returned zero — while the docs agent owns documentation, the customer-marketing lead owns adoption plays and the enablement agents train sellers, not customers. It ships the freshness register that re-records every lesson a product release breaks (a stale course is not old, it is wrong to someone following it step by step), the gate decision per asset with its measurement consequence written beside the lead-capture one, a credential built as a verifiable, dated, revocable asset rather than a rendered PDF (1EdTech Open Badges 3.0; HubSpot's published one-year/two-year validity rule as a precedent for setting your own), an exam blueprint with a defended pass mark and a ban on grading the program by pass rate, `Course` structured data and the query boundary that stops the academy cannibalising the docs and the blog, learner records treated as personal data before marketing signal, and a standing refusal to claim education caused retention without a controlled comparison — because the people who finish a six-hour certification had already decided to build a career on your product. Asserts no benchmark anywhere.
- **New agent: the Conversational Agent Strategist — eighty agents, and nobody owned the one marketing surface that improvises** (new [`sales/sales-conversational-agent-strategist.md`](sales/sales-conversational-agent-strategist.md), dual-located at [`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/sales-enablement/agents/`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/sales-enablement/agents/sales-conversational-agent-strategist.md); roster **80 → 81 agents**, skills stay 19, disciplines stay 17). The website AI chat agent, inbound AI SDR or live chat answers anonymous buyers in real time with nobody reviewing it first, and `chatbot`, `AI SDR`, `Intercom` and `Drift` had zero owners repo-wide. The agent holds: an approved answer set grounded in the solutions engineer's four-state capability answer; five answer classes never generated (price, discount, contract terms, roadmap dates, security/legal assurances); AI disclosure at the first message (EU AI Act Art. 50(1)/(5), unaffected by the Digital Omnibus; Cal. Bus. & Prof. Code §§ 17940–17942), with wording left to `ops-legal-compliance`; a human handoff that shows the response time actually achievable at that hour; a pre-release test suite (forbidden classes under pressure, prompt injection, pasted credentials) re-run on every model, prompt, source or product change; a stratified transcript audit with an error taxonomy; a key register for the CRM tokens the widget holds, grounded in the August 2025 Salesloft Drift OAuth compromise (Google Threat Intelligence Group); and a front-door funnel measured on the self-selected visitors who open the chat, with no causal pipeline claim without a holdout. *Moffatt v. Air Canada* (2024 BCCRT 149) is cited for its reasoning and flagged as a BC consumer decision. No benchmark is set as a target. Discoverability: README, `AGENTS_INDEX.md`, `llms.txt`, `CITATION.cff`, both manifests, the suite router, the CATALYST orchestrator and both `catalyst-strategy.md` copies, the Executive Brief, the `sales-enablement` skill description (new phrases: *website chatbot*, *AI SDR*, *our chatbot quoted the wrong price*, *do we have to disclose it is AI*), and the live GitHub About description (now naming *chatbots*).
- **New agent: the Interactive Demo Strategist — seventy-nine agents and nobody owned the one marketing asset that *is* the product** (new [`growth/growth-interactive-demo-strategist.md`](growth/growth-interactive-demo-strategist.md), dual-located at [`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/growth-ops/agents/`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/growth-ops/agents/growth-interactive-demo-strategist.md)). The self-guided interactive demo — the click-through product experience a buyer opens **before** signing up or talking to anyone — had no owner: `sales-solutions-engineer` runs the gated *live* demo inside a sales cycle (and its first rule forbids demonstrating without a gate), `growth-plg-activation-strategist` owns everything **behind** the login wall, `content-video-script-writer` owns the linear product video, and `design-ui-landing-page-specialist` owns the page it sits on. `interactive demo`, `demo automation`, `self-guided` and `product tour` (as an owned artefact) all grepped to zero. The new agent owns the demo portfolio and the job each demo does, flow scripts and checkpoints, **gate policy read as a measurement decision before a lead decision**, build modality and the freshness debt it creates, a register giving every published demo an owner, a **release-bound re-capture trigger** and an expiry, purpose-built seed data with no live-account capture, step-level instrumentation with stated denominators, and the rule that **no demo uplift is reported as a cause without a controlled comparison** — because everyone who clicks *see the product* was already self-selected. Roster **79 → 80 agents**; skills stay at 19 and disciplines at 17.
- **New agent: the Demand Planner — seventy-eight agents executed a plan, and nobody in the repo wrote one** (new [`analytics/analytics-demand-planner.md`](analytics/analytics-demand-planner.md), dual-located at `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/marketing-analytics/agents/`; roster **78 → 79 agents**, skills unchanged at **19**, disciplines unchanged at **17**). Grep-verified gap before writing: `demand plan`, `funnel model`, `planning cadence`, `target setting`, `bottoms-up`, `conversion assumptions`, `plan of record` and `operating cadence` returned **zero** repo-wide, and `capacity model` returned one hit in the maintenance ledger — while `ops-financial-tracker` Rule 6 already required aligning spend to "implied customer acquisition targets" it had no method to compute, `analytics-performance-analyst` read funnel metrics "with clear definitions and targets" it does not set, and `sales-pipeline-analyst` built coverage models over opportunities that **already exist**. Nobody owned the pipeline that does not exist yet. The agent owns: **the bottom-up build before the top-down target**, with the gap stated as a number and named scenarios rather than spread across channels; the **assumption register**, where every rate, cost and duration carries its source, sample, segment, date and a verified-or-inferred stamp (the repo's standing *inferred never rounds to verified*, applied to a plan); the **sales-cycle lag** — carry-in, carry-out and the **latest useful creation date**, read off your own cycle-time distribution with the median and a longer percentile rather than a mean, because a plan with no time dimension promises revenue in the period that generated the leads and a lead created in January cannot close in March; **no uncaused lift** (an assumed conversion improvement enters only with a named intervention, owner and start date, and becomes an input only once `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer` has validated it — "close rate improves as the team gets better" is the gap wearing a disguise); the **ceilings that bind before budget does** — sales absorption, production capacity, and the **addressable-audience ceiling**, the one most often missed because it is the only one with no invoice attached; **marginal rather than blended cost per source**, with extrapolation beyond measured range marked and non-purchasable sources (organic, community, partner-, sales-sourced) planned as their own lines; a **stretch target as a scenario with owners**; a **scenario band whose width is derived from your own recorded plan-versus-actual dispersion**; **re-forecast on triggers declared at authoring time, inside a declared window**; a **versioned plan of record** that preserves every replaced baseline so a miss can be attributed to a named assumption; and an explicit statement of the demand the plan does **not** model (dark social, partner- and sales-sourced pipeline, self-serve PQLs), so a plan that appears to explain 100% of revenue is never used to defund what it never measured. Rule 12 is an explicit boundary naming the eight agents that own everything adjacent — lifecycle stage definitions to `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`, actuals to `analytics-performance-analyst`, money and approvals to `ops-financial-tracker`, in-channel allocation to `paid-media-budget-optimizer`, the existing opportunity book and its coverage to `sales-pipeline-analyst`, validated lift to `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`, account-unit measurement to `abm-account-based-strategist` (**never impose lead arithmetic on the account plan**), and team capacity to `pm-resource-allocator`. **No benchmark is asserted anywhere in the file** — no conversion rate, cycle time, coverage ratio, cost per lead, variance threshold, trigger level or band width — and two circulating conventions are named in order to be **refused**: the ±20-30% construction of scenario bands, and the quoting of coverage-ratio and variance thresholds as universal standards. Program shapes learned (ideas only, no text reused; both licences verified 2026-09-09) from [NEON-Rutger/B2B-revops-skills](https://github.com/NEON-Rutger/B2B-revops-skills) (MIT) — bottom-up before top-down with the gap named, a stretch as a scenario with owners, plan versioning, re-forecasting on declared triggers — and [indranilbanerjee/digital-marketing-pro](https://github.com/indranilbanerjee/digital-marketing-pro) (MIT) — a projection published as a band with the signals that say which case is unfolding. Their planning calendars, capacity formulas, trigger thresholds and worked figures were deliberately not carried. Lint 2/2; both copies diff-identical; all count surfaces re-sum to 79.
- **New agent: the GTM Data Strategist — every other agent started from a list, and nobody owned where the list came from** (new [`analytics/analytics-gtm-data-strategist.md`](analytics/analytics-gtm-data-strategist.md), dual-located at `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/marketing-analytics/agents/`; roster **77 → 78 agents**, skills unchanged at **19**, disciplines unchanged at **17**). Grep-verified gap before writing: `Clay`, `GTM engineer`, `list building` and `data provider` returned **zero** repo-wide, `waterfall` never appeared in the enrichment sense, and `enrichment` appeared in exactly four agents as a vendor name-drop — while `sales-outbound-strategist` Rule 1 already forbade emailing "a purchased list without signal validation" and nothing in the repo said how to choose a provider, tell coverage from accuracy, price a match, measure decay, or record where the data legally came from. The agent owns: **the data requirement written field by field before any vendor is contacted**; **the bake-off that replaces the published match rate** — a random held-out sample of your *own* accounts, submitted blind, scored against a truth set you assembled independently, reported with denominator, segment and sample date; **coverage and accuracy as two tests scored as three outcomes** (returned-and-correct, returned-and-wrong, no-answer), with providers ranked on the wrong-answer rate first because a confident wrong address costs the send, the seller's credibility and a bounce against a shared domain while looking like a success in every report; **identity-match corroboration** on a second independent attribute, quarantine rather than guess, because the most expensive record is the plausible one for the wrong person; **waterfall ordering derived from measured cost per usable record per segment** rather than price per call, with a spend gate before the expensive step and a re-derivation date; **three verification states, not two** — *accept-all* is neither valid nor invalid, and collapsing it into valid is how a bounce rate climbs with no decision behind it; **decay measured, never quoted**, with the circulating "B2B data decays X% a year" figures refused by name and replaced by your own re-verification curve per field and segment plus an as-of date on every record; **provenance recorded at ingestion**, because GDPR Article 14(2)(f) requires naming the source and Article 14(3) sets the deadline — the notice is impossible to give if nobody wrote down where the data came from — with a standing refusal to ingest any source whose supplier will not state provenance; and **the suppression screen as a blocking gate** at every acquisition and refresh, since a bought list is the standard route by which an opt-out is quietly reintroduced. Rule 10 is an explicit boundary naming the nine agents that own everything adjacent — targeting, signals and sequences to `sales-outbound-strategist`, the target-account list and tiering to `abm-account-based-strategist`, the internal data model and field governance to `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`, the provider-as-a-system and its switching cost to `analytics-martech-stack-strategist`, contract and negotiation to `ops-financial-tracker`, the lawful-basis determination and the suppression record itself to `ops-legal-compliance`, the sending estate to `email-deliverability-specialist`, match-list upload rules to `paid-media-social-ads-specialist`, and primary research to `analytics-customer-insights-researcher` (**vendor data is not voice of customer**). `sales-outbound-strategist`'s *Data Sourcing Strategy* and *List Hygiene* bullets were re-seamed in the same ship to point at the new owner and to name the accept-all state, so both files are reciprocal. **No match rate, coverage figure, decay percentage, bounce threshold, cost per record or sample size is asserted anywhere in the file**; the only external material is the GDPR text quoted from primary sources with read dates. Program shapes learned (ideas only, no text reused; licence verified 2026-09-07) from [getaero-io/gtm-eng-skills](https://github.com/getaero-io/gtm-eng-skills) (MIT) — waterfall sequencing with an approval gate before an expensive run, hit rate tracked per provider against your own ICP, and strict identity validation — with its CLI and provider bindings deliberately not carried. Lint 78/78; both copies diff-identical; all count surfaces re-sum to 78.
- **New agent: the Content Operations Manager — the repo had seven people who *make* content and nobody who runs the line that gets it made** (new [`content/content-operations-manager.md`](content/content-operations-manager.md), dual-located at `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/content-marketing/agents/`; roster **76 → 77 agents**, skills unchanged at **19**, disciplines unchanged at **17**). Grep-verified gap before writing: `content operations manager`, `managing editor`, `content debt`, `content taxonomy`, `swipe file`, `content sourcing` and `interview-based content` returned **zero** repo-wide, and `content ops`, `content governance`, `editorial standards` and `subject matter expert` returned exactly one file each — while `content-blog-strategist` already required "headline and outline approval before writing" and a publishing velocity with nothing in the repo defining the brief, the approval chain, or how capacity is computed. The agent owns: **the brief as a contract that names the claim in one sentence** (an unbriefable piece is a research task, not an assignment); **capacity planned against the real constraint** — SME access and review turnaround, measured from your own completed pieces, because the queue almost never waits on writing; **scheduled, recorded expert access**, with a piece whose expert is unavailable *Held* rather than written around; **a review chain with named roles, one question each, a stated turnaround and pass-by-default**, where reviewers comment and one editor holds the pen; **Hold and Kill as first-class states**, so "publish fewer and better" is a decision and not a slogan; **a versioned style guide with a changelog**, so a ruling made in review is not relitigated next piece; **a content inventory where every live URL has an owner and a date a human last checked it**, which is the only artifact that can answer "which pages did we just make false?" when a price or product changes; **an AI-assisted drafting policy** naming what may never originate from a model (a statistic, a quote, a capability claim, a citation, a bylined opinion) and the human accountable for every published sentence; and **freelance/agency onboarding as an inputs problem**, since a contractor handed a keyword returns the generic piece your process specified. Rule 10 is an explicit boundary naming the sixteen agents that own everything adjacent — strategy and the plan-level publish/hold/cut to `content-blog-strategist`, targets and the refresh-or-retire decision to `seo-keyword-researcher` and `seo-content-optimizer`, the quality rubric and sign-off to `ops-quality-assurance`, claims, rights and disclosure to `ops-legal-compliance`, product language to `pmm-messaging-architect`, bounded campaigns to `pm-campaign-coordinator` and headcount to `pm-resource-allocator`. No throughput, cycle-time, capacity, kill-rate or review-turnaround benchmark is asserted anywhere in the file. Program-shape ideas learned (ideas only, no text reused; licences verified 2026-09-03) from [rampstackco/claude-skills](https://github.com/rampstackco/claude-skills) (MIT), [majiayu000/claude-skill-registry](https://github.com/majiayu000/claude-skill-registry) (MIT) and [AgriciDaniel/claude-blog](https://github.com/AgriciDaniel/claude-blog) (MIT).
- **New agent: the Brand & Demand Strategist — the marketing owner of *the buyers who are not in the market yet*, and the first instrument in the repo for the "brand awareness" five other agents already spend against** (new [`product-marketing/pmm-brand-demand-strategist.md`](product-marketing/pmm-brand-demand-strategist.md), dual-located at `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/product-marketing-ops/agents/`; roster **75 → 76 agents**, skills unchanged at **19**, disciplines unchanged at **17**). Grep-verified gap before writing: `brand lift`, `brand tracking`, `brand health`, `share of search`, `category entry point`, `demand creation`, `demand capture` and `mental availability` all returned **zero** repo-wide, while `brand awareness` appeared as a stated objective in five agents with no owner, no instrument and no baseline behind it. The agent owns: **your own out-of-market share**, computed from your own contract term and replacement cycle rather than inherited from a published ratio; the **demand-creation versus demand-capture split** as an evidenced decision with a review date and the evidence that would change it; the **category entry points** — the buying situations you must be remembered in, derived from customer evidence rather than a workshop; the **distinctive-asset register**, where an asset counts only if it is both recognized *and* uniquely attributed to you, and consistency beats freshness; and a **brand-measurement plan** — baseline taken *before* the spend, share of search with its B2B-volume limits (an empty low-volume series is *no data*, not a decline), a tracker treated as the survey it is with unprompted and prompted never merged, and a controlled holdout preferred to an argument. Includes the three-move answer to *"we can't attribute it"* — the accountable metric, the window, and a falsifiable test, all agreed **before** the spend — and a boundary rule naming the twelve agents that own everything adjacent. **No benchmark is asserted anywhere in the file**: Binet and Field's 60:40 is named and explicitly refused as a default, and the two external figures that appear (the 95-5 derivation and the share-of-search caveats) are quoted from primary sources with URLs and read dates and flagged as category-dependent. Lint 76/76; both copies diff-identical; all count surfaces re-sum to 76.

### Changed
- **The Brand Identity Strategist no longer asserts invented brand-awareness and perception benchmarks as facts — every read now runs against your own prior study, with the rebrand confound named** (anti-fabrication sweep, second design persona; both dual-located copies of [`design/design-brand-identity-strategist.md`](design/design-brand-identity-strategist.md); no count change, still **85 agents / 19 skills**). The Success Metrics asserted a "75%+ unaided awareness" target, "40%+ faster asset creation", an arbitrary "85%+ agreement" and "20%+ improvement" on rebrand perception — the same borrowed-benchmark defect the design-ad-creative, social, analytics, email, sales and paid sweeps already removed. All four are now own-baseline reads with a rubric intro and no figure asserted: **Brand Differentiation Score** is a perception study read against its own prior wave, with the note that differentiation and unaided awareness are separate items and must not be conflated; **Design Efficiency Gains** times the same asset classes against this team's own pre-system baseline with the count printed; **Stakeholder Alignment** drops the fixed percentage for a survey read against the pre-project baseline that names disagreement per stakeholder rather than averaging it away; and **Buyer Perception Shift** is a pre/post read against your own pre-rebrand wave with the confound named explicitly — a rebrand almost always ships alongside positioning, product or campaign changes, so credit is shared and any "the identity system caused it" claim routes to a controlled read. The two genuinely **controllable** bars — **Visual System Adoption** (guideline compliance) and **System Completeness** (application coverage) — keep their targets, since the agent owns them through the system's clarity and enforcement, matching the controllable-process-bar rule the sweep has held throughout. Lint 2/2, both copies diff-identical, all four fabricated figures now appear only in negation context, 0 broken links. **Sweep status:** design continues one persona per run — `design-content-visual-designer`, `design-presentation-designer` and `design-ui-landing-page-specialist` still to scan, then the project-management nuanced case.
- **The security questionnaire grew an AI annex, and the Solutions Engineer now owns it** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-solutions-engineer.md`](sales/sales-solutions-engineer.md): new Critical Rule 12, a new section *The Security Review Has an AI Annex*, an **AI Feature & Model Disclosure Register** deliverable, and five benchmark-free Success Metrics).
  - **Answers are given per AI feature and per model route, never once for the company** — a product with three AI features on two providers has three answers, and the reviewer finds the third one.
  - **The four capability states assume determinism.** For a generated output, *supported today* carries a **basis**: what was measured, on what inputs, by whom, when, and what the wrong answer looks like. A demo that worked three times is not an accuracy claim.
  - **"We don't train on customer data" is a statement about you, not about your model vendor.** The provider's default, the tier that changes it, and whether you are on that tier are three separate facts, each cited to the vendor's own current terms.
  - **A mapping is not a certification.** Mapped-to, aligned-with, self-assessed and audited are four different claims; a certification scope names the systems it covers, never "the company."
  - **The sub-processor row that moves in a sprint** — a changed model provider or inference region silently invalidates an answer the buyer has on file, so a release-checklist trigger routes it to the register and to `ops-legal-compliance`.
  - Grounded in the European Commission's Article 50 FAQ, CSA's AICM / AI-CAIQ and STAR for AI, ISO/IEC 42001, and published model-provider data terms — all with read-dates, and no accuracy, adoption or breach-cost figure asserted anywhere.
- **Four agents now point back to the Solutions Engineer's AI annex, so a reader landing on any of them learns the sell-side AI register exists and how their own territory relates to it** (one grep-verified-absent, in-voice line each, both dual-located copies; no count change, still **84 agents / 19 skills**). The [Solutions Engineer's AI Feature & Model Disclosure Register](sales/sales-solutions-engineer.md) had no reciprocal pointer from the four agents that each answer an adjacent AI question, so the boundary was legible only from the sell side. Fixed on each file's existing boundary rule: [`analytics-marketing-ai-ops-lead`](analytics/analytics-marketing-ai-ops-lead.md) Rule 10 — its AI use register is an **internal control record** of how the *team* uses AI, kept separate from and never merged with the product's customer-facing register (different readers, different obligations); [`sales-conversational-agent-strategist`](sales/sales-conversational-agent-strategist.md) Rule 12 — its first-message disclosure and that register answer the **same EU AI Act Article 50 from opposite sides** (AI the company *runs* on its own site vs AI it *sells*), and must not contradict; [`pmm-agent-readiness-strategist`](product-marketing/pmm-agent-readiness-strategist.md) Rule 10 — the register audits *our own* models for a human reviewer, the **opposite direction of travel** from this role's buying-agent-side audit, so the machine-readable evidence pack an evaluating agent scores cites the register rather than restating it; and [`ops-legal-compliance`](client-ops/ops-legal-compliance.md) Rule 10 — names the register as the recorded, dated source the **provider-vs-deployer determination, the DPA and the sub-processor notice** are decided from, and the sub-processor row it is *notified* of when engineering swaps a model, not one it finds at renewal. Lint 8/8, both copies diff-identical per agent, all cross-refs resolve, 0 broken links.
- **Four agents now point back to the new Marketing AI Operations Lead, so anyone reading them first learns where the "who governs our AI use" boundary is** (one grep-verified-absent line each, both dual-located copies; no count change, still **84 agents / 19 skills**). When the [Marketing AI Operations Lead](analytics/analytics-marketing-ai-ops-lead.md) was added its Rule 10 named ten neighbours in one direction, but the four most likely to be opened *first* by someone with an AI-governance question still described the territory as unowned. Fixed by mirroring that Rule 10: [`analytics-martech-stack-strategist`](analytics/analytics-martech-stack-strategist.md) Rule 10 — it owns the *systems* and every AI-tool purchase still runs through its capability-first selection and lands in its stack register, while the ops lead owns the *uses* running on those systems; [`content-operations-manager`](content/content-operations-manager.md) Rule 8 — the editorial AI-drafting policy stays this agent's to write and enforce, but it is one use case on the team-wide AI use register the ops lead keeps, carried there with its autonomy level and never-generate list; [`sales-conversational-agent-strategist`](sales/sales-conversational-agent-strategist.md) Rule 6 — its pre-release test suite is the test-of-record the register *links to* rather than duplicates, so the website chat agent is one row on that register; and [`ops-legal-compliance`](client-ops/ops-legal-compliance.md) Rule 10 — the ops lead routes the legal determinations behind AI-use governance here (whether the EU AI Act Article 4 literacy obligation is triggered and on whom, whether deploying an AI tool on staff engages works-council/co-determination and employment-law duties, and which data classes may lawfully enter which tool). Lint 8/8, both copies diff-identical per agent, all cross-refs resolve, 0 broken links.
- **AEO/GEO freshness pass — the playbook and the AI-search agent gain a peer-measured citation-fidelity finding: being cited by Google's AI Overviews is not the same as being cited *accurately***, and rank and citation are measurably decoupling ([`guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md`](guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md) §2 + §4 + Sources + `Last reviewed` → 2026-09-21; the same two facts added to both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md) — the *Citation accuracy* metric and the *Engine-specific reality* Google bullet; no count change, still **83 agents / 19 skills**). The playbook's trust guidance covered "engines will cite an inaccurate page"; it did not cover the subtler risk a 2026 measurement study surfaces — that the engine's answer is often *unfaithful to the pages it does cite*. Decomposing **98,020 atomic AI-Overview claims** across 55,393 Overviews (Xu, Iqbal & Montgomery, [arXiv:2605.14021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14021), March–April 2026), the study found **~11% of claims were unsupported by the pages cited, with omission the dominant failure** — the answer states a fact *no* cited source mentions ~2.6× more often than one a source contradicts. So your accurate page can be attached as a citation to a claim it never made, which means AI-citation monitoring has to check **claim-level fidelity** (is the sentence credited to you one you actually support?), not just whether your URL appeared. The same paper strengthens §2's previously commercial-tool-only "ranking→citation is loosening" note with a peer measurement: **nearly 30% of AI-Overview-cited domains do not appear in the first-page results at all**, and cited domains were on average *more* credible than the first-page results beside them — discovery is decoupling from rank, so a trusted page can be cited without ranking page one and being off page one does not rule you out. Verified against the primary source (arXiv abstract, read 2026-09-21), all figures quoted from it; no GEO effect size changed (none was contradicted). Lint 2/2, agent copies diff-identical, 0 broken links.
- **The Public Relations Strategist now knows the AI-commentary rule of each source-request platform by name — and that Help a B2B Writer is now MentionMatch** (both dual-located copies of [`comms/comms-pr-strategist.md`](comms/comms-pr-strategist.md); no count change, still 83 agents / 19 skills). Rule 12 previously stated Qwoted's LLM ban and then told the agent to "treat every other platform the same way until you have read its current rules" — a placeholder. Read each platform's current terms (primary sources, 2026-09-21) and replaced it with the actual gradient: **Qwoted** and **SourceBottle** ban AI-written responses outright ("DON'T send an AI-generated response to a call out. Journalists can tell and will generally ignore this type of response" — off-topic replies are spam and can get you blacklisted); **Featured**'s Terms (effective 2026-06-02) permit an *AI-assisted* response only where the source reviews it "for accuracy and ownership before submitting" and never represents AI output as human-generated where that is deceptive; **Help a B2B Writer** has rebranded to **MentionMatch** (confirmed on-site, "Same platform, new name!"), which states no AI clause but defines the deliverable as a "unique quote … written by a subject matter expert just for you and your specific article." **Source of Sources**' written terms were unreachable at review (site returning an index error), so the strict default is retained there rather than assumed loose. The stale "Help a B2B Writer" platform name is corrected to name the MentionMatch rebrand in the Rapid Response Rulebook deliverable too. Every rule is quoted from the platform's own page with a read date; lint 2/2, both copies diff-identical, 0 broken links.
- **The Public Relations Strategist now triages a news story before reacting to it, and no longer drafts the quote a source-request platform bans** ([`comms/comms-pr-strategist.md`](comms/comms-pr-strategist.md), both copies). Four ordered checks: who authored the item (never react to a competitor's own release), standing graded strong / partial / none against a named reporter beat, the story's real first-public clock (syndication does not restart it), and kill switches. Stories are routed as pitchable, big-story awareness, or watch, and none are dropped silently. New Rule 12: on Qwoted and similar platforms the spokesperson writes the quote, because Qwoted prohibits LLM-generated commentary. Adds a Newsjack Triage Record deliverable. Inspired by [elvisun/newsjack](https://github.com/elvisun/newsjack) (MIT).
- **The Podcast & Audio Strategist can now tell you whether a show is worth appearing on when nobody audits its numbers** ([`social/social-podcast-strategist.md`](social/social-podcast-strategist.md), both dual-located copies). Rule 9 already said to qualify a show on *observable audience evidence, not a download figure the host self-reports* — and then named three evidence types and stopped, with no source order, no grading and no decision rule. New section **"Qualifying a Show You Cannot Audit"** supplies the method, built on the asymmetry that a sponsor can demand a media kit and a guest cannot: four evidence classes ordered by who could have inflated them — **Class A** public artifacts the host does not control (the RSS feed including its *gaps*, the show page's ratings and review wall, per-episode video view counts, who else has appeared or sponsored), **Class B** a host-supplied number with an audit trail, **Class C** an estimate that admits it is one, **Class D** a number in an email — and the rule that **composition is decided on Class A alone**, with size evidence able to reorder priority between shows that already passed but never to rescue one that failed. **No podcast is IAB-certified**: IAB Tech Lab's Podcast Measurement Compliance program certifies measurement companies and hosting platforms, so a certified figure is *well-made, not relevant*. Listen Notes' **Listen Score** is an explicit estimate shown only for the top 10% of podcasts, so its absence says little about a niche B2B show. Apple's ratings and reviews are **specific to a country or region** and creators cannot remove them — which is both why the wall counts as evidence and why a US read is not your German buyer's read. The **guest history** is the one read that answers the composition question, and the feed doubles as a dying-show check via Rule 1's four-leg test applied to someone else's program. Adds a **Show Qualification Record** deliverable and three benchmark-free metrics (**Qualification Provenance**, **Class A Sufficiency**, **Declined-Show Retention**). Every platform fact is quoted from primary documentation read 2026-09-20; **no audience, download, rating or appearance-rate benchmark is asserted**. Research source-order adapted in structure only from [coreyhaines31/marketingskills](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills) (MIT, no text reused).
- **The Pipeline Analyst gains the commit call — the forward forecast judgment the stage-weighted roll-up cannot make** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-pipeline-analyst.md`](sales/sales-pipeline-analyst.md); no count change, still 83 agents / 19 skills). The agent already owned the mechanical forecast — stage-weighted probability, variance tracking, bias tuning, stall alerting — but had no named forecast-category taxonomy, and `forecast category`, `commit forecast`, `weighted pipeline`, `sandbagging` and `forecast call` all returned **zero** across the agent files. Added a **"Forecast Categories: The Commit Call the Weighted Roll-Up Cannot Make"** section: the four categories each earned by an exit criterion (**Commit** only when the economic buyer has agreed a date and the mutual action plan is Shared/Co-owned/Current with every remaining step owned and in your influence; **Best Case**, **Pipeline**, **Omitted** as a recorded forecast decision, not a data-quality lapse); the reconciliation between the category and the stage weight, where each *divergence* is the diagnostic (a late-stage deal only called Best Case means the rep does not believe the stage); the inspection questions a Commit must survive or drop a category; and the two symmetric failure modes a forecast culture usually rewards asymmetrically — **happy-ears commit** (fixed by enforcing the criterion, not haircutting after the miss) and **sandbagging** (an upside category converting far above its own history), both invisible to aggregate accuracy. Added **Critical Rule 9** ("Forecast Category Is a Commitment, Not a Probability"), a benchmark-free **Commit Reliability** Success Metric, and a pointer from the Probabilistic Forecast Calculation block. It reads `sales-deal-strategist`'s one-date-two-systems mutual action plan as Commit evidence and never authors it; the `majiayu000/claude-skill-registry` `forecast-discipline` source (MIT, already credited on the Deal Strategist) supplied structure only — its numeric targets deliberately not adopted, no benchmark asserted. Gap re-verified zero before writing; lint 2/2, both copies diff-identical, 0 broken links.

Rule 9 already required an opt-out to be one cross-channel record every channel reads before contact — and said nothing about the one event that deletes it. `right to erasure`, `erasure`, `Article 17`, `right to delete`, `data broker` and `Delete Act` all returned **zero** repo-wide, while the only deletion content in the file was a one-line deliverable stub. Added as **Critical Rule 11** (classify the ask, decide what survives on purpose, then fan out — in that order) plus a new section, ***Erasure Is Not Deletion: The Request That Puts Someone Back on the List***: the collision between erasure and suppression, and the regulators' own resolution — the ICO's direct-marketing guidance that suppression is *preferable* to deletion, that it means keeping *"just enough information"*, and that a suppression list is held to meet a statutory obligation rather than for marketing; Cal. Code Regs. tit. 11 **§ 7022(e)**'s mirror-image permission to retain a record of the request so the data stays deleted; the three-way read of the incoming ask (object / withdraw consent / erase) against GDPR **Art. 17(1)(c)** and the **17(3)(b)/(e)** carve-outs; a **fan-out register** naming the marketing systems an erasure must reach — with already-uploaded ad custom and matched audiences called out as the most-missed row and downstream notification grounded in **Art. 19** and **§ 7022(b)** — where an unreachable row is a named exception, never a blank; the **restore step** built on § 7022(d)'s backup delay, since every restore is a re-subscribe event until suppression is re-applied; three disagreeing clocks (GDPR one month + two, CAN-SPAM's 10 business days, and the FTC's rule that opt-out addresses may be transferred only to a party hired to help you comply — which is what a suppression file *is*); the **California Delete Act / DROP** regime (consumer requests since 1 Jan 2026; brokers accessing the mechanism *"at least once every 45 days"* from 1 Aug 2026) read from the *buyer's* side, so a drop in enrichment coverage is triaged as possible lawful deletion before it is booked as provider decay; and the one-human-many-records identity problem that leaves a person suppressed and contactable at once. New deliverable **Marketing Erasure Runbook and Fan-Out Register**, and four benchmark-free Success Metrics (**Erasure Fan-Out Coverage**, **Suppression Survival**, **Restore Re-Application**, **Request Clock Integrity**).
