- **Synthetic media gets a disclosure owner — and two channel agents stop implying the question is settled** (backlog line 116, a maintainer call open since 2026-08-19, decided 2026-09-12; both dual-located copies of [`client-ops/ops-legal-compliance.md`](client-ops/ops-legal-compliance.md), [`social/social-youtube-producer.md`](social/social-youtube-producer.md) and [`social/social-podcast-strategist.md`](social/social-podcast-strategist.md); no count change). The defect was concrete: the YouTube producer's Rule 4 read *"AI-generated voices acceptable for tutorials"* as a bare permission with no disclosure attached, while the podcast strategist's 16 CFR § 255.5 rule covers *material connections* — a relationship the listener would not expect — and would reasonably be misread as covering synthetic voice too. Grep-verified before writing: **`voice clone`, `deepfake`, `avatar`, `C2PA`, `AI Act` and `watermark` returned zero repo-wide.** The decision: the position belongs to `ops-legal-compliance` as **Critical Rule 10** plus a **Synthetic Media Disclosure Position** deliverable, not to the channel agent holding the tool and not to a new persona. The rule names three instrument families that do not answer for each other — law, platform policy (usually stricter about *realism* than about generation), and the FTC deception frame — and the deliverable carries an explicit **not-yet-validated** state, so the legal posture stays gated behind the counsel review the repo already requires. Two verified facts do the work. The EU AI Act (Regulation (EU) 2024/1689) has applied since **2 August 2026** under Article 113; Article 50(4) provides that *"Deployers of an AI system that generates or manipulates image, audio or video content constituting a deep fake, shall disclose that the content has been artificially generated or manipulated,"* with Article 50(5) requiring the information reach the person *"in a clear and distinguishable manner at the latest at the time of the first interaction or exposure"* ([EUR-Lex](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2024/1689/oj/eng), read 2026-09-12). And YouTube requires disclosure of GenAI content that *"Makes a real person appear to say or do something they didn't do"* or *"Generates a realistic scene that didn't actually occur,"* penalising consistent non-disclosure with *"removal of content or suspension from the YouTube Partner Program"* — while applying a label **automatically** to anything carrying C2PA provenance metadata, a label the publisher cannot then adjust ([YouTube Help](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/14328491), read 2026-09-12). That last fact has a practical edge the channel rule now states: provenance metadata riding along in an agency-supplied asset can decide the disclosure question before anyone is asked it, so check provenance before upload. No agent issues legal advice, and nothing added permits an undisclosed synthetic likeness of a real person.
- **The customer referral program gets a decided owner — the Customer Advocacy Manager, and the one ask that spends someone else's trust** (backlog line 121, a maintainer call open since 2026-08-23, decided 2026-09-12; both dual-located copies of [`product-marketing/pmm-customer-advocacy.md`](product-marketing/pmm-customer-advocacy.md), with reciprocal seams on [`partnerships/partner-ecosystem-marketer.md`](partnerships/partner-ecosystem-marketer.md) and [`growth/growth-customer-marketing-lead.md`](growth/growth-customer-marketing-lead.md); no count change). `referral` grepped **zero** in both candidate files. The decision follows the repo's own text: Customer Marketing Rule 3 already routes every installed-base ask through the advocacy ledger and forbids a second one, and its Rule 5 scopes it to revenue inside contracted accounts — while a referral produces a **new logo**, which is acquisition. Advocacy already owns the three instruments the motion needs: the per-person ask ledger, the consent record, and the recipient's-rules compensation bind. Shipped as Critical Rule 11 plus a section, a **Customer Referral Program Design** deliverable and two metrics. The organising idea: every other advocacy ask spends something the advocate owns — their time, their name, their willingness to be quoted — while a referral spends **their standing with a third party who never agreed to anything**, a cost no thank-you reimburses if the peer's experience goes badly. Hence: price it at the top of the ledger and never make it an advocate's first ask; qualify the *advocate's* own outcome before the prospect's, because the implicit claim is "this worked for me" and an account ninety days in cannot make it honestly; decide **who may receive the reward before deciding what it is**, since a personal reward for steering a purchase at one's own employer is the shape gift, procurement and anti-bribery policies are written about, and a company-directed or charitable alternative must always exist; treat a referred person as an introduction rather than a list entry, so nothing automated fires at a name a customer handed over in confidence; note that a rewarded referrer is a connected endorser from that moment on, so reward-for-what is logged against the named person; and count referred opportunities through to close plus **repeat-referral rate** rather than referral volume, whose classic failure signature is volume climbing while close rate falls. Three introduction paths are distinguished, with the public referral link flagged as the point where the program becomes partner-ecosystem territory and that agent's payout and fraud controls apply. No referral-volume, close-rate or reward benchmark is asserted.
- **AEO/GEO freshness pass — Reddit stops being treated as one reliable AI citation channel; the playbook and the AI-search agent gain a flagged, engine-specific caveat** ([`guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md`](guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md) §2 + checklist #12 + Sources + `Last reviewed` → 2026-09-12; the same fact added to the "Engine-specific reality (2026)" **Per-engine** bullet and a caveat on the "Reddit ≈ 6× G2" line in both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md); counts unchanged at 79 agents / 19 skills). The playbook leans on **Reddit ≈ 6× G2** and "engage in the subreddits your buyers read" (checklist #12), but that figure predates a mid-August-2026 split: two independent GEO trackers reported **ChatGPT Search's Reddit citations collapsing** — ~86% by citation share ([Promptwatch via explainx.ai](https://explainx.ai/blog/reddit-citations-chatgpt-search-drop-august-2026): 3.83% → 0.52%, Jul→Aug) to ~94% by daily volume ([Pierview](https://www.pierview.ai/guides/reddit-citations-chatgpt-perplexity-decline-2026): ~250 → ~16 citations/day in six days) — while **Perplexity's held or rose** (Pierview: Perplexity supplied ~71% of all tracked Reddit citations Jul–Aug). The **cause is flagged contested**: Promptwatch attributes the ChatGPT drop to Reddit adding a domain-wide `robots.txt` disallow (~Aug 20 — Reddit issued **no official statement** on motive), Pierview to a ChatGPT-side query-fan-out retrieval change it says was "not on Reddit's side." Both are commercial GEO-analytics vendors, so the numbers are marked **directional** — but they independently agree the *engines split*. The 6× figure is now labelled a pre-August-2026, engine-blended measurement; the durable lesson (reinforcing §1's "optimize per-engine") is that no single third-party platform is a stable citation channel across all engines, so weight owned properties and diversified brand ubiquity and read Reddit's contribution per engine, never blended. Bumped the "Last reviewed" date to 2026-09-12; no GEO effect size or other engine fact changed (none was contradicted). Lint 2/2, agent copies diff-identical, 0 broken links.
- **CATALYST's playbook list stops calling its own reference guide a "70-agent" guide — a stale count left over from when the roster was 70** ([`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/catalyst-orchestrator/SKILL.md`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/catalyst-orchestrator/SKILL.md); counts unchanged at 79 agents / 19 skills). The CATALYST Playbook Files list described `catalyst-strategy.md` as a *"70-agent reference guide,"* but that file enumerates **79** agents (its twelve functional-team sub-counts sum to 79) and its own prose reads *"all 79 agents,"* matching every other count in the same SKILL.md (the Full-GTM scope line and the timeline team line both say 79). Corrected `70-agent` → `79-agent`. This was the only surviving present-tense `70-agent` reference in the repo; the rest are dated append-only history in `CHANGELOG.md`/`RUN_LOG.md` from when the roster genuinely was 70. No roster change, so no badge/manifest/index/About sweep owed — all were already 79.
- **Two more rotted vendor names corrected roster-wide — the vendor-name-rot scan promised after the LinkedIn fix, now executed** (both dual-located copies of [`analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md`](analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md), [`email/email-automation-engineer.md`](email/email-automation-engineer.md), [`email/email-lifecycle-architect.md`](email/email-lifecycle-architect.md), [`paid-media/paid-media-programmatic-buyer.md`](paid-media/paid-media-programmatic-buyer.md) and [`strategy/playbooks/phase-4-launch.md`](strategy/playbooks/phase-4-launch.md); counts unchanged at 79 agents / 19 skills). All 17 category dirs were grepped against ~50 martech brand names and the flagged ones verified against live sources: **Pardot** was renamed by Salesforce to *Marketing Cloud Account Engagement* in 2022 and **Clearbit** was acquired by HubSpot (Dec 2023), its standalone product rebranded *Breeze Intelligence* (2024) and its free tools sunset (Apr 2024) — both are now corrected everywhere they appeared as recommendations, each with a `(formerly X)` recognition note on first mention so a reader who knows the old name still lands. Deliberately left alone: `Drift` (both hits are the English word — *Authentication Drift*, *Roadmap Drift* — not the chatbot vendor, which a blind rename would have wrongly rewritten), `Leadiro` (a rename could not be verified, so not guessed at), and the `Clearbit` *search-trigger keyword* in the `marketing-analytics` skill (a discoverability match term, not a recommendation). Policy recorded in the backlog for future runs: name capabilities over vendors, exempt genuinely load-bearing platforms, and on a rename correct-with-`(formerly X)` rather than delete. Lint 8/8; all five dual pairs diff-identical.
- **The Financial Tracker stops asserting a fixed annual vendor-savings percentage and a single on-time-contract rate as results — closing the anti-fabrication sweep across product marketing and client-ops** (both dual-located copies of [`client-ops/ops-financial-tracker.md`](client-ops/ops-financial-tracker.md); counts unchanged at 79 agents / 19 skills). Two Success-Metrics bullets carried the invented-benchmark defect: **Vendor Cost Competitiveness** promised *"10-15% cost reduction … achieved through vendor negotiation or consolidation annually"* and **Vendor Management Effectiveness** promised *"95%+ of vendor contracts on-time and within budget"*. The savings figure is re-based on the account's **own prior contracted rates and market benchmarks**, with realized savings from the file's own *Conducting the Renewal* method read as a **trend, no figure asserted** — and the confound named: a book already negotiated to market yields less to cut than a neglected one, so a small savings number can mean last year's renewals held, not that this year fell short. The on-time bullet is split **controllable from vendor-decided** — on-time, in-budget delivery is the vendor's to hit and Rule 4's scorecard to record as a per-vendor trend against each vendor's own baseline (a falling rate the signal to act on), while what this agent is actually held to is controllable: every vendor issue caught and escalated within SLA, the scorecard kept current, and the annual review completed on schedule. Kept as-is per scope, matching the sweep's controllable-bar rule: the `<5%` budget variance and `48h` escalation (Rule 2 and the Budget-Accuracy metric), and the hedged *"typically 12-24 months"* payback range (a rule-of-thumb operating input defining "acceptable", not an asserted outcome — the measured metric is "payback improving or stable"). Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; both retired strings grep to zero; out-of-block figure scan clean; 0 broken links. **This closes the anti-fabrication sweep** — content, social, SEO, analytics, paid-media, sales, email, product marketing and client-ops are now all audited end to end; `ops-reporting-specialist`, the other client-ops agent, was confirmed already honest in the 2026-09-11 spot-check.
- **The LinkedIn Organic Strategist gains the subscription surfaces — the only part of a LinkedIn program that compounds instead of resetting on every post** (both dual-located copies of [`social/social-linkedin-strategist.md`](social/social-linkedin-strategist.md); roster unchanged at **79 agents / 19 skills / 17 disciplines**). `LinkedIn newsletter`, `LinkedIn Live` and `LinkedIn Events` all returned **zero** repo-wide before this change — seventy-nine agents and no owner for the three LinkedIn surfaces that turn rented feed reach into a notified subscriber or a consented contact record. New **Critical Rule 10** (treat them as permission architecture, not content formats; every one has a one-way door in it) and a new section, *The Subscription Surfaces: Newsletters, Events and Live*, covering: the **newsletter as the one place a follower becomes a subscriber** — existing followers and connections are invited by notification on first publication and every *new* follower is invited automatically thereafter, which gives the follower metric this file is otherwise sceptical of a second job; the **scoping decision that cannot be undone**, since LinkedIn allows five newsletters with unlimited editions but documents no merge and no transfer of subscribers between them (and requires a two-week wait before creating the next), so a split you regret is repaired only by abandoning an audience; the **Page-versus-person** choice, where a newsletter under an individual's name takes its subscription list with them when they leave; the **rented-reach boundary**, giving every edition one job beyond its content — move the subscriber to a list `email-newsletter-growth-strategist` owns; **Events as the one social surface arriving with a consent record**, where a pre-filled registration form collects name, email, job title, company and country/region and a checkbox decides whether you may market to them at all, producing **three different permissions from one Event** (viewed / registered / consented) that a single headline number silently collapses; the form's **post-start immutability**, which makes capture a decision taken at creation; and **Live's access criteria** — a >150 follower/connection evaluation threshold, a 30-day account-age floor, good standing, and a mainland-China exclusion — checked in the planning window rather than launch week, with the Page-age clock flagged as a sequencing input for `pmm-international-gtm-strategist`. Adds one deliverable (**LinkedIn Subscription Surface Plan**) and three benchmark-free Success Metrics (**Subscription Conversion**, **Owned-List Conversion per Edition**, **Consent Yield on Events** — which requires registrations and consented records to be printed as two numbers, never one). Every platform mechanic is quoted from LinkedIn Help with a read-date; **no** subscriber, registration, attendance or open-rate benchmark is asserted anywhere, and the section says so in-file. Boundaries stated explicitly against `events-field-marketing-strategist` (owns the webinar program and the badge-scan consent discipline), `email-newsletter-growth-strategist` / `email-lifecycle-architect` (own the list), `analytics-marketing-ops-architect` (owns CRM mapping and routing) and `paid-media-social-ads-specialist` (owns every paid version, including Thought Leader Ads).
- **Two correctness fixes shipped in the same pass.** The employee-advocacy deliverable named "Bambu" (renamed by its vendor to *Employee Advocacy by Sprout Social*) and "LinksHedge", a product that could not be verified to exist — both replaced with a capability-first instruction that starts from LinkedIn's native *My Company* tab and employee notifications and tells the reader to verify a vendor still exists under that name before writing it into a rollout plan. And the `social-media-ops` skill's prose said it coordinates "6 specialist agents" while its own table listed **7**; corrected to 7.
- **The Launch Manager stops grading launches against invented awareness-lift, lead-spike, win-rate and adoption figures — the anti-fabrication discipline reaches product marketing, a discipline the seven-part sweep never covered** (both dual-located copies of [`product-marketing/pmm-launch-manager.md`](product-marketing/pmm-launch-manager.md); counts unchanged at 79 agents / 19 skills). The file was already half-honest: its first five Success Metrics — Launch Readiness Score, Pre-Mortem Coverage, Internal Alignment Read, Risk Calibration, Retrospective Decision Yield — were carefully written as *completion* measures that refuse to double as confidence signals (*"log it as unrun, not as green"*, *"deliberately not reported as a percent-aligned figure"*), but the results half below still asserted unsourced round numbers as expected outcomes: **Awareness Lift *"40-60% increase"***, **Lead Volume Spike *"35-50%"***, **Win Rate Impact *"15-25% increase"***, **Customer Adoption *"40%+ activates"***, plus **Sales Engagement *"80%+"*** and an **Analyst Coverage *"100% … publish within 3 weeks"*** that promised an outcome an analyst — not the launch — decides. The receiver- and market-decided figures are re-based on **your own pre-launch baseline read as a movement** with **no figure asserted**, each tied to the file's own machinery: awareness becomes a tier-aware brand-study / branded-search read (a quiet launch is not held to a maximized launch's expectation); lead movement routes its causal share to `paid-media-attribution-analyst` rather than claiming a spike is launch-caused; the win-rate gap becomes a **powered comparison** with `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer` read at the account's own sales-cycle horizon (the same clock the pre-mortem uses) with the self-selection confound named — deals that attach a new feature may already be better-qualified; adoption becomes a 30/60/90-day curve with **sustained** usage, not first-touch activation, as the meaningful read. The controllable bars stayed as completion measures — 100% of tactics executed, sales enablement finished, every target analyst *briefed* on schedule (co-owned with `comms-analyst-relations-manager`, since briefing is the controllable act and publishing is not). The five already-honest bullets were left **verbatim**. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; all six retired figure-strings grep to zero; 0 broken links. Found by a 2026-09-11 spot-check confirming the email sweep's *"no anti-fabrication discipline remains open"* held only for the seven named disciplines — product marketing and client-ops were never swept; `client-ops/ops-financial-tracker` carries the same defect and is queued next.
- **The Deliverability Specialist stops grading itself against invented inbox-placement, per-ISP, delivery-variation and inactive-rate targets — while keeping the one ceiling mailbox providers actually publish — closing the email anti-fabrication sweep** (both dual-located copies of [`email/email-deliverability-specialist.md`](email/email-deliverability-specialist.md); counts unchanged at 79 agents / 19 skills). This was the last email persona and the one flagged to **judge case by case, not blanket-retire** — because unlike the other twelve swept personas, most of its numbers are sourced primary-doc thresholds. So the pass was surgical: it retired the figures that assert a **receiver-decided outcome** as if the sender set it — *"inbox placement above 95%"* (also in Core Mission), *">90% at Gmail / Outlook / Yahoo"*, *"week-to-week variation <2%"*, *"<3% quarterly inactive"* and the invented *"<0.5% on healthy lists"* bounce floor — each re-based on **your own per-provider baseline read as a trend** on the clean instruments the file already names (seed-list placement, per-provider feedback loops, bounce-by-domain; Rule 9), with a falling baseline as the signal rather than a distance from a made-up target. It **kept** the complaint-rate ceiling as a figure — *below 0.10%, never reaching 0.30%* — because that one is **published by the mailbox providers, not invented** ([Google, *Email sender guidelines*](https://support.google.com/a/answer/81126), the same source already cited in the file's cold-estate section), now labelled in-block as a mandated limit you clear rather than a target you chose; it kept the **<1% hard-bounce** figure as Rule 2's own controllable target; and it kept the **100% authentication / 100% compliance** bars and the **24h/12h recovery SLAs** as configuration and process you author, not outcomes you await. A leading italic note now states the block asserts no invented placement or list-health target and explains which two figures survive and why. In the same pass the **IP Reputation** metric was reconciled with the 2026-09-10 warming re-scope — it now reads *"a dedicated IP where you own one, the domain and mailbox where you don't (Rule 3, the usual B2B SaaS case)"* and points at the fragmentation-surviving signals for low-volume domains a reputation dashboard has no data for. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; all retired outcome strings grep to zero; 722 internal links checked, 0 broken. **This closes the email anti-fabrication sweep** — content, social, SEO, analytics, paid-media, sales and now email are all audited end to end.
- **The Automation Engineer stops grading itself against invented MQL-velocity, MQL→SQL, CAC, open/click, nurture, uptime and data-quality figures — and fixes a scoring example that contradicted its own Rule 9 — the email anti-fabrication sweep reaches its fourth persona** (both dual-located copies of [`email/email-automation-engineer.md`](email/email-automation-engineer.md); counts unchanged at 79 agents / 19 skills). The Success Metrics block closed fourteen bullets with unsourced round numbers dressed as results — Lead Scoring Accuracy *"15-25% higher"*, MQL Velocity *"14-30 days"*, MQL→SQL *"20-35%"*, Sales Efficiency *"2-3x more leads"*, Execution Rate *"95%+"*, Email Performance *"20-30% open / 3-5% click"*, Lead Cost Reduction *"30-50% lower"*, Nurture Effectiveness *"5-15%"*, Platform Reliability *"99%+ uptime / <1 incident per month"*, Data Quality *"<2% duplicate / <5% invalid"*, and Optimization Velocity *"2-4 improvements monthly"* — in the one persona whose own Rule 9 and *An Engagement Event Is an Input; a Score Is an Action* section already establish that acting on a machine-contaminated signal spends real resources and corrupts the conversion data the scoring model is tuned on. Each is re-based on **this team's own baseline** and read as a **trend** or gated behind a **holdout/control**, with **no figure asserted**, and tied to the file's own machinery: scoring accuracy is validated by the file's win-rate-by-score / historical-deal analysis (miscalibrated if the top band doesn't out-convert the bottom); velocity is read off direction and tails against a trailing baseline (minutes-fast crossings flagged as possible scanner trips per Rule 9, never-crossings as sunset candidates) rather than a target window; MQL→SQL becomes a threshold-validation read with the sales-acceptance definition held constant; email performance is weighted below the machine-survivable signals with open/click contamination co-owned by `email-deliverability-specialist` and causal conversion credit routed to `paid-media-attribution-analyst`; nurture effect is tied to the file's own control-group discipline with powered-test lift routed to `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`; execution rate, platform reliability and data quality are restated as self-measured engineering bars driven toward zero-defect (tracked against the platform's contracted SLA and the account's own baseline, not an assumed figure); and optimization velocity becomes an evidence-backed, change-logged cadence rather than a monthly quota that rewards churn. Scoring math stays `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`'s throughout. The four already-honest bullets (**Scoring Signal Integrity**, **Customer Acquisition Cost**, **Lead Velocity**, **Sales Alignment**) were left **verbatim**. Fixed in the same pass: the explicit-scoring example read *"email open = 1 point"*, directly against Rule 9 and the file's own Engagement-scoring deliverable (*"open = 0 points"*) — corrected to *"0 points (machine-contaminated, per Rule 9)"*. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; all retired strings grepped to zero; 0 broken links. **Only `email-deliverability-specialist` remains** in the email sweep (judge its sourced primary-doc thresholds case by case, do not blanket-retire).
- **The Email Copywriter stops grading itself against invented open, click, conversion, personalization and mobile-share figures — and two fabricated "industry averages" it claimed to beat — the email anti-fabrication sweep reaches its third persona** (both dual-located copies of [`email/email-copywriter.md`](email/email-copywriter.md); counts unchanged at 79 agents / 19 skills). The Success Metrics block closed twelve bullets with unsourced round numbers dressed as results — subject-line *"30-40% open rate exceeding industry 15-25%, winners 40-50%+"*, preview text *"5-15% lift"*, CTR *"3-7% exceeding industry 2-3%, high performers 10%+"*, CTA *"50-70% of clicks"*, testing *"1-2 winning approaches monthly"*, personalization *"15-30% better"*, conversion *"2-5% … 5-10%"*, unsubscribe *"<0.5%"*, predictability *"70%+ accuracy within 6 months"*, segmented copy *"20-35% better"*, and mobile *"50%+ of clicks"* — a copywriting function grading itself by figures no reader's list ever produced, and inventing an open- and click-rate "industry average" to beat while the file's own **Rule 9** and its *Testing Subject Lines on a Signal Machines Fake* section already establish that open rate is the most machine-contaminated instrument in email. Each is re-based on **the reader's own list baseline** and read as a **trend** with **no figure asserted**, tied to the file's own machinery: open rate is weighted below every downstream signal and a subject-line winner clears the trust gate on the least-fakeable signal the send can power (Rule 9; *Signal Machines Fake*), with both invented benchmarks removed; preview-text, personalization and segmentation lift become **powered A/B questions** routed to `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`, not asserted uplifts; CTA share becomes a self-competition diagnostic anchored to the single-primary-CTA discipline (Rules 5 and 8), not an outcome; testing "wins" become documented *learnings* where a logged no-difference is the real finding at B2B volumes; conversion credit routes to `paid-media-attribution-analyst` since the same conversion splits differently by attribution model, with the copy's share bounded by the offer and landing page; unsubscribe/spam-complaint becomes a copy-relevance guardrail co-owned with `email-deliverability-specialist` (the placement floor owned there, not restated as a copy target); "prediction accuracy" becomes a falling share of no-difference tests against your own history, since a claimed accuracy is itself untested; and mobile share is restated as a descriptive fact to design for, not a target the copy achieves. The one already-honest bullet (**Copy Consistency** — a qualitative reduced-revision read) was left **verbatim**. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; all flagged strings grepped to zero from the block; 712 internal links checked, 0 broken. Directional hedged rules-of-thumb in the explainer prose (Rule 6's *"10-30% lift"* personalization note, the deliverables' *"5-15% / 10-25%"* testing examples) were left in place as operating inputs and flagged for a later judgment call, matching the Lifecycle and Newsletter precedent. **Remaining email personas:** `email-automation-engineer`, `email-deliverability-specialist` (judge the deliverability agent's sourced thresholds case by case).
- **Competitive Intelligence Specialist now owns incumbent displacement — the deal shape where the competitor is already installed, and the loss your CRM files as "no decision"** ([`product-marketing/pmm-competitive-intelligence.md`](product-marketing/pmm-competitive-intelligence.md), both dual-located copies). Grep-verified gap before writing: `rip-and-replace`, `competitive takeout`, `compete play`, `unseat`, `evaluation window`, `dual-run`, `contract end date` and `value engineering` all returned **zero** repo-wide. New **Rule 12** makes the incumbent's contract a *qualification* question rather than legal diligence — term end, notice period and auto-renewal mechanism, early-termination terms, signature authority — because if the notice window has closed the cycle is unwinnable at any price, and saying so beats a quarter of activity that can only end one way. New section **"Displacing an Incumbent: The Two Clocks…"** covers: the contract clock (mirroring `analytics-martech-stack-strategist`'s **Exit & Portability Record**, the same fields read from the other side of the table); the migration clock (the dual-run period the buyer discovers *after* signing, which turns a won displacement into a churn risk); the **EU Data Act** switching regime, applicable since 12 Sep 2025 and following the EU customer rather than the vendor's address — two-month notice cap (Art. 25(2)(d)), 30-calendar-day maximum transition with the *customer* holding the extension (Art. 25(2)(a), 25(5)), ≥30 days data retrieval (Art. 25(2)(g)), and switching charges prohibited from **12 Jan 2027** (Art. 29(1)) — with the precision that **functional equivalence is owed only for infrastructural services** (Art. 30(1)), so SaaS gets open interfaces and *exportable* data, making this a timing-and-cost argument and never a "you will land intact" one; and a correction that **GDPR Art. 20** is a *data subject's* right over *personal data they provided*, not a right to bulk-export a tenant. The sharpest finding is a **census defect**: a failed displacement gets filed as no-decision/timing/budget when the buyer actually *renewed*, so unless the close-reason picklist carries a distinct *renewed the incumbent* value, the most common loss in a mature category is invisible to every rate the census computes — including this file's own Win Rate by Competitor. And the payoff: a renewal loss is the **only loss in the taxonomy that closes with a date on it**, so it carries a re-attack register (term end, notice-window opening, the unmet expectation the buyer named) handed to `abm-account-based-strategist`. Adds a **Displacement Readiness Read** deliverable and two benchmark-free Success Metrics (**Contract-Clock Coverage**, **Renewal-Loss Visibility**). Boundaries stated in-file to `sales-deal-strategist` (owns the deal and the no-decision mode classification), `abm-account-based-strategist` (owns account selection), `ops-legal-compliance` (owns every claim about obligation) and `analytics-martech-stack-strategist` (owns our own exit record). Every legal claim quoted with a read-date; the widely-repeated vendor-marketing displacement statistics (a "75% success rate", "95%+ renewal conversion", "3-10x switching cost") were traced to no primary source and deliberately not carried.
- **The Deliverability Specialist's warming rule stops naming a control most B2B SaaS senders never own — "IP Warming" becomes "Warming Discipline," scoped to the sending domain and mailboxes where there is no IP to warm** (both dual-located copies of [`email/email-deliverability-specialist.md`](email/email-deliverability-specialist.md); counts unchanged at 79 agents / 19 skills). Critical Rule 3 was titled *IP Warming Discipline* and the *IP Warming Strategy & Execution* deliverable was written for a dedicated IP — but on modern B2B SaaS infrastructure (shared ESP pools, Google Workspace, Microsoft 365 mailboxes) the IP belongs to the provider and is shared across tenants, so the rule prescribed a control the reader cannot apply while the one they can (warming the *domain* and *mailbox* the receiver actually identifies them by) sat in prose further down. The 2026-09-05 cold-estate ship had corrected Core Mission bullet 3 and added the *Warm whatever has no history — which is usually not an IP* section, but deliberately left the rule and deliverable IP-framed to keep that change to one seam. Rule 3 is now **Warming Discipline**, scoped to *"a dedicated IP where you own one, and the sending domain and individual mailboxes where you do not"* (mirroring the corrected Core Mission bullet and Rule 9's confirmed-human warming-seed tier); the deliverable is **Sending-Infrastructure Warming Strategy & Execution** with its lead line re-scoped from *"Warming protocol for new IPs"* to *"…for new sending infrastructure (a dedicated IP where you own one; the sending domain and mailboxes otherwise)"*; the cold-estate cross-reference now points at the renamed deliverable and no longer describes Rule 3 as IP-only; and the Gmail/Outlook ISP note's *"warm new IPs slowly"* becomes *"warm new domains and IPs slowly"*, consistent with Rule 7's "Outlook is aggressive with new domains." **No external source, no new claim, no figure asserted** — the argument was already in-file. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; residual grep for the old control-name titles = 0. Closes backlog line 166.
- **The Newsletter Growth & Monetization Strategist stops grading itself against invented subscriber-count, growth-rate, open/click-rate, retention, conversion, revenue and cost figures — and two fabricated "industry averages" it claimed to beat — the email anti-fabrication sweep reaches its second persona** (both dual-located copies of [`email/email-newsletter-growth-strategist.md`](email/email-newsletter-growth-strategist.md); counts unchanged at 79 agents / 19 skills). The Success Metrics block closed fourteen bullets with unsourced round numbers dressed as results — growth *"15-30% monthly … reaching 5K-50K by month 12"*, open rate *"25-35% exceeding industry 12-15%, engaged 40-50%+"*, CTR *"3-5% exceeding industry 1-2%"*, referral *"15-30%"*, partnership *"10-20%"*, organic *"10-15%"*, retention *"80%+ at 6 months, 90%+ engaged"*, paid-tier *"2-5% … 15-30% of revenue"*, deliverability *">95% inbox, <0.5% complaint"*, sponsorship *"$2,000-10,000+ … 60%+ repeat"*, affiliate *"$500-2,000+ … 1-3 partners"*, customer conversion *"5-15%"*, cost-per-subscriber *"<$5 organic, <$1-3 paid"*, and MQL quality *"2-3x cold outreach"* — a growth-and-monetization function grading itself by figures no reader's newsletter ever produced, and, sharpest of all, inventing an open- and click-rate "industry average" to beat while the file's own Rule 9 and its long *Engagement Tiers* section already establish that open rate is a machine-contaminated instrument. Each is re-based on **the reader's own baseline** and read as a **trend**, with **no figure asserted**, and tied to the file's own machinery: growth reads against the roadmap's own month-3/6/12 targets and alongside list health (a number bought by loosening hygiene is not growth, Rule 6); open rate is weighted below the machine-survivable signals (clicks-to-session, replies, referrals, paid conversions) and read only across an unchanged bot-filtering setting, with both invented benchmarks removed; retention reads as cohort-vs-own-cohort with a **holdout** required to credit a tactic (the engaged were the more likely to stay before any campaign — selection bias); sponsorship/affiliate revenue reads against your own rate card and is priced on the **verified read** not proxy-inflated opens (the *Engagement Tiers* section); newsletter→customer credit routes to `paid-media-attribution-analyst` since the same deal splits differently by model; MQL quality becomes a comparison against your own cold-outreach baseline rather than a multiple; and deliverability is handed to `email-deliverability-specialist`'s cited sender-requirement limits rather than a competing threshold restated here. The two Core Mission bullets restating *"15-30% monthly"* and *"20-40% of marketing budget"*, the Growth-Analytics dashboard's mirrored *"target 25-35%+ / 3-5%+"* open/click targets, and the Monetization section's *"Revenue targets: $2,000-10,000 … $500-2,000 … 15-25% lift"* restatement were all retired in the same pass. The two already-honest bullets (**Brand Authority** — a qualitative trend read; **Monetization Diversity** — a 3+-channel diversification posture, not a self-graded number) were left **verbatim**. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; all flagged strings grepped to zero from the block and its restatements; 706 internal links checked, 0 broken. Directional hedged rules-of-thumb in the explainer prose (the *"typically 10-30%"* referral projection, the *"typically 2-5%"* paid-conversion note, the *"<0.5% per edition"* unsubscribe ceiling, and the pricing-menu examples) were left in place as operating inputs and flagged for a later judgment call, matching the Lifecycle Architect precedent. **Next email personas:** `email-copywriter`, `email-automation-engineer`, `email-deliverability-specialist` (judge the deliverability agent's sourced thresholds case by case).
- **The Lifecycle Architect stops grading itself against invented open/click/activation rates, retention and LTV multiples, and a fabricated "industry average" it claimed to beat — the anti-fabrication sweep opens on email** (both dual-located copies of [`email/email-lifecycle-architect.md`](email/email-lifecycle-architect.md); counts unchanged at 79 agents / 19 skills). The Success Metrics block closed fourteen bullets with unsourced round numbers dressed as results — onboarding *"60-70% open / 8-15% click / 30-40% activation"*, nurture *"5-15% to SQL, 20-25% of deals"*, re-engagement *"20-40% / 10-15%"*, churn prevention *"15-25% retained, 2-4x ROI"*, engagement *"25-35% open exceeding industry 15-25%, 3-7% CTR exceeding 2-3%"*, LTV *"20-35% higher … 2-3x revenue"*, segmentation *"15-30% better"*, personalization *"10-30% better"*, activation *"70%+ … 80%+"*, retention *"15-25% vs control"*, SQL *"15-25% above average"*, and automation *"10-15% monthly growth"* — a lifecycle function grading itself by figures no reader's program ever produced, and inventing an industry benchmark to clear. The two Core Mission bullets that restated the same *"20-40%"* churn and *"15-25%"* recovery figures were retired in the same pass. The Success Metrics block closed nine of ten bullets with unsourced round numbers dressed as targets — forecast accuracy *"90%+ within ±5%"*, coverage *"3-4x for healthy pipeline"*, stage velocity *"within ±2 days"*, conversion *"70%+ advancement"*, win rate *"20-30% overall"*, deal-quality *"70+ for Stage 4+"*, zombie deals *"<5%"*, forecast bias *"<±5%"*, and early-warning *"80%+ of at-risk deals recovered"* — a pipeline function grading itself by figures no reader's pipeline ever produced. Each is re-based on **this team's own baseline** and read as a **trend**, with **no figure asserted**, and tied to the file's own machinery: forecast accuracy and bias are paired as one diagnostic (direction read separately from spread); coverage points at the segment derivation the file already teaches (a 40%-converting segment needs roughly half the coverage of a 20% one), so a flat multiple is dropped as either starving the strong segment or flattering the weak one; conversion and velocity are read together to expose the fast-but-low-converting qualification leak; deal-quality score is made conditional on calibration and on higher scores actually winning; the zombie threshold is defined from the deal's own expected stage velocity rather than a flat day count; and early-warning is re-framed to lead time and what the flag triggered, since a fixed recovery-rate target invites flagging only the easy saves and not every at-risk deal *should* be recovered. The out-of-block restatement of the same *"3-4x"* on the health dashboard was retired in the same pass; the already-honest Pipeline Inflow Velocity bullet and the file's worked segment-derivation example (SMB/mid-market/enterprise multipliers) were left **verbatim** as reader-fills-own-specifics guidance, matching the discovery-coach/proposal-architect precedent. The seam with the open forecast-category lead was grep-verified clear before touching (no `forecast category` / `weighted pipeline` / `sandbag` content present). Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; all flagged strings grepped to zero. **This closes the sales anti-fabrication sweep** — every flagged sales persona is now de-fabricated (backlog line 157).

The seam was named on one side only: the GTM agent's Rule 10 hands field governance to `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`, but nothing told the automation engineer that an appended field is not a native one. It now must carry its **source**, an **as-of date** and a **verification state** (the three-state discipline — valid / invalid / accept-all — from the GTM Strategist's Rule 6) so an appended value never looks the same in the record as a confirmed one; provenance, lawful basis and per-provider hit rate belong to the ingestion gate; and mapping, lifecycle and whether the field feeds a score stay `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`'s. - **The ABM Strategist now states the reciprocal of the paid audience-assignment order — it yields an account that opens an opportunity and reclaims released accounts at the quarter roll-over** (both dual-located copies of [`abm/abm-account-based-strategist.md`](abm/abm-account-based-strategist.md); counts unchanged at 78 agents / 19 skills). `paid-media-budget-optimizer` already states and enforces the collision-assignment order that decides which campaign owns an account when several could claim it — open-opportunity accounts to the deal-support campaign, named targets to ABM, the rest to retargeting and prospecting — and hands the list and tiers to ABM, but the ABM agent said nothing back: neither that its own list *feeds* that order, nor that an ABM account which opens an opportunity **yields** its exclusive paid audience to the deal-support campaign (so air cover stops serving over an account the deal team is closing), nor that released and reclaimed accounts are handed back as an **updated assignment at the quarter roll-over** when Rule 5 re-sets the list. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; 0 broken links. Closes the 2026-09-06 collision-ship question of whether the assignment order belongs to ABM as well.
- **The Outbound Strategist stops grading itself with invented meeting-rate, cost-per-meeting, completion and unsubscribe targets — the sales anti-fabrication sweep reaches its next persona** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-outbound-strategist.md`](sales/sales-outbound-strategist.md); counts unchanged at 78 agents / 19 skills). Each is re-based on **this account's own baseline** and read as a **trend**, with **no figure asserted**, and tied to the file's own machinery: meeting rate is flagged as a confirmed-human signal weighted above the open rate (per Rule 9 and *Opens Measure the Scanner, Replies Measure the Buyer*) and read cold-vs-account-based separately; cost per meeting is judged against the account's deal economics (a cost healthy for a six-figure deal is ruinous at low ACV) and paired with cost per *qualified* meeting; completion rate is read paired with reply and meeting rates, since finishing steps into an unresponsive list is not improvement; and unsubscribe rate is read as a rising-trend signal alongside the file's existing bounce/complaint monitoring (which carries the genuine hard deliverability limits an ESP suspends over). The already-disciplined Open Rate and Response Rate bullets — corrected in the earlier cold-estate ship, which deliberately kept their targets as the denominator-fix hinges on them — were left **verbatim**, as were the deliverable-body teaching figures (the personalization-tier outcomes and the Key Metrics benchmark table), matching how the discovery-coach and proposal-architect sweeps left their deliverable figures in place; the *"0.5-2% meeting rate"* the *Opens* section references still lives in that table, so the cross-reference stays anchored. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; all four retired target strings grepped to zero. - **The Local & International SEO Specialist stops requiring a Google Business Profile most B2B SaaS cannot lawfully have — and says what to build in that market instead** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-local-and-international.md`](seo/seo-local-and-international.md); counts unchanged at 78 agents / 19 skills). Rule 5 read *"Require local citation building (Google Business Profile, regional directories) for each market"* — a blanket requirement inherited from local-business SEO, where eligibility is never in question. Google's guideline is a **condition**: a profile needs *"a physical location that customers can visit"* or a business that *"travels to customers where they are"*, and a SaaS company opening a market with remote sellers has neither; the two workarounds teams reach for are excluded by name — a rented mailing address (*"virtual office"*) *"isn't eligible"*, and a co-working desk qualifies only with *"clear signage"*, customers received during business hours, and staffing *"by your business staff."* The rule is now a **gate followed by two branches**, with a new section behind it. **Eligible branch:** the profile, service-area configuration where you travel rather than host, citation consistency across the citations you actually maintain, local schema for the location that exists. **No-address branch (the usual one):** in-market *authority* rather than in-market *presence* — links and mentions from the associations, regional publications and industry directories that market's buyers read, in-language content answering that market's own regulatory and integration questions, the review and comparison platforms your category is shortlisted on there, and the locale signals Rule 3 already owns. Two guardrails: **NAP consistency is only a control where you have an N, A and P you maintain** (a forwarding number and a mailbox stood up for a checklist manufacture the very inconsistency the metric exists to catch), and **`LocalBusiness` markup with no local business behind it is misrepresentation** — structured data must be *"a true representation of the page content"*, and a violation *"can prevent syntactically correct structured data from being displayed as a rich result … or possibly cause it to be marked as spam."* This is also the one failure in this file that is **not** silent, and the section says so: Google *"may suspend or disable Business Profiles that don't follow our guidelines"*, so the bill arrives as a removal months after the profile was reported as a completed local signal. Core Mission bullet 5, the **In-Market Presence & Citation Strategy** deliverable (formerly *Local Business Profile & Citation Strategy*) and the **Presence branch, then citation / NAP consistency** metric were carried through in the same change — a market in the no-address branch has no NAP to score and must not appear as a zero. **No ranking effect, local-pack weight, suspension rate or citation benchmark is asserted**; every external claim is a quoted condition or consequence from a Google primary source read 2026-09-08 ([Business Profile guidelines](https://support.google.com/business/answer/3038177), [profile removal](https://support.google.com/business/answer/4569145), [structured data guidelines](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/appearance/structured-data/sd-policies)), and the local-SEO literature's vendor-published figures were deliberately not carried. The *branch-before-you-audit* shape is credited in-file to [AgriciDaniel/claude-seo](https://github.com/AgriciDaniel/claude-seo) `seo-local` (MIT, ideas only, no text reused), with [mshahiddigital/agentic-local-seo-audit](https://github.com/mshahiddigital/agentic-local-seo-audit) (MIT) credited as the contrast that raised the question. Lint 78/78; both copies diff-identical; 728 internal links checked, 0 broken.

Each is re-based on **this account's own baseline** and read as a **trend**, gated behind a **powered test** or the **CRM**, or restated as a **discipline**, with **no figure asserted**, and each tied to the file's own machinery: CPL and channel mix are routed through [`paid-media-attribution-analyst`](paid-media/paid-media-attribution-analyst.md)'s platform-vs-CRM reconciliation rather than read off the platform's self-reported number (Rule 2 + the delivery audit); lead quality is validated downstream in the CRM with the sales-acceptance definition held constant (Rule 2); ABM penetration is handed to `abm-account-based-strategist`'s per-tier scoreboard and read as coverage, since air-cover tiers run a reach objective that cannot feed a conversion optimizer (Rule 11); creative lift is reported only from a test small ABM lists can actually power (Rule 5 + the fatigue section); expanded audiences are held to the seed's own CPL/quality bar and Audience Expansion is *excluded* on enumerated ABM lists, not scored (the delivery-vs-targeting audit); and form completion is tracked against the account's own baseline with the 3–4-field design (Rule 7), never read alone because a higher completion rate that lowers lead quality is the loss Rule 2 catches. The four already-disciplined bullets (on-ICP delivery share, category-determination timing, claim traceability, tier-segmented delivery) were left **verbatim**, as were the sourced platform figures (Meta's ~50-event learning window, LinkedIn's ≥300-match floor, EMQ ≥7) and the directional fatigue thresholds. Each is re-based on **this account's own baseline** and read as a **trend**, gated behind a **powered test or holdout**, or restated as a **discipline** with **no figure asserted**, and each tied back to the file's own machinery: CTR lift is reported only from a test powered to detect it with the minimum detectable effect stated (per *Power Before Verdict* and *Record what the test could see*); conversion is tracked as a trend that ad-level creative tests rarely power (per *Pick a metric at an altitude you can actually power*), confirmed on pooled data and routed to [`paid-media-attribution-analyst`](paid-media/paid-media-attribution-analyst.md); refresh recovery is measured against the same creative's own pre-refresh fatigue curve (the file's *Creative Performance Database* deliverable already tracks that decline); channel-specific and audience-specific lift are each measured in that channel's own powered test (Rule 8 forbids assuming creative transfers); the illustrative example drops its invented number to *"at equal spend"*; and the validity rate becomes a tracked, driven-up share rather than an asserted ceiling. The two already-disciplined bullets (message-angle win rate at LinkedIn's own p ≤ 0.1 bar, traffic-powered testing velocity) were left **verbatim**, as were the sourced LinkedIn budget/duration/significance figures, the 60-90-day refresh cadence, and the generic power-arithmetic and MDE-logging examples. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; all seven retired strings grepped to zero. Each is re-based on **this account's own baseline** and read as a **trend**, gated behind a **powered test or holdout**, or restated as a **policy**, with **no figure asserted**, and each tied back to the file's own machinery: CAC and ROAS are read only after the changing edit has cleared learning (a number pulled inside a learning reset is the reset, not a result) with causal credit routed to [`paid-media-attribution-analyst`](paid-media/paid-media-attribution-analyst.md) and the target anchored to trailing-achievable per *Signal before strategy* (LTV is the ceiling, not the day-one number); the conversion-rate claim is credited to a change only through a test that cleared its minimum detectable effect; Quality Score is tracked as a distribution improving against baseline toward Rule 3's 7+ operating target rather than a fixed hit-rate, with the CPC effect read at equivalent competition because CPC also moves with auction pressure; wasted-spend reduction is measured in *this* account through the search-term loop and paired with the withheld/invisible-search-term share that caps what the loop can clean, never an imported "average wasted spend"; impression share is surfaced as a diagnostic and **not chased as a target, per Rule 1**, justified only where the incremental clicks stay profitable at target CAC; and lead quality is validated downstream in the CRM per Rule 8, with the sales-acceptance definition held constant so the number is not moved by redefining the bar. Rule 4's out-of-block *"wastes 30-40% of budget"* claim for uncontrolled broad match was retired in the same pass to *size the leak in your own account*. The three already-disciplined bullets (learning-state, signal-fit bidding, policy pre-flight) were left **verbatim**, as were the Google/Meta figures cited to primary docs. - **The Programmatic Buyer's Success Metrics stop asserting invented efficiency, lift and attribution numbers as fact — the paid-media anti-fabrication sweep reaches its third persona** (both dual-located copies of [`paid-media/paid-media-programmatic-buyer.md`](paid-media/paid-media-programmatic-buyer.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Six of the block's eight bullets stated unsourced round numbers as targets — a *"30-40% reduction"* in cost-per-influenced-opportunity, a frequency cap holding *"70%+ reach"*, a *"15-25% lift"* in aided brand awareness, intent-qualified impressions performing *"40-50% better"* than contextual-only, creative rotation held to *"<15% performance variance"*, and programmatic attributed *"10-15%"* of monthly influenced opportunities — a display-buying function grading its own indirect influence by numbers nobody measured, several of them unprovable without the incrementality machinery the file's own Rule 4 and its *"What this audit cannot tell you"* section already reserve. Each is re-based on the account's **own baseline** and read as a **trend**, or gated behind a **control/holdout**, with **no figure asserted**, and each tied back to the file's own machinery: cost-per-influenced-opportunity is read as a trend against the account's attribution baseline, with the causal credit routed to [`paid-media-attribution-analyst`](paid-media/paid-media-attribution-analyst.md) — already this file's own credit owner — so an uncontrolled before/after is directional, not proof; reach is read as the share of the audience *you actually defined* that was served, pairing the reached share with the unmatched/unknown share the same way the supply audit refuses to let unmeasurable spend round to viewable, while the genuine 3–7 frequency band stays where it belongs, as the operating input Rule 2 sets rather than a claimed outcome; brand-awareness lift requires a genuine unexposed control and is reported at the confidence the study reached (a lift below its own minimum detectable effect is a survey artifact); intent-data precision becomes the account's own measured gap between intent-qualified and contextual-only arms held to the same attribution model, reported *unverified* where intent coverage is thin; creative rotation runs as many variations as the audience's impressions can actually separate, watching dispersion as a trend (a tight variance on too few impressions is an underpowered read, not stability); and influenced-pipeline share is whatever the attribution model and incrementality tests support, since a fixed contribution goal is a standing incentive to tune the model until it hits — the vanity read Rule 9 exists to prevent. The two already-honest bullets (Impression quality, Supply quality coverage) were left **verbatim**, as were the section's cited vendor/MRC viewability thresholds and its illustrative examples.
