- **The Outbound Strategist stops claiming the sending estate it doesn't own — and retires a fabricated warming ramp — closing the seam the cold-estate ship opened** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-outbound-strategist.md`](sales/sales-outbound-strategist.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). When [`email-deliverability-specialist`](email/email-deliverability-specialist.md) gained the deep, primary-sourced cold sending estate (see below), its "Boundaries" paragraph assigned estate safety and brand-domain insulation to itself and sizing/cost/volume to this agent — but the outbound file's *Compliance and Deliverability Playbook* was left as the un-updated mirror, still reading as though it owned the sending infrastructure ("Email authentication: SPF, DKIM, DMARC setup for sender domain") and still asserting an unsourced round ramp (*"100 → 500 → 2000 daily over 30 days"*) with no stated basis. Two fixes, one edit: authentication (SPF/DKIM/DMARC on the cold sending domains), the warming schedule, and the estate's isolation from the brand domain now point to the deliverability specialist as owner, while outbound explicitly keeps its real half — the **demand** on the estate (how many mailboxes and domains a pipeline target implies, what that capacity costs, and the daily volume the program must sustain) — making the two files reciprocal. The ramp is de-fabricated the way the anti-fabrication sweep has done elsewhere: **start conservative and increase against the estate's own reputation and confirmed-human engagement signals** (ties to the specialist's Rule 9 tiering the file already references) rather than a fixed calendar curve, sizing the end state from the volume target, with the warming method and seed-segment discipline deferred to the specialist — **no figure asserted**. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; the retired ramp string greps to zero; backtick agent references only (matching the file's existing usage), no new markdown links. The same file's Success-Metrics block (meeting-rate, cost-per-meeting, completion, unsubscribe targets) stays queued for the sales anti-fabrication sweep, one change per run.
- **The Attribution Analyst — the repo's designated truth-seeker against unearned credit — stops grading itself with invented benchmark numbers; the paid-media anti-fabrication sweep advances to its second persona** (both dual-located copies of [`paid-media/paid-media-attribution-analyst.md`](paid-media/paid-media-attribution-analyst.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Seven Success-Metrics bullets and one Critical Rule asserted unsourced figures as fact in the one agent whose Rule 9 explicitly forbids "point estimates dressed up as certainty": attribution *"±5% variance month-over-month"*, *"95%+ of platform-reported conversions map to actual CRM opportunities"*, incrementality *"proving 20-30% of attributed channel conversions are incremental"*, last-click-vs-multi-touch *"typically 30-50% differences"*, a platform-vs-CRM correlation *"typically 0.6-0.8"*, an MMM *"R² >0.85"* fit target, transparency achieved *"within 2 weeks"*, and Rule 4's claim that platforms *"often overstate their contribution by 30-50%."* Each is re-based on the reader's **own data**, read as a **trend against its own history**, powered by a **pre-declared MDE/confidence**, or **validated against a holdout** with **no figure asserted** — and each is tied back to the file's own machinery: stability is read against the model's **credible intervals** (Rule 9); CRM validation becomes a coverage-and-decomposition of the *unmapped* set rather than a mapping-rate target a loose CRM can hit; incrementality reports the measured share with its interval because the incremental fraction is *what the holdout exists to discover*; the platform-vs-modeled gap is quantified against the account's own data because Rule 4's whole premise is that "by how much" is exactly what you measure; MMM validity is judged by **out-of-sample geo/audience-holdout agreement** (Rule 9 + the MMM deliverable), never an in-sample fit an over-parameterized model can always inflate. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grepped to zero; out-of-block scan confirmed no fabricated outcomes remain. Five `paid-media/` siblings remain, one per future paid run.
- **The Budget Optimizer's Success Metrics stop asserting invented allocation, ROI-lift and spend-tolerance numbers as fact — the anti-fabrication sweep opens the paid-media discipline** (both dual-located copies of [`paid-media/paid-media-budget-optimizer.md`](paid-media/paid-media-budget-optimizer.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Five of the block's bullets stated unsourced round numbers as targets — marginal ROI *"within 10% across top 3 paid channels"*, a *"15-20% total marketing ROI improvement within 90 days"* of reallocation, an emerging channel reaching *">70% of top-channel ROI within 6 months"*, a *"<10% unallocated budget reserve"* with the ability to move *"15-20% of total spend within 30 days"*, and overspend held *"beyond recommended level by >10%"* — a portfolio function grading its own allocation by numbers nobody measured. Each is re-based on the reader's **own baseline** and read as a **trend**, an explicit **policy**, or a **controlled** comparison with **no figure asserted**, and each is tied back to the file's own machinery: allocation efficiency becomes the dispersion of marginal ROI narrowing toward *your* historical floor, compared only across channels whose curves are **bounded by delivered spend** (per the block's own curve-validity metric) so a collision-starved channel reads as unmeasured, not inefficient; reallocation ROI is read against *your* pre-reallocation baseline over a window set before the move, with causal credit gated behind a like-for-like control and the attribution model owned by [`paid-media-attribution-analyst`](paid-media/paid-media-attribution-analyst.md) — already this file's own credit owner — so an uncontrolled before/after is reported as directional, not proof; growth-channel discovery reads an emerging channel on its own accumulating, delivered-bounded curve rather than a fixed share or count (a thin, flattering dataset is a small sample, not a winner); budget flexibility becomes a reserve-and-response-time **policy** sized to *your* volatility and approval latency, tested by whether money could actually move inside the window it promised; and spend discipline holds each channel against **its own saturation threshold** on its delivered-bounded curve rather than a fixed percentage, since a channel pushed past the region its curve covers is extrapolating, not optimizing. The seven benchmark-free bullets added with the 2026-09-06 collision ship, and the quarterly-cadence bullet, were left **verbatim**. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grepped to zero; the rest of the file scanned and its remaining numbers confirmed to be cited vendor quotes (LinkedIn/Meta/Google pacing docs), guardrail policy settings, planning horizons or illustrative examples — not fabricated outcomes. This **opens the paid-media anti-fabrication sweep**; six `paid-media/` siblings remain, one per future paid run.
- **The Budget Optimizer learns to see audience collision — the negative channel interaction its own Rule 4 named and nothing in the file could detect** (both dual-located copies of [`paid-media/paid-media-budget-optimizer.md`](paid-media/paid-media-budget-optimizer.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Grep-verified gap before writing: `audience overlap`, `self-competition`, `bidding against`, `audience collision` and `campaign overlap` returned **zero** across all seven `paid-media/` agents. The new **Critical Rule 10** and section **"Two Campaigns, One Buyer: Audience Collision Inside the Portfolio"** correct the premise the market states and every vendor contradicts: overlapping audiences do **not** make your campaigns bid up your own CPM. Google documents that keywords in one account eligible for the same search "don't compete with each other in the auction" and publishes the order that picks the winner (exact match identical to the query first, Ad Rank last); LinkedIn documents that "competing campaigns from within the same account are prioritized and filtered out prior to the auction" and will not "drive up the auction price for your other campaigns"; Meta documents *auction overlap*, where only the highest-value ad is entered and the excluded ad sets may fail to spend their budget or exit the learning phase. So the cost is **selection, delivery and measurement**, not price — and the third one is why this belongs to the budget owner: a campaign the platform kept out of auctions produces a thin, expensive-looking dataset that this agent's curves read as a verdict on quality, so the model defunds the campaign that was silenced and refunds the one that silenced it. Also new: the exception that turns a budget decision into a targeting decision (Google states a budget-restricted campaign's keyword "won't always be able to trigger an ad even if it otherwise could"); **the one place price competition is real — a second ad account**, which LinkedIn confirms is outside its deduplication and Google handles as policy ("won't show multiple ads leading to identical or similar landing pages at the same time"), the structure B2B SaaS assembles by accident across agency, subsidiary, ABM-vendor and acquired-company accounts; the **B2B inversion** that overlap here is the design rather than a bug (one universe of a few thousand accounts feeds the ABM list, the retargeting pool, the job-title audience and the lookalike), so the instrument is an **assignment order** with exclusions rather than de-duplication, exclusions preferred to consolidation; the **inference read** when no platform reports overlap (reach flat while impressions climb; a launch that drops the incumbents' delivery within days; strict-subset audience definitions; silent automatic expansion), every finding labeled inferred or confirmed; the **cross-platform frequency** question no platform deduplicates, on a buying committee of six to ten people, reported explicitly as an estimate; and **suppression as portfolio policy** with a named owner and a review date. A **Collision-starved** row joins the pacing driver table, an **Audience Collision & Suppression Map** joins the deliverables, and four benchmark-free Success Metrics are added. No figure is asserted anywhere in the addition; the widely-repeated claim that self-overlap inflates CPM by a specific percentage is named and **not** repeated, because no vendor documents it and all three document deduplication that contradicts it. Vendor behaviour quoted with read-dates (2026-09-06); Meta's help pages are JavaScript-rendered and are cited without quotation. Discipline surfaced by [MadalaVijay/paid-ads-skills](https://github.com/MadalaVijay/paid-ads-skills) (MIT) — ideas only, attributed in-file, no text reused.
- **The Marketing Ops Architect's Success Metrics stop asserting invented data-quality, uptime and attribution numbers as fact — and the analytics anti-fabrication sweep is complete** (both dual-located copies of [`analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md`](analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Nine of the block's thirteen bullets stated unsourced round numbers as targets — data-quality completeness *"95%+"* / duplicates *"less than 1%"* / entry accuracy *"99%+"*, high scorers converting *"70%+"* with *"30%+"* lift over low scorers, integration uptime *"99.9%+"* with *"less than 0.5%"* of syncs failing, handoffs *"90%+ … within 48 hours"*, sales agreeing *"80%+"* of MQLs are sales-ready on a survey, marketing attributing *"80%+"* of deals, new workflows *"within 2 weeks"*, *"90%+"* of stakeholders trusting the data on a survey, and dashboards loading *"under 10 seconds"* — an ops function grading its own plumbing by numbers nobody measured. Each is re-based on the reader's **own baseline** and read as a **trend**, **committed-SLA**, or **behavioral** signal with **no figure asserted**: data quality becomes source-of-record reconciliation against *your* materiality threshold before a figure is certified; lead-scoring value becomes the **gap** between how high- and low-scoring leads actually convert with the qualification bar held constant (a narrowing gap is the model decaying, not the market); uptime and sync failures are held against the SLA *you* committed to their consumers with every failure carrying a named cause; alignment and stakeholder confidence are read from **behavior** — whether routed leads advance and where sales rejects them, whether teams pull the reports into decisions unprompted and stop keeping shadow spreadsheets — not a self-scored survey; attribution becomes a coverage measure against your own baseline with the unattributable remainder reported and the model that assigns credit routed to [`paid-media-attribution-analyst`](paid-media/paid-media-attribution-analyst.md), since the same deals attribute differently under a different model; and delivery and load times are measured against committed cadence and your own baseline as data volume grows. The four already-honest bullets were left **verbatim** — the two `target 100%` fit/engagement-routing and consent-coverage zero-drift aspirations, the instrumentation-contract `target 100%` coverage bullet, and the `100%`-field-ownership governance bullet. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grepped to zero. **With this the analytics-discipline sweep is complete** — all six non-clean analytics personas de-fabricated, `analytics-martech-stack-strategist` verified already clean; the sales sweep continues on its own track.
- **The Performance Analyst's Success Metrics stop asserting invented adoption, accuracy, ROI and attribution numbers as fact — the analytics anti-fabrication sweep reaches its fifth persona** (both dual-located copies of [`analytics/analytics-performance-analyst.md`](analytics/analytics-performance-analyst.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Seven of the block's twelve bullets stated unsourced round numbers as targets — dashboard access *"80%+ … weekly"* and satisfaction *"85%+"*, metric accuracy *"99%+"*, reports with recommendations *"90%+"* driving *"70%+"* of actions implemented, a *"20-30% improvement in overall marketing ROI"*, pipeline/revenue attributed *"85%+"*, monthly delivery *"by 5th business day"* with quarterly *"within 10 days"*, and *"60%+ of tests"* improving performance — a measurement function grading itself by numbers nobody measured. Each is re-based on the reader's **own baseline** and read as a **trend**, **named-decision** record, or **controlled** comparison with **no figure asserted**: adoption becomes which dashboards get pulled into decisions unprompted (a suite crediting every stakeholder as an active user is measuring provisioning, not decisions); accuracy becomes source-of-record reconciliation against *your* materiality threshold, with definitions held identical and any drift a defect to drive to zero; report actionability becomes a per-recommendation status (adopted / set aside / dropped) with the dropped ones counted and a hold-course recommendation read as a real outcome; channel-ROI and revenue-attribution *improvement* claims are gated behind a like-for-like control, with the attribution model that assigns channel credit routed to [`paid-media-attribution-analyst`](paid-media/paid-media-attribution-analyst.md) — already this file's own routing-table owner — since a channel's apparent ROI moves with the model that credits it; delivery is measured against the cadence *you* committed with slippage causes distinguished (unreconciled source data vs. capacity); and whether a test *increased performance* is deferred to the experiment's own controls per [`analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`](analytics/analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer.md), since a change that shipped is not evidence it worked. The five already-honest bullets were left **verbatim** — Funnel Performance Transparency, Anomaly Detection Timeliness (its *95%+*-within-declared-range coverage aspiration), Verdict Quality (its *100%*-closed aspiration plus false-alarm tracking), Segmentation Depth (its *5-7*-segment work-scope with the anti-quota discipline), and Movement Reconciliation. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grepped to zero. One flagged analytics persona remains, one per run: `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`; the sales sweep runs on its own track.
- **The Deliverability Specialist gains the cold sending estate — and names the reputation dashboard that has no data for the domains carrying the risk** (both dual-located copies of [`email/email-deliverability-specialist.md`](email/email-deliverability-specialist.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Cold outbound infrastructure had no owner: our ground was split between `sales-outbound-strategist` (a four-bullet playbook) and this agent (authentication and warming written for the brand domain). It is now one discipline here. New **Critical Rule 10** isolates the cold estate from the domain that carries invoices, password resets and support replies, and a new section works out that the two estates run **deliberately opposite defaults** — phased `p=none → quarantine → reject` on the brand domain versus enforcement from day one on a cold domain (containment, not compliance: Google explicitly permits `p=none` for bulk senders); tracked links versus tracking off, because click-wrapping on a young domain is the exact shape a security gateway unwraps *and* buys the most machine-contaminated number in the file; repair versus **retirement with a record**, so a burned domain is never quietly recycled. The finding underneath it: fragmenting volume across many domains to protect the brand domain puts every one of them below Google Postmaster Tools' reporting floor — Google says the floor exists and publishes no threshold — so the domain-reputation dashboard Rule 2 depends on has no data where all the risk is. The section names that trade (**isolation is bought with observability**), lists the substitutes that survive fragmentation, and kills the adjacent misreading that being under 5,000/day is an exemption from the all-sender floor. Also new: a **Cold Sending Estate Architecture & Authentication Baseline** deliverable built on the rule that **authentication is a monitored state, not a setup task** — where the alarm is not `fail` but **regressed**, and the two silent SPF defects (a second record; an over-budget include chain, both `permerror` per RFC 7208 §4.5/§4.6.4) that every green tick in a UI checker will miss; two benchmark-free Success Metrics (**Estate Isolation**, **Authentication Drift**); and a correction to Core Mission bullet 3, which asserted IP warming on infrastructure where the sender owns no IP. Every mechanism claim quoted from a primary source with a read-date; the source's circulating `77.9%` bulk-registration figure was checked against the cited paper, could not be verified, and is not repeated. Ideas only from [`Growth-Today/claude-skills`](https://github.com/Growth-Today/claude-skills) (MIT) and [`chunkydotdev/email-skills`](https://github.com/chunkydotdev/email-skills) (MIT), attributed in-file
- **The Reporting Specialist's Success Metrics stop asserting invented adoption, accuracy and satisfaction numbers as fact — the analytics anti-fabrication sweep reaches its fourth persona** (both dual-located copies of [`client-ops/ops-reporting-specialist.md`](client-ops/ops-reporting-specialist.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Seven of the block's ten bullets stated unsourced round numbers as targets — dashboard access *"80%+ … weekly"* and usefulness *"90%+"*, delivery *"100% … on time"* with *"within 5 business days"*, accuracy *"99%+"* and discrepancies *"(over 5%)"*, comprehension *"90%+"*, insight generation *"70%+"* driving *"60%+"* of actions implemented, a *"80% reduction"* in reporting time, and satisfaction *"85%+"* with a *"Net Promoter Score of 50+"* — a reporting function grading its own reports by numbers nobody measured. Each is re-based on the reader's **own baseline** and read as a **trend** or a **named-decision** record with **no figure asserted**: adoption becomes which dashboards actually get pulled into decisions unprompted (a suite crediting every member as an active user is measuring account provisioning, not decisions); timeliness is measured against the cadence *you* committed with slippage causes distinguished (unreconciled source data vs. capacity); accuracy becomes source-system reconciliation (Rule 8) with any discrepancy beyond *your* materiality threshold investigated, and names the trust cost of a number quietly corrected between reports; comprehension becomes the count of "what does this mean" clarification requests falling as the data dictionary (Rule 4) improves; insight/action becomes a per-recommendation status (adopted / set aside / dropped) with the dropped ones counted, and a report that confirmed staying the course read as a real outcome not a miss; efficiency is read against your own pre-automation baseline with the failure mode named (time shifting from preparing reports to reconciling broken integrations); and satisfaction becomes the behavioral signals — clients acting on reports and reaching for the dashboard's own figures — not a self-scored rating or NPS. The three already-honest bullets (Strategic Alignment, Comparative Performance, Scalability) were left **verbatim**. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grepped to zero. A re-scan corrected the backlog's optimistic "probably clean" note on the remaining analytics siblings: `analytics-martech-stack-strategist` is clean, but `analytics-performance-analyst` and `analytics-marketing-ops-architect` still carry round numbers in the bullets their recent targeted edits did not touch — so the sweep is **not** yet complete and continues on those two, one per run; the sales sweep runs on its own track.
- **The Conversion Rate Optimizer's Success Metrics stop asserting invented lift and benchmark numbers as fact — the analytics anti-fabrication sweep reaches its third persona** (both dual-located copies of [`analytics/analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer.md`](analytics/analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Seven of the block's eleven bullets stated unsourced round numbers as targets — a *20–35%* overall conversion improvement *"in first 6 months"*, a *15–30%* page-specific improvement, a *30–50%* form-completion gain over a *"typically 25–35% for B2B"* benchmark, top-three wins driving *"5–10% overall improvement"*, industry benchmarks of *"typically 2–5%"* top-of-funnel and *"25–40%"* bottom-of-funnel, a *20–30%* user-satisfaction rise, and a *"10% conversion improvement … driving 8–12% increase in pipeline"* multiplier. Each is re-based on the reader's **own baseline** and read as a **trend** with **no figure asserted**: overall and page-specific lift are read against their own pre-program/pre-test baseline, with a page's lift weighed next to the **confidence the test earned it** (a large lift from an under-powered test is not a larger result) — reinforcing the file's own SRM/stopping-rule trust gate; form completion is measured against the form's own baseline as friction comes out, with a watch that the gain isn't just a lower-intent audience slipping through; the biggest-wins bullet tracks **concentration of effect** against program history rather than a fixed percentage; benchmark performance is read against your own history and segment mix first, with published B2B benchmarks named as orienting a conversation, not setting a target (a page "below benchmark" on high-intent traffic can beat one "above benchmark" on cheap volume); satisfaction becomes the direction of the friction/drop-off signals you actually collect, not a percentage; and revenue impact becomes a like-for-like read on your own funnel that names the other drivers (lead quality, sales capacity, deal mix) rather than asserting a conversion-to-pipeline multiplier. The four already-honest bullets were left **verbatim** — Lead Quality Consistency, the earlier-cleaned Test Velocity (which keeps its *2–4 experiments/month* work-cadence and its *60–70%-win-rate-is-a-peeking-flag* framing), Experimentation Documentation (100%-documented kept as a zero-drift aspiration), and the Feasibility Discipline record. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grepped to zero. Sweep continues one persona per run on `ops-reporting-specialist`; the sales sweep runs on its own track.
- **The Technical SEO Auditor's Success Metrics stop asserting invented benchmarks as fact — and the SEO anti-fabrication sweep is complete** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-technical-auditor.md`](seo/seo-technical-auditor.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Four of the block's bullets stated unsourced round numbers as targets — a crawl-waste reduction of *"40%+ … from 1.8x to under 1.2x"*, a *"30%+"* enhanced-SERP-feature increase *"within 90 days"*, a *"20-30%"* search-visibility-score increase, and a *"50%"* TTFB reduction — sitting beside four process-check bullets the repo had already made honest. Each fabricated bullet is re-based on the site's **own measured baseline** and read as a **trend**, metric and diagnostic kept, **no figure asserted**: the crawled-to-indexed ratio is driven down against its own start (no universal target — a large catalog and a few-thousand-URL B2B SaaS site have different healthy baselines) and read against the **intended-exclusion map**, so a ratio that improved only because low-value URLs are now correctly excluded is read as the system working, not as waste progress; SERP-feature eligibility becomes a count of pages earning features as a trend after valid, deployed structured data (eligibility is Google's decision and which features exist shifts over time — FAQ rich results, for one, were withdrawn in 2026), not a percentage-by-deadline; tracked-keyword visibility is flagged as a **third-party tool aggregate, not a Google-reported metric** and gated behind a **control** before any move is credited to a technical fix; and TTFB is re-based to the same **75th-percentile field data** as its LCP parent rather than a lab number. As the sweep's own instruction required, the **web.dev-cited Core Web Vitals field thresholds** (LCP ≤2.5s / INP ≤200ms / CLS ≤0.1, 75th-pct mobile) and the four process-check bullets (index-disposition coverage, migration signal retention, sitewide-drop class-before-fix, audit shelf life) were left **verbatim**. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grepped to zero. **With this the SEO-discipline sweep is complete** — all six non-clean SEO personas de-fabricated, `seo-programmatic-strategist` verified already clean; the analytics and sales sweeps continue on their own tracks.
- **The AI Search Optimizer's Success Metrics stop promising invented citation and traffic numbers — the anti-fabrication sweep advances through the SEO discipline** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Six of the block's nine bullets asserted unsourced round numbers as targets — a *"2x increase in citation frequency … within 12 months"*, citation quality *"by 40%+ within 6 months"*, competitor citation share *"by 50%+ within 12 months"*, *"attribute 5-10% of monthly qualified traffic to AI answer engine referrals"*, a *"25-35%"* content citation rate, and being present on emerging platforms *"30-60 days before mainstream adoption"* — several of them unmeasurable in principle, since AI-answer metrics have **no reliable denominator of total possible citations** to make a rate a true percentage, and AI-referred traffic is a **floor, not a total** (much of it arrives with no referrer and files under Direct, as the agent's own *Measuring Arrival* section already establishes). A new lead-in makes the discipline explicit — read against your own tracked baseline on a fixed question set, print the question count and engine list under every rate, flag small-sample rates as directional, and require a like-for-like control before calling any movement *caused* by the work, because citations also shift with model updates and index refreshes over the same window. Each figure is re-based on the reader's own history and read as a trend with no number asserted: citation frequency and content citation rate become baseline-relative trends with their sample printed; citation accuracy drives a wrong-but-cited defect to zero rather than counting a gain; competitive citation share is stated only against a named competitor basket and question set; AI-referred traffic defers channel-group construction and the observed/modeled/missing decomposition to the verified [`analytics-marketing-ops-architect`](analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md) per the existing seam; and emerging-platform reach becomes a coverage *state* (being early on a platform your buyers never adopt is not a win) rather than a days-ahead number. The three already-disciplined bullets (entity fact consistency, brand-question recognition, topical authority) were left verbatim. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grepped to zero. Sweep continues one persona per run on the remaining flagged SEO sibling (`seo-technical-auditor`).
- **The Thought Leadership Ghostwriter's Success Metrics stop asserting invented benchmarks as fact — the anti-fabrication sweep reaches the content discipline** (both dual-located copies of [`content/content-thought-leadership-ghostwriter.md`](content/content-thought-leadership-ghostwriter.md); counts unchanged at 77 agents / 19 skills). Nine of the block's thirteen bullets stated unsourced round numbers as targets — *"500+ engagements … within 2 weeks"* with outliers *"1,000–5,000+"*, follower growth *"2–5% per month"*, *"2–4 industry publications per month"*, *"1–2 analyst reports annually"*, *"4–8 speaking engagements annually"*, *"50,000+ impressions"* / *"500+ profile visits"*, deal cycles accelerated *"by 5–10 days"*, an earned-media dollar value of *"$50K–200K annually"*, and topic authority as ranking *"in top 5 results"* — the last two doubly wrong, since **advertising-value-equivalency is already banned repo-wide** by [`comms-pr-strategist`](comms/comms-pr-strategist.md) Rule 9 (Barcelona Principles 4.0, AMEC, June 2025, names AVEs an invalid measure) and the executive doesn't control search rank. Each is re-based on the executive's **own history** and read as a **trend** with **no figure asserted**: engagement is read per content type against their own prior posts with the impression count printed (a single outlier is one data point, not a baseline); follower growth and speaking/media/analyst pickup become coverage-against-effort counts (mentions per pitch, slots per proposal, citations earned) with earned-media and analyst-relations strategy handed to their verified owners ([`comms-pr-strategist`](comms/comms-pr-strategist.md), [`comms-analyst-relations-manager`](comms/comms-analyst-relations-manager.md)); the deal-acceleration claim becomes a within-pipeline comparison against comparable deals, stated as a hypothesis needing a control since content engagement correlates with buyer intent, with velocity measurement handed to [`sales-pipeline-analyst`](sales/sales-pipeline-analyst.md); earned-media *value* becomes earned-media *reach* (placements, audiences, citations — never a rate-card dollar figure); and topic authority becomes which named executive-name+topic queries return the executive and how that set moves over time. A stray restatement of the *"500+ engagements typical"* figure in the editorial-calendar deliverable was retired in the same pass. The four already-honest bullets (Voice Authenticity, First-hand Density, Employee Perception, Board/Investor Impact) were left verbatim. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grepped to zero. Sweep continues one persona per run on the remaining flagged content siblings.
- **The Data Storyteller's Success Metrics stop asserting invented percentages as fact — the analytics anti-fabrication sweep reaches its second persona** (both dual-located copies of [`analytics/analytics-data-storyteller.md`](analytics/analytics-data-storyteller.md); counts unchanged at 76 agents / 19 skills). The whole ten-bullet **Success Metrics** block stated unsourced round numbers as targets — report utilization *"80%+"* and satisfaction *"85%+"*, executive comprehension *"90%+"* on one read, decision velocity *"70%+ … within 2 weeks"*, delivery *"within 5 business days / 10 days"*, data accuracy *"99%+"*, visualization takeaway *"95%"*, post-mortem learning application *"80%+"*, stakeholder trust *"90%"*, and QBR satisfaction *"85%+"* driving renewal in *"60%+"* of strategic accounts — a self-scored reporting scorecard that grades its own reports by numbers nobody measured. Each bullet is re-based on the reader's **own history** and read as a **trend** or a **named-decision** record with **no figure asserted**: utilization becomes which reports actually get cited or asked for when late (a suite crediting every report as useful is measuring politeness); comprehension becomes the count of follow-up "what does this mean" questions falling over time, not a share who understood; decision enablement is tracked per report as the named decision it informed and the elapsed time to it, with a report that confirmed staying the course read as a real outcome not a miss; timeliness is measured against the cadence *you* committed with slippage causes distinguished (unreconciled source data vs. capacity); accuracy becomes source-system reconciliation with any discrepancy beyond *your* materiality threshold investigated, definitional consistency kept as a **zero-drift aspiration**; visualization effectiveness becomes the text-removed comprehension test from Rule 3 plus the count of charts that drove no question (Rule 4's cut list); post-mortem **coverage** stays a full-coverage aspiration so the sample is not just the wins, with each learning carried as a status (applied / set aside / forgotten) and the forgotten ones counted; stakeholder confidence becomes the external, adversarial signals — finance validating the ROI numbers independently and the rate of challenged numbers falling — since a suite never questioned is as likely un-scrutinized as trusted; and the QBR's renewal/expansion attribution is handed to the verified [`analytics-performance-analyst`](analytics/analytics-performance-analyst.md) as a many-driver like-for-like read the reporting function does not claim for itself, its own bar being whether the account left with a shared, accurate picture. The change **removes** fabricated figures and asserts none. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grepped to zero. Sweep continues one persona per run on the unaudited siblings: `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`, `ops-reporting-specialist`.
- **The Proposal Architect's Success Metrics stop asserting invented benchmarks as fact — the sales anti-fabrication sweep reaches its third persona** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-proposal-architect.md`](sales/sales-proposal-architect.md); counts unchanged at 76 agents / 19 skills). Eight of the block's ten bullets stated unsourced round numbers as targets — a *20–30%* proposal win rate, a *14–30 day* proposal-to-close velocity, a *40–60%* RFP win rate, a *<15%* price-cited loss rate, *70%+* customization depth, a *<5%* compliance-breach rate, *90%+* implementation-timeline accuracy with *no slippage beyond one week*, and an *80+/100* quality score — and a ninth identical assertion sat in the Effectiveness-Metrics deliverable above (*"10–15% higher win rate with proposal"*). Each is re-based on the reader's **own historical baseline** and read as a **trend**, metric and diagnostic kept: win rate is comparable only behind a consistent qualification bar and segmented before comparing (deal mix moves it more than proposal craft); velocity is read within a segment with the distribution watched (deal size and procurement drive it); the RFP rate is paired with a bid/no-bid discipline so a rate that climbs by dropping long-shots reads as the selection effect it is; the price-objection rate is read against how deals were qualified and priced; customization depth is watched paired with win rate (more bespoke is not automatically better); the compliance-breach aspiration is honestly zero, every breach a review-process failure to trace rather than a rate held under a ceiling; timeline accuracy shrinks the gap between promised and actual for *your* deals with directional bias watched separately from spread; and the quality score is only as meaningful as its rubric, calibrated across reviewers and checked against whether higher-scoring proposals actually win. The proposal-vs-no-proposal comparison becomes your own measured gap over time, comparable only when both groups are qualified alike. The two already-honest bullets (Executive Summary Utilization, Reference Call Volume) were left verbatim, and the file's illustrative template figures — which teach the reader to fill in *their own* specifics — were left alone. No figure is asserted anywhere in the block. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2; retired strings grepped to zero. Two flagged sales personas remain, one per run: `sales-pipeline-analyst` (entangled with the forecast-category lead), `sales-outbound-strategist`.
- **The Discovery Coach's Success Metrics stop asserting invented benchmarks as fact — the anti-fabrication sweep reaches the sales discipline's second persona** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-discovery-coach.md`](sales/sales-discovery-coach.md); counts unchanged at 76 agents / 19 skills). Nine of the block's ten bullets stated unsourced round numbers as targets — *8–12* questions per call, a *30–40% rep / 60–70% buyer* talk ratio, *2–3* problems per call, a *60–80%* quantification rate, *85%+* qualification accuracy, a *60–70%* discovery-to-advance rate, a *10–15% year-over-year* cycle reduction, a *35%+* win rate for strong-qualification deals, and a *15–25%* disqualification rate. Each is re-based on the reader's **own historical baseline** and read as a **trend**, with the metric and its diagnostic kept: questions and problems are read against your own strong-discovery calls (more is not better if the questions are shallow); the talk ratio keeps its durable principle (the buyer should talk more than the rep) but drops the fixed split and flags a rep who dominates rather than a percentage; quantification and qualification accuracy become trends against your own prior quarters, with directional bias (optimistic vs. sandbagging) watched separately from spread; the advance rate is read *against* the disqualification rate (an advance rate that climbs because nothing is disqualified is weaker, not stronger); cycle length is read against your own segment history with the distribution watched; the win-rate metric's signal becomes the **gap** between high- and low-quality discovery close rates with the qualification bar held constant; and the disqualification rate is read against your own baseline, a near-zero rate meaning bad-fit deals are being carried. The tenth bullet (Rep Coaching Adoption), already process-framed, was left untouched. No figure is asserted anywhere in the block. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2. Three flagged sales personas remain, one per run: `sales-pipeline-analyst`, `sales-proposal-architect`, `sales-outbound-strategist`.
- **The Deal Strategist gains the mutual action plan — the close plan the buyer co-owns, and the earliest honest signal that a deal is not real** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-deal-strategist.md`](sales/sales-deal-strategist.md); counts unchanged at 76 agents / 19 skills). The agent already told you to work the buyer's buying process (Rule 5) and set exit criteria for eight stages, but produced no artifact the *buyer* had agreed to — every document in the deal was the seller's. The new section **"The Mutual Action Plan: The Close Plan the Buyer Co-Owns"** supplies it: plan **backward from the buyer's dated business event**, never forward from your quarter (and when there is no such event, the close date is one you invented — re-date the forecast rather than work the plan harder); grade the plan **Shared / Co-owned / Current** rather than counting whether one exists, since a plan living only in your CRM is a forecast note and a plan whose owners are all seller-side is a to-do list; put the steps you cannot run into it — security-review queue depth, InfoSec questionnaire, vendor onboarding, legal redlines and whose paper, budget-approval cadence, committee or board thresholds, and freeze windows; treat a **refusal to co-author as the finding**, with three readings that take different actions; **never use it as leverage**, because quoting the buyer's own dates back at them as a debt spends the honesty that made it useful; keep **one date across two systems** (the CRM close date *is* the plan's signature milestone, and when they disagree the plan is right), with `sales-pipeline-analyst` consuming the plan as forecast evidence and never authoring it; and use it as the delivery vehicle for the JOLT moves *Limit the exploration* and *Take risk off the table*, so an indecision-mode stall gets a dated step instead of a reassurance. Ends at signature, with explicit boundaries against the POC charter (`sales-solutions-engineer`), the proposal (`sales-proposal-architect`), pricing floors (`pmm-pricing-packaging-strategist`) and post-signature onboarding (`pm-client-success-manager`, `growth-customer-marketing-lead`). Adds a **Mutual Action Plan (Close Plan)** deliverable and two Success Metrics — **Mutual-Plan Health** and **Date Integrity** — neither carrying an invented benchmark. Buyer enablement credited to Toman, Adamson and Gomez (*HBR*, 2017); structure learned ideas-only from [OneWave-AI/claude-skills](https://github.com/OneWave-AI/claude-skills) and [majiayu000/claude-skill-registry](https://github.com/majiayu000/claude-skill-registry) (both MIT, verified 2026-09-02), whose numeric targets were deliberately not adopted.
- **The Deal Strategist's own Success Metrics stop asserting invented benchmarks as fact — the standing anti-fabrication sweep reaches the sales discipline** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-deal-strategist.md`](sales/sales-deal-strategist.md); counts unchanged at 76 agents / 19 skills). The section added last run was written benchmark-free, but the pre-existing Success Metrics block directly below it still stated six unsourced round numbers as targets: a *60–90 day (mid-market) / 120–180 day (enterprise)* sales cycle, a *25–40%* win rate, *80%+* forecast accuracy within a two-week window, *3–5 / 5–7* active stakeholders per deal, an *80%+* champion-identification rate, and a *~10%* unclassified-loss ceiling. Each is now re-based on the reader's **own historical baseline** and read as a **trend**, with the metric and its diagnostic kept: cycle length is judged against the team's own segment history and its distribution watched for stalls, win rate only compared behind a consistent qualification bar and segmented by source and competitive-vs-uncontested, forecast accuracy tracked for shrinking, unbiased error with directional bias watched separately from spread, the stakeholder count read only as a trend (a fixed target invites the headcount-padding the metric is meant to catch), champion identification measured as *validated, not merely nominated* over the honest Stage-3-open denominator, and the unclassified-loss share driven down against its own trend rather than a fixed cutoff. No figure is asserted anywhere in the block. Both copies diff-identical; lint 2/2.
- **The Link Building Strategist stops promising links, authority, and rankings it can't guarantee — the SEO anti-fabrication sweep reaches its fourth persona** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-link-building-strategist.md`](seo/seo-link-building-strategist.md); counts unchanged at 76 agents / 19 skills). The **Success Metrics** block asserted seven unsourced round numbers as fact — acquisition *"10-15 links/month from domain authority 40+ sources … within 6 months"*, referral *"25-40% increase … within 12 months"*, authority *"5-10 point increase in root domain authority … within 12 months"*, a *"15-20% correlation between link velocity and ranking improvements"*, guest-post *"3-5 publications … each generating 500+ monthly referral clicks"*, digital-PR *"Convert 20-30% of pitches … within 6 months"*, and *"20-30+ media mentions/expert quotes annually"* — sitting directly beside four bullets the repo had already made honest. Each fabricated bullet is rewritten to the sweep's baseline-from-your-own-history pattern with **no figure asserted**: a leading paragraph requires every rate to be read against the profile's own pre-campaign baseline (how fast authority accrues and how far a link moves a ranking is set by your starting profile, each target SERP's competition, and the newsworthiness of what you actually have to pitch), demands a **control** before any ranking movement is called *caused* by the links (a site also rides Google updates, seasonality, competitor moves, and its own on-page work), and flags that domain-authority scores (Moz DA, Ahrefs DR) are third-party estimates, not a Google signal and not ground truth; the acquisition, referral, authority, ranking-impact, digital-PR/guest-post and media-mention bullets all become tracked movements and quality reads against your own prior cadence, with pipeline attribution handed to the verified `analytics-performance-analyst` and the ranking-impact claim required to be a like-for-like read rather than a fixed correlation. The four already-disciplined bullets — shortlist-presence coverage, the paid-placement-hygiene binary, the >15%-per-source velocity-diversity constraint (Rule 7), and the disavow-discipline share-resolved-as-do-nothing metric — were left untouched. Both copies diff-identical, lint 2/2, retired strings grepped to zero. Two flagged SEO personas remain, one per run: `seo-ai-search-optimizer`, `seo-technical-auditor`.
- **The Keyword Researcher stops promising traffic and rankings it can't guarantee — the SEO anti-fabrication sweep reaches its third persona** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-keyword-researcher.md`](seo/seo-keyword-researcher.md); counts unchanged at 75 agents / 19 skills). The **Success Metrics** block asserted seven unsourced round numbers as fact — organic traffic *"40-60% increase … within 6 months"*, commercial-keyword ranking *"30%+ … in top 3 … within 12 months"*, qualified traffic *"by 50%+ within 6 months"*, long-tail capture *"60%+ … by month 6"*, difficulty win rate *"at 60%+ rate within 12 months"*, SERP-feature capture *"for 20%+ … within 6 months"*, and competitive share of voice *"by 35%+ within 12 months"*. Each is rewritten to the sweep's baseline-from-your-own-portfolio pattern with **no figure asserted**: a leading paragraph requires every rate to be read against the keyword set's own pre-work trend and starting distribution (how far a portfolio can move is set by its starting positions, each SERP's competition, and the real demand behind the terms), demands a **control** before any movement is called *caused* by the research (a portfolio also rides Google updates, seasonality, and the site's changing authority), and flags that third-party volume/difficulty scores are directional estimates, not scored outcomes; portfolio-traffic and commercial-ranking bullets become tracked movements read against where the set started; long-tail composition and share of voice become directions over successive measurements with the keyword basket stated (an SOV number is meaningless without its basket); the difficulty-band win rate becomes a calibration check on your own targeting rather than a promised percentage; and buyer-qualified pipeline is handed to the verified `analytics-performance-analyst` as an attribution question rather than a lead-increase figure. The already-disciplined AI-query-landscape-coverage bullet (reported as questions-observed-vs-inferred, never a demand percentage) was left untouched. Both copies diff-identical, lint 2/2, retired strings grepped to zero. Three flagged SEO personas remain, one per run: `seo-link-building-strategist`, `seo-ai-search-optimizer`, `seo-technical-auditor`.
- **The On-Page SEO Content Optimizer stops promising rankings it can't guarantee — the SEO anti-fabrication sweep reaches its second persona** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-content-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-content-optimizer.md); counts unchanged at 75 agents / 19 skills). The **Success Metrics** block opened with seven bullets asserting unsourced round numbers as fact — *"Average +2-3 position improvement … within 30 days"*, *"featured snippet positioning for 15-20% of target commercial keywords within 90 days"*, *"25-35% increase in monthly search impressions … within 60 days"*, *"20-30% CTR increase … within 45 days"*, *"Increase crawl depth … by 40%+"*, *"15-20% of monthly qualified leads … within 6 months"*, and a *"5-10 position improvements per hour"* refresh-ROI ratio — sitting directly above six bullets the repo had already rewritten into honest process-checks. Each fabricated bullet is rewritten to the sweep's baseline-from-your-own-history pattern with **no fixed figure asserted**: a leading paragraph requires every rate to be read against the page's own pre-optimization baseline (headroom is set by current position, query competition, and demand size) and demands a **control** before any movement is called *caused* by the edit (a page also rides Google updates, seasonality, and the site's changing authority); ranking, snippet, impressions/CTR and internal-link bullets become tracked movements read against where each page started, with the impression count printed under every CTR and the two conflatable moves (impressions vs. CTR) held apart; content-driven pipeline is handed to the verified `analytics-performance-analyst` as an attribution question rather than a monthly lead share; and the refresh-vs-new-content case becomes a comparison measured on *your own* library rather than a fixed positions-per-hour ratio. The six already-disciplined process-check bullets (decay diagnosis, disposition mix, credibility routing, coverage-and-gain balance, cannibalization coverage, comparison-page trust) are left untouched. Note: the *"40%+"* crawl-depth bullet was caught in the block though the backlog's flagged-string list had not enumerated it. Both copies diff-identical, lint 2/2, retired strings grepped to zero. Four flagged SEO personas remain, one per run: `seo-keyword-researcher`, `seo-link-building-strategist`, `seo-ai-search-optimizer`, `seo-technical-auditor`.
- **The Customer Insights Researcher's Success Metrics stop asserting invented percentages — the analytics anti-fabrication sweep finishes the job the Rule 3/5 rewrite started** (both dual-located copies of [`analytics/analytics-customer-insights-researcher.md`](analytics/analytics-customer-insights-researcher.md); counts unchanged at 75 agents / 19 skills). Four Success-Metric bullets still printed unsourced round numbers as fact — *"80%+ of research findings directly influence product decisions"*, *"85%+ of sales and product team agree personas accurately represent their customers"*, *"70%+ of major recommendations … are implemented"*, and insights *"within 3-4 weeks of completion"* — sitting beside the same agent's already-rewritten Claim Discipline and Research Scope metrics that refuse exactly this move. Each is rewritten into a tracked, measured discipline that asserts **no figure**: **utilization** recorded as the named product/positioning/GTM decision a study informed (and studies that changed nothing reported as plainly as those that did, since a program crediting every project with a decision is measuring attribution after the fact); **persona accuracy** checked by putting each persona in front of the sales and product people who meet those customers and logging where they disagree (a review that surfaces no disagreement means the personas were shown, not tested); **recommendation adoption** carried as a per-item status — implemented, tested, explicitly declined, or silently disappeared — with the disappearances counted because they measure whether the research is trusted enough to be argued with; and **velocity** reported as a fieldwork-close-to-recommendations trend rather than a calendar SLA (a synthesis that is fast because it skipped the disconfirming cases is slower, not faster). Both copies diff-identical, lint 2/2, retired figures grepped to zero. Sweep continues one persona per run on the unaudited siblings (`analytics-data-storyteller`, `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`, `ops-reporting-specialist`).
- **The Customer Insights Researcher learns how to know a finding is real — and stops telling you to invent a percentage** (both dual-located copies of [`analytics/analytics-customer-insights-researcher.md`](analytics/analytics-customer-insights-researcher.md); counts unchanged at 75 agents / 19 skills). The thinnest agent in the analytics discipline (1,333 words) carried a defect rather than a gap: **Rule 5**, the rule written to stop anecdotes, instructed *"Report findings as: '80% of customers mentioned X'"* — a prevalence statistic manufactured out of a purposively recruited sample, which is exactly the fabrication the repo has spent five runs sweeping out of Success Metrics blocks, phrased here as best practice. **Rule 3** had the matching hole, naming *"representative sample"* as a control with no instrument (`sampling frame`, `non-response`, `self-selection` and thematic `saturation` appear in one file each repo-wide, and none of them in this agent). Rule 3 becomes **"Recruit Against a Named Sampling Frame, and Record Who Did Not Answer"** — name the population, state what share your reach covers, record the study's own funnel (frame → invited → responded → completed → analyzed) with segment mix at each step, recruit on behaviour rather than demographics, and deliberately sample or explicitly declare the two structurally unreachable populations (churned customers, buyers who chose a competitor). Rule 5 becomes **"Report Counts and Denominators — Never Turn a Qualitative Sample Into a Percentage"**: *9 of 14 interviewed, 6 unprompted*, with corroboration counted in **independent channels, not records** (two interviews are one channel; an interview, a support ticket and a review are three). New **Rule 9** grades a finding by the strongest evidence actually observed — context < stated problem < observed workaround or substitute < stated preference < money or retained use — and forbids promoting a lower rung, because a hundred people describing a frustration is not evidence that any of them would buy the fix; new **Rule 10** draws the boundary to the ten agents that own adjacent ground (win/loss to `pmm-competitive-intelligence`, behavioural series to `analytics-performance-analyst`, controlled tests to `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`, participant consent and retention to `ops-legal-compliance`, and so on). A new **"Knowing When a Finding Is Real"** section supplies the method: name the decision the study will change or cut the study; state the objective with a verb that has a finish line (*understand* and *explore* cannot be completed); the study funnel as a table where each stage's absence is named; the **two silences** (non-response, and the churned / lost / never-considered populations that carry the findings you most need); a **claim table** giving six designs and what each can and cannot support, filled in *before* fieldwork; a dated **watchlist** for single-channel themes whose expiries are logged; the **empty-source-versus-broken-source** distinction, which is how a failed integration becomes evidence of absence; **saturation per segment** as the stopping rule instead of an interview quota; and a **what never enters a report** list led by model-generated personas and verbatims. Adds a **Research Design & Claim Record** deliverable, rewrites the *"minimum 15-20 interviews / minimum 200 survey responses"* success metric onto the study's own stopping rule, and adds a **Claim Discipline** metric. Method learned as ideas only from [roy-tong/user-demand-research](https://github.com/roy-tong/user-demand-research) (MIT — the evidence ladder and the ban on prevalence from convenience samples), [Aatirs-Vault/voc-claude-skills](https://github.com/Aatirs-Vault/voc-claude-skills) (MIT — channel-counted corroboration, the logged watchlist, empty-versus-broken sources), [cookiy-ai/user-research-skill](https://github.com/cookiy-ai/user-research-skill) (MIT — decision mapping, the finite-verb objective, behaviour-over-demographics recruiting, saturation) and [coreyhaines31/marketingskills](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills) (MIT — the best-customer recruiting pattern this section deliberately bounds). No text reused, and **not one figure is asserted**: every threshold, sample-size floor, incentive amount and confirmation count in the sources was refused. Agent 1,333 → 3,544 words; both copies diff-identical, lint 2/2, 0 broken links.
- **The Local & International SEO Specialist stops inventing benchmarks — the anti-fabrication sweep reaches the SEO discipline** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-local-and-international.md`](seo/seo-local-and-international.md); counts unchanged at 75 agents / 19 skills). The **Success Metrics** scorecard asserted unsourced round numbers as fact — organic growth *"30-50% monthly … per new market within 12 months"*, ranking *"40%+ of target keywords in top 3 … within 12 months"*, localization *"native-level content quality review scores (80%+)"*, competitive capture *"20-30% share of voice … by month 12"*, lead generation *"100+ qualified leads/demos monthly … within 18 months"*, and citation *"95%+ consistency"* — sitting directly below a section that tells the reader to measure their *own* market. The Deliverables also carried a *"minimum 200-300 keywords per market"* quota. Every metric is rewritten to the sweep's baseline-from-your-own-history pattern with **no fixed figure asserted**: growth, ranking and share of voice are read per market against that market's own pre-launch baseline and printed with the count and date behind them; a **control** is required before any outcome is called *caused* by the SEO work (a market gets ad spend, PR and its own maturing demand over the same window); the *"native-level score"* bullet becomes the review-record process check the agent already defines (a named in-market reviewer per locale, target 100%), since no tool publishes such a score; and international pipeline is handed to the verified `analytics-performance-analyst` as an attribution question rather than a monthly lead count. The keyword quota becomes *"sized to the market's real query space"* (a count target pads a small market and caps a large one). **Left intact as a genuine binary:** the *Hreflang set validity* metric (reciprocity, fully-qualified URLs, valid codes, one `x-default`, self-canonical) keeps its 100% target because a discarded annotation, like every failure in this discipline, reports no error on its own. This is the first of the six flagged SEO personas (the programmatic strategist was already clean); the rest continue one-per-run, matching how the content (2026-08-27) and social (2026-08-28) sweeps were run. The change **removes** fabricated figures and asserts none. Lint 2/2, both copies diff-identical, retired strings grepped to zero, 0 broken links.
- **The Local & International SEO Specialist gains a silent-failure discipline — and stops telling you to configure a Search Console setting Google deleted** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-local-and-international.md`](seo/seo-local-and-international.md); counts unchanged at 75 agents / 19 skills). Two defects in the repo's thinnest SEO agent (849 words). **First, a stale instruction:** Rule 3, a Core Mission bullet and the Hreflang Implementation Specification deliverable all mandated *"geotargeting configuration in Google Search Console"* — a control that no longer exists, since Google states *"the ability to target search results to specific countries using Search Console country targeting was determined to have little value for the ecosystem, and is no longer supported"* ([Search Console Help](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/12474899), read 2026-08-30). All three now name what actually carries geographic relevance (ccTLD, hreflang, server location, on-page local signals). **Second, two controls with no instrument:** Rule 2 mandated *"hreflang correctly with self-referential tags"* with no reciprocity, no `x-default` and no canonical check, and Rule 1 banned auto-translation outright without stating what *is* acceptable — while `x-default`, `machine translation`, `auto-redirect`, `translation memory` and `language switcher` all grepped to **zero** repo-wide. Added a new **"Every International SEO Failure Is Silent"** section, built on the observation that international defects are *non-events* — the pages return 200, the reports are green, and the market never arrives. It covers: the **auto-redirect trap** (Google says *"Avoid automatically redirecting users from one language version of a site to a different language version of a site"*, and the reason it is fatal is on the same page — *"We do not attempt to vary the crawler source"*, so a crawler redirected on arrival never fetches your other locales and no error is ever raised); **hreflang as a set that fails whole** (*"If two pages don't both point to each other, the tags will be ignored"* — plus one `x-default` per set, fully-qualified URLs, ISO 639-1 + ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 codes, and the self-referencing canonical per locale that kills more localized pages than hreflang ever does); **machine translation as a policy question** (Google's spam policy names *"automated transformations like synonymizing, translating, or other obfuscation techniques… where little value is provided to users"*, so the trigger is scale-without-review, not the tool — Rule 1 now sets the standard at *reviewed* and makes the **review record** the deliverable); **parity patterns and drift** (declare mirror / subset / local, then hold every translated page against its source's last-modified date, because a stale localized page is more dangerous than a broken one precisely because it works); and **the in-language demand read** — Search Console segmented by query, page and country together — which closes the un-instrumented rung-1 trigger on the day-old `pmm-international-gtm-strategist`. A closing seam routes market-entry decisions to that agent, crawl/indexation to `seo-technical-auditor`, lawfulness of localized claims to `ops-legal-compliance`, and per-market reporting and rollout series-breaks to `analytics-performance-analyst`. Structure and audit ideas learned from [AgriciDaniel/claude-seo](https://github.com/AgriciDaniel/claude-seo) `seo-hreflang` (MIT) and [rampstackco/claude-skills](https://github.com/rampstackco/claude-skills) `internationalization` (MIT) — ideas only, no text reused, and neither source's unverifiable thresholds carried. **No benchmark or performance figure is asserted anywhere in the new section.** Agent 849 → 2,548 words; both copies diff-identical, lint 2/2, 614 internal links checked and 0 broken.
- **The ABM Strategist now owns corporate gifting and direct mail — as a sequenced touch inside the play, never a channel of its own and never an incentive** (both dual-located copies of [`abm/abm-account-based-strategist.md`](abm/abm-account-based-strategist.md); counts unchanged at 75 agents / 19 skills). `direct mail` and `cold call` grepped to **zero** in agent content repo-wide, yet a gift or a mailer to a named account stakeholder is a classic ABM touch — and Rule 8 already named "the direct-mail send" under suppression without anything owning the send itself. Settled the open ownership question (ABM vs. events) in favour of ABM: the physical touch lives *inside an account play*, so the strategist owns its **placement in the orchestration contract** and nothing else. New **Rule 11** draws three seams to owners that already exist rather than re-deriving them — the consent/suppression query in Rule 8 binds the send; the anti-bribery gift **ceiling** is the one `events-field-marketing-strategist` Rule 7 already draws (5 CFR Part 2635, Sunshine Act/Open Payments, FCPA, UK Bribery Act) plus the recipient's-own-policy constraint `pmm-customer-advocacy` draws, both routed to `ops-legal-compliance` before the send; and the **incentive bright line** (a gift buys attention, never a conditioned action — a "$500 card if you take the call" is an inducement, not an ABM play). Surfaced by [TheCraigHewitt/sales-skills](https://github.com/TheCraigHewitt/sales-skills) `direct-mail` (MIT); ideas only, no text reused. Both copies diff-identical, lint 2/2.
- **The Financial Tracker gains a named renewal-negotiation method — closing a hand-off the stack strategist already routes here** (both dual-located copies of [`client-ops/ops-financial-tracker.md`](client-ops/ops-financial-tracker.md); counts unchanged at 75 agents / 19 skills). The agent owned vendor contracts, renewal dates, the scorecard and "negotiate better terms when possible" (Rules 3–4) and even a *"10-15% cost reduction through negotiation"* success metric — but named **no method** for conducting the renewal itself (the repo's recurring *control named, instrument absent* gap). The seam made it concrete: `analytics-martech-stack-strategist` explicitly hands *"the budget decision, the contract terms and the negotiation itself"* to this agent (its Rules 4 & 10) and **deliberately left the negotiation half unwritten**, so the hand-off landed on an empty method. Added a **"Conducting the Renewal: Leverage Before the Ask"** section — six leverage angles prepared *before* the ask (usage against contracted capacity; the vendor's fiscal-quarter clock, framed as timing not a quotable concession; a credible *named* alternative priced with the switching cost already subtracted; commitment you can trade without locking into shelfware; bundle/package seams; the tenure penalty where long customers quietly pay the most) plus two disciplines: **honesty is the leverage** (a real alternative and a walk-away you'd actually take beat any bluff) and **carry no borrowed thresholds** (the source's "typical" utilization triggers and "standard" discount ranges are unsourced round numbers, anchored instead to this vendor and this account's real usage). Discipline surfaced via [modest-curator478/claude-skills](https://github.com/modest-curator478/claude-skills) `revops/martech-contract-auditor` (MIT), read 2026-08-26; ideas only, written from scratch, its benchmark numbers deliberately not carried — the same stance the stack strategist took. Both copies diff-identical, lint 2/2.
- **The Automation Engineer's one-line "seed send reviewed" becomes a real method — prove *every* branch renders, not just the one your test profile draws** (both dual-located copies of [`email/email-automation-engineer.md`](email/email-automation-engineer.md); counts unchanged at 75 agents / 19 skills). Pre-send checklist **item 7** named a control — *"seed send reviewed in Gmail, Outlook, and one mobile client"* — with no instrument behind it (the repo's recurring *control named, instrument absent* gap), and this is the one agent that actually *builds* branched, personalized email, so the untested arms are its own output. Added a **"The Email You Seed Is One Render of Many"** section: an automation email is a **program, not a document** — it branches on plan tier, lifecycle stage, locale, entitlement flags and empty-state fallbacks, and a seed profile walks exactly one path, hiding the arm that only fires at `seats == 0` or renders a bare `$` with no amount. The method is to **enumerate the state space and proof-render every arm — including the ones nobody has produced**: list each conditional block's firing conditions *and* its fallback, each merge field's present-value *and* missing-value render (never a raw `[FIRST_NAME]` reaching an inbox), build a seed profile per arm to force it, and record a **count** ("6 branches × present/absent fallback, all rendered") rather than a vibe. Added a send-blocking **Gmail clip-line check** (~102 KB of HTML *source* hides the CTA and unsubscribe/legal block below "View entire message" — framed honestly as ESP-documented, not Google-published) and an explicit **seam**: WCAG/alt-text/contrast and the general cross-client render matrix stay owned by `client-ops/ops-quality-assurance` and `design-content-visual-designer`; what this agent owns is proving the branches resolve. Item 7 now points to the method. Method surfaced via the open-source [justinwilliames/orbit-for-claude](https://github.com/justinwilliames/orbit-for-claude) (MIT) skills `liquid-branch-coverage`, `email-render-qa`, `email-production-qa` — ideas only, written from scratch and scoped to the repo's existing QA/accessibility owners; Gmail clip threshold cited to Litmus/Mailchimp with the not-Google-published caveat, verified 2026-08-29. Both copies diff-identical, lint 2/2.
- **Competitive Intelligence learns to read a competitor's tech stack from public signals — a new independent source, held to the repo's evidence discipline** (both dual-located copies of [`product-marketing/pmm-competitive-intelligence.md`](product-marketing/pmm-competitive-intelligence.md); counts unchanged at 74 agents / 19 skills). Rule 1's independent-source mix (product, reviews, pricing, hiring, financial, social) and Rule 9's live-advertising source both omitted the competitor's *technical* stack — the CMS, host/CDN, tag-management, analytics, CRM/CDP, and marketing-email/ESP layers a rival runs, which are legible for free from signals they serve every visitor. Added **Critical Rule 11** and a **"Reading a Competitor's Stack from Public Signals"** section: a signal→layer→confidence table (page source and the live tag container, HTTP headers and cookie names, public DNS MX/SPF/DKIM, DNS/CT-log records, sitemaps and subdomains, the consent banner, and the tools the competitor's own job posts name); the repo's four-state **Confirmed / Inferred / Assumed** labelling with **Unknown held open**; the hard rule that **a clean scan is Unknown, never "no tracking"** (server-side tagging, Conversions-API pipes, first-party CDP proxies, and consent-gated tags are invisible from the outside); a **public-and-authorized-only** boundary that never authenticates, probes behind a login, or crosses a `robots`/rate-limit line (CFAA/ToS deferred to `ops-legal-compliance` per Rule 4); and a **capability-not-performance** framing (a detected CDP means high switching cost, not that they use it well). A clean seam is drawn with `analytics-martech-stack-strategist` (which owns *our own* stack); findings route to the Detailed Competitive Profile's switching-cost line and to `pmm-messaging-architect`/`pmm-positioning-strategist` as a "where they can't see you" note. Added a matching **Competitor Tech-Stack Profile** deliverable and a **Stack-Inference Provenance** success metric. Method surfaced via the open-source [almoretti/martech-ai-skills-and-tools](https://github.com/almoretti/martech-ai-skills-and-tools) `martech-teardown` (MIT; the script-shaped executable direction this repo declines) — ideas only, no text reused, and the evidence-labelling half is the repo's own four-state discipline. Both copies diff-identical, lint 2/2.
- **The Discovery Coach gains a per-call coaching instrument — how to grade one recorded discovery call, not just the deal** (both dual-located copies of [`sales/sales-discovery-coach.md`](sales/sales-discovery-coach.md); counts unchanged at 74 agents / 19 skills). The agent claimed a "Discovery Coaching and Training Program" and a monthly role-play cadence, and its **Discovery Call Review Process** told a manager to review *"quality of discovery"* — a named control with no instrument behind it (the repo's recurring *control named, instrument absent* gap). It already carried a **Qualification Scorecard**, but that grades the *opportunity*; nothing graded the *rep's execution on a single call*. Added a **Single-Call Discovery Scorecard**: pick the framework the call *should* have run on from the existing Discovery Methodology Taxonomy; grade each element **Covered / Partial / Missing** and cite the moment with speaker attribution (`Rep: "…"` / `Buyer: "…"`) — no quote, no Covered; **coach the rep, not the buyer** (the buyer's words are evidence of what the rep's questions produced, never the grade — a new **Critical Rule 9** states this); **lower confidence rather than invent** when a recording is partial or ambiguous; and **return one focus for the next call, not a teardown**. The Review Process now routes to the scorecard, and its evidence-verdict discipline reuses the repo's own four-state evidence practice applied to a transcript. Method surfaced via the open-source [matteotitta/genesys-skills](https://github.com/matteotitta/genesys-skills) `sales-enablement/call-coaching` (MIT), described in our own words, ideas only. Both copies diff-identical, lint 2/2.
- **The YouTube Producer stops inventing benchmarks — and a wrong platform fact is corrected — closing the social anti-fabrication sweep** (both dual-located copies of [`social/social-youtube-producer.md`](social/social-youtube-producer.md); counts unchanged at 74 agents / 19 skills). The **Success Metrics** scorecard asserted unsourced round numbers as fact — View Growth *"10-20% monthly … 3-8% by year one … 5,000-15,000 views by month 12"*, CTR *"0.8-2.5%"* with top videos *"3-5%"*, Watch-Time *"60-75% … 75%+ on tutorials"*, View Duration *"2-4 minutes"* with a *"<1.5min"* revision floor, Subscriber *"2-5% monthly"* with *"30-50%"* from recommendations, Traffic-to-Website *"10-25% … 15%+"*, SEO *"30-50% of keywords in top 20 … 10-20% top 3"*, Engagement *"2-5%"* with a *"1%"* floor, Lead Attribution *"2-3x higher LTV … 5-10% of organic SQLs"*, Consistency *"50%+"*, and Repurposing *"5+ derivative assets"*.
