### Fixed
- **The Product Marketing Ops skill's frontmatter is valid YAML again — a strict SKILL.md parser no longer chokes on the one skill that had malformed metadata** ([`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/product-marketing-ops/SKILL.md`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/product-marketing-ops/SKILL.md); no count change). Its `description:` was a double-quoted YAML scalar containing an unescaped inner quote pair — `and how to answer "we cannot attribute it"` — so `yaml.safe_load` failed at line 2 with *expected block end, but found scalar*. Any tool that parses SKILL.md frontmatter as YAML (rather than grepping for keys, as `scripts/lint-agents.sh` does) would have failed to load this skill's metadata while the other 18 skills and all 82 agent files parsed clean. Fixed by escaping the inner quotes (`\"we cannot attribute it\"`), matching the repo's existing convention for inner double quotes in a double-quoted scalar. The description text is unchanged. Verified: a repo-wide frontmatter parse is now 190/190 clean, 0 bad.

### Added
- **New agent: the International GTM Strategist — the marketing owner of *which country next, how deep, and what that commits you to forever*** (new [`product-marketing/pmm-international-gtm-strategist.md`](product-marketing/pmm-international-gtm-strategist.md), dual-located to [`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/product-marketing-ops/agents/`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/product-marketing-ops/agents/); roster **74 → 75 agents**, skills unchanged at **19**, disciplines at **17**). The territory grepped as unowned: `internationalization`, `localisation`, `EMEA` and `market entry` returned **zero** across the agent roster, and `localization` resolved only to `seo-local-and-international` (hreflang, ccTLD-versus-subfolder, local search) — the *site architecture* of a multi-market presence with nothing above it deciding whether to enter the market at all. The agent ranks candidate markets on **observed pull** (unattended signups, in-language organic traffic, support tickets by timezone, inbound with no campaign behind it, existing customers with offices there) and treats a country TAM figure as a hypothesis, recording unavailable signals as unavailable rather than defaulting them; holds focus to one or two markets at a time because references, channel learning and search presence compound *inside* a market and transfer badly; runs a **four-rung localization ladder** (sell-as-you-are → surface localization → transcreation → full in-market motion) where every rung carries a written trigger **and the standing maintenance bill it creates**, because localization is a subscription rather than a project; sequences the compliance question **before** the campaign on a real legal mechanism — GDPR Article 3(2)(a) with Recital 23, which names *"the use of a language or a currency generally used in one or more Member States … or the mentioning of customers or users who are in the Union"* as the evidence of intent to serve that market, so the translated page with local pricing is what *raises* the question rather than what follows it; and flags that whether your outbound motion is legal is a **per-country** question, not a per-region one, because ePrivacy Article 13(1) requires prior consent for direct marketing by electronic mail while Article 13(5) applies that only to *natural persons* and leaves business recipients to Member-State law, so the answer differs *within* the EU. Also: **proof does not travel** (a market has no references until it has its own, bought deliberately, never manufactured — no invented local customers, no logo without written permission for that market and use, no implied local presence), the channel mix is **rebuilt per market from in-market evidence** with unverified channels labelled unverified, every market is reported as **its own cohort against its own baseline** with counts printed beside rates, and **exit criteria are written before entry** and reviewed on the stated date whether or not anyone asks. Rule 11 draws the seams to `seo-local-and-international`, `pmm-pricing-packaging-strategist`, `pmm-messaging-architect`, `pmm-positioning-strategist`, `partner-ecosystem-marketer`, `events-field-marketing-strategist`, `comms-pr-strategist`, `comms-analyst-relations-manager`, `ops-legal-compliance`, `email-deliverability-specialist`, `email-automation-engineer`, `sales-outbound-strategist`, `analytics-marketing-ops-architect` and `abm-account-based-strategist` — and states plainly that entity formation, tax registration, employment law and the employer-of-record-versus-subsidiary decision are **not marketing's call**. Both EU citations are quoted verbatim from EUR-Lex with read-dates; **no benchmark, conversion, ramp-time or market-size figure is asserted anywhere in the file**.
- **New agent: the Solutions Engineer — the technical evaluation finally has an owner, and "we won the POC and lost the deal" becomes a measurable thing** (new [`sales/sales-solutions-engineer.md`](sales/sales-solutions-engineer.md), dual-located to [`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/sales-enablement/agents/`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/sales-enablement/agents/); roster **73 → 74 agents**, skills unchanged at **19**). Demos, proofs of concept and security questionnaires appeared across four existing agents only as checklist items — `"proof of concept"` and `sales engineer` / `solutions engineer` grepped to **zero** repo-wide — so nobody owned whether the product is believed to work. The agent gates the demo on four written answers (named pain, attendee roles, the buyer's definition of "good", the agreed next step) with an explicit *scoped overview* escape hatch instead of a silent skip; runs a POC as a **contract** with falsifiable, dated success criteria signed by the evaluator who will judge them, the closing question asked at the **start**, the criterion you expect to fail declared up front, and no mid-flight scope change without re-signature; answers every capability question in exactly **four states** from one maintained answer library so the demo, the questionnaire and the RFP cannot contradict each other; keeps a **commitment register** so no deferred answer or roadmap reference goes uncleared; treats the demo environment as a **privacy surface** (no customer records, no live-account screenshots, no production access); and records the **technical win** as a dated state with a *named grantor*, splitting technical losses into five modes so the fixable *perceived-gap* bucket stops hiding inside "product gaps". Rule 10 draws the seams to `sales-deal-strategist`, `sales-proposal-architect`, `sales-discovery-coach`, `sales-enablement-content-creator`, `pmm-competitive-intelligence`, `pmm-launch-manager`, `ops-legal-compliance` and `growth-plg-activation-strategist`. No benchmark or win-rate figure is asserted anywhere in the file.
- **New agent: the MarTech Stack Strategist — the marketing tech stack finally has an owner, and a platform migration stops being a surprise** (new [`analytics/analytics-martech-stack-strategist.md`](analytics/analytics-martech-stack-strategist.md), dual-located to [`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/marketing-analytics/agents/`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/marketing-analytics/agents/analytics-martech-stack-strategist.md); roster **72 → 73 agents**, Analytics **5 → 6**, skill count unchanged at 19). The repo *claimed* this ground and did not hold it: `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`'s description opened with "Marketing technology stack" while its 8,600 words are entirely about the data model *inside* the stack — lead lifecycle, scoring, MAP/CRM integration, consent, field governance, the instrumentation contract. Nothing decided **which systems exist**, whether one earns its place, or what replacing one costs. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`martech`, `tool consolidation` and `database health` returned zero hits repo-wide**. The agent's organising argument is that **a platform change is a measurement break before it is a migration** — swapping a MAP, ESP, CRM, analytics property or CMP voids the instrumentation contract and breaks every series computed from the old system, with no error, no gap in the chart, and a new number that looks perfectly plausible — so it is filed in `analytics-performance-analyst`'s series-break register (which gained a row for whole-instrument replacement in the same change) and the contract is re-issued **before** cutover. Around that it ships: **the suppression record as the one dataset with no acceptable loss rate**, grounded in the statute that both requires and permits the transfer — it is unlawful to "sell, lease, exchange, or otherwise transfer or release the electronic mail address" of an opted-out recipient "for any purpose other than compliance with this chapter or other provision of law" ([15 U.S.C. § 7704(a)(4)(A)(iv)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/15/7704), read 2026-08-26), with counts reconciled on both sides before the first send; **engagement history usually does not travel**, so "opened in the last 90 days" is empty on day one and every sunset rule, re-engagement trigger and engagement-gated segment needs a carry / rebuild / freeze decision before cutover; **capability-first selection** that checks what you already own before any vendor is shortlisted; **shadow tools in the register** because an inventory of what finance knows about is not an inventory; **overlap as a contact-collision problem** before a cost one, since two tools sending to one audience bypass either one's frequency cap; **switching cost split into what you pay and what you lose**, with losses never folded into a savings number; **personalization re-authored and branch-tested**, since an unresolved merge tag renders blank rather than erroring; and **an exit-and-portability record written at purchase** — 1,367 martech products left the 2026 landscape of 15,505 in a single year ([chiefmartec / MartechTribe](https://chiefmartec.com/2026/05/2026-marketing-technology-landscape-supergraphic-peak-martech-achieved-maybe/), read 2026-08-26), so vendor exit is a base rate. Critical Rule 10 draws explicit boundaries against seven existing agents: `ops-financial-tracker` keeps the budget, contract terms and negotiation; `analytics-marketing-ops-architect` keeps everything inside the systems (its description was rewritten in the same change so the two no longer both claim the stack); `ops-legal-compliance` keeps DPAs, consent law and the cross-channel suppression record. Ideas-only, written from scratch, MIT-credited to [NEON-Rutger/B2B-revops-skills](https://github.com/NEON-Rutger/B2B-revops-skills), [justinwilliames/orbit-for-claude](https://github.com/justinwilliames/orbit-for-claude) and [modest-curator478/claude-skills](https://github.com/modest-curator478/claude-skills). **No stack-size, consolidation-saving, migration-duration or tool-count benchmark is asserted** — every source's figures ("55% of CRM initiatives fail", "70% of contracted capacity", "15-25% for a 2-year commitment", "3x post-migration cost") were refused as unsourced or vaguely attributed. Discoverability sweep ran in the same commit (README badge + tagline + discipline and skills tables, `AGENTS_INDEX.md`, `llms.txt`, `CITATION.cff`, both manifests, the suite router, the CATALYST orchestrator tables, and the GitHub About description). Lint 73/73, all 73 dual-located pairs byte-identical, 570 internal links / 0 broken.
- **New agent: the Sponsorship & Syndication Buyer — the B2B media no auction sells finally has a buyer** (new [`paid-media/paid-media-sponsorship-syndication-buyer.md`](paid-media/paid-media-sponsorship-syndication-buyer.md), dual-located to [`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/paid-media-ops/agents/`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/paid-media-ops/agents/paid-media-sponsorship-syndication-buyer.md); roster **71 → 72 agents**, Paid Media **6 → 7**, skill count unchanged at 19). Newsletter, podcast and community sponsorships, industry-publication placements, **paid review-site listings on G2/Capterra/TrustRadius/Software Advice**, and **pay-per-lead content syndication** are all media a B2B SaaS team buys from a publisher rather than winning in an auction — and the repo had no owner for any of them. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`content syndication`, `insertion order`, `pay-per-lead`, `media kit` (as a buy-side artifact), `vanity URL`, `make-good`, `G2 category` and `review generation` returned zero hits repo-wide**; `sponsorship` resolved only to the *sell* side (`email-newsletter-growth-strategist` monetizing our own newsletter) and to conference sponsorship (`events-field-marketing-strategist`). The agent's organising argument is the asymmetry that defines the channel: **in an auction the platform counts the impressions and you argue about price; here the seller counts everything and the argument is about the truth** — every figure on a media kit was produced by the party being paid, and none of it is audited. It ships a provenance ladder for quoted numbers (public rate card > directory > a figure in an email), a pulse-before-price check, **repeat sponsors as the only external audit available**, the placement-versus-endorsement line that routes an in-voice endorsement to `social-influencer-partnerships` under the 16 CFR § 255.5 disclosure discipline, a measurement plan that has to be a contract term because there is no pixel to retrofit, the rule that **one insertion is not a test**, a deliverables register with a renewal-amnesia rule, and a paid-review-listing section holding a bright line that money buys placement and never sentiment. Two primary-source contract facts do the heaviest lifting, both quoted from the [AAAA/IAB Standard Terms and Conditions v3.0](https://www.iab.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/06/IAB_4As-tsandcs-FINAL.pdf) (read 2026-08-23): the standard's own preamble says it "may not fully cover sponsorships and other arrangements involving content association or integration" and "is not meant to cover ... direct advertiser buys with publishers" — so the terms most digital IOs incorporate **exclude the deals B2B SaaS actually does**; and §VI(c) states that on CPA/CPL/CPC deliverables "guaranteed delivery, even delivery, and makegoods are not available" — so **the moment you buy content syndication on a cost-per-lead basis you give up the delivery guarantee**, and the protection has to be replaced with a negotiated lead-acceptance standard and rejection-credit path. Ideas-only, written from scratch, MIT-credited to [coreyhaines31/marketingskills](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills), [LeadMagic/gtm-skills](https://github.com/LeadMagic/gtm-skills), [maryakul/influencer-profile-research](https://github.com/maryakul/influencer-profile-research) and [guerrilla2799/event-and-scale-os](https://github.com/guerrilla2799/event-and-scale-os). **No CPM, CPL, conversion-rate or channel benchmark figure is asserted.** Lint 72/72, copies diff-identical, 532 internal links / 0 broken.
- **New agent: the Podcast & Audio Strategist — the B2B show, the guest tour, and the two questions nobody owned** (new [`social/social-podcast-strategist.md`](social/social-podcast-strategist.md), dual-located to [`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/social-media-ops/agents/`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/social-media-ops/agents/social-podcast-strategist.md); roster **70 → 71 agents**, Social Media **6 → 7**, skill count unchanged at 19). Audio was the largest untouched channel in the repo. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`audiogram`, `RSS`, `Apple Podcasts`, `Spotify`, `podcast tour`, `podcast guesting`, `guest booking`, and `host-read` returned zero hits repo-wide**, and the only substantive `show notes` line in the repo sat in `social-youtube-producer`'s deliverables as a handoff to an owner that did not exist — the repo's recurring *dead-ending handoff* pattern. The agent starts where the market's podcast skills don't: **the go/no-go on having a show at all** (a four-leg readiness test — named host with a protected calendar block, named producer, 8–12 episodes banked before launch, a cadence holdable in the team's worst month — with the guest tour as the honest recommendation when a leg is missing, because an abandoned feed stays public), and **the guest list as an account plan** sourced from `abm-account-based-strategist`'s target list rather than from follower counts, under a bright line that the invitation is not a sales sequence and the recording is not a discovery call. Ships six further mechanics: **a download is a file request, not a person** — the IAB Tech Lab [Podcast Measurement Technical Guidelines](https://iabtechlab.com/standards/podcast-measurement-guidelines/) (v2.2, May 2024, read 2026-08-19) measure from server logs and treat *downloads*, *audience* and *ad delivery* as separate categories, so the B2B scoreboard is guest-to-opportunity progression, target-account reach, sales usage and influenced pipeline under the same credit rule the ABM agent imposes elsewhere; **the transcript is the compounding asset and must be proofread**, because ASR reliably mangles product, person and category names and the error becomes the canonical text an answer engine lifts (citability graded by `seo-ai-search-optimizer`, indexation by `seo-content-optimizer`); **record video by default, but the YouTube channel is not yours** (`social-youtube-producer` keeps watch-time, thumbnails and playlists); **rights, consent and takedown settled in writing before the record button**, with a customer guest also routed through `pmm-customer-advocacy`'s expiring consent record and the legal verdict left to `ops-legal-compliance`; **material connections disclosed in the spoken audio, not only the show notes** — paid guest placements, sponsored appearances, compensated partners and gifts, under [16 CFR § 255.5 "Disclosure of material connections"](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/16/part-255) (read 2026-08-19), distinct from the Part 465 review rule already cited by `pmm-customer-advocacy`; and **the guest tour as an earned placement** qualified on observable audience evidence rather than self-reported download claims, with genuine press outlets routed to `comms-pr-strategist`. Critical Rule 10 draws explicit non-overlap boundaries against nine existing agents, including the seam where a host relationship becomes *compensated* and moves to `social-influencer-partnerships`. Ideas-only, MIT-credited to three independent open-source podcast skills read and licence-verified 2026-08-19 — [OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills](https://github.com/OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills) `podcast-marketing`, [social-media-skills/skills](https://github.com/social-media-skills/skills) `podcast-and-audiograms`, [OneWave-AI/claude-skills](https://github.com/OneWave-AI/claude-skills) `podcast-content-suite`; none of the three owns the go/no-go or the guest tour, and the fullest of them is built for a creator media business (sponsorship CPMs, download charts, monetization ladders), which is exactly the scoreboard Rule 4 rejects for B2B. **No download, listener, conversion or pipeline benchmark asserted** — every success metric is a definition, not a number. Discoverability sweep ran in the same commit (README badge + tagline + team table + skills table, `AGENTS_INDEX.md`, `llms.txt`, `CITATION.cff`, both manifests, the suite router, the CATALYST orchestrator tables, and the GitHub About description). Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical, 0 broken links across 480 internal links.

### Changed
- **The B2B Influencer & Creator Partnerships Manager learns to verify an audience before paying for it — and learns whose problem the FTC disclosure actually is** (both dual-located copies of [`social/social-influencer-partnerships.md`](social/social-influencer-partnerships.md), dual-located to [`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/social-media-ops/agents/`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/social-media-ops/agents/social-influencer-partnerships.md); counts unchanged at 73 agents / 19 skills). The agent named creator vetting as a control in three places — Rule 1's *"validate engagement rate before outreach"*, Rule 2's audience-overlap mapping, and a Deliverables line promising a *"creator evaluation framework with scoring criteria"* — and supplied **no method for any of them**, the repo's recurring *control named, instrument absent* pattern. Worse, the thresholds it did state were invented: a *"15%+ engagement rate"* entry gate, a *"5-10x more value"* multiplier, and an Identity contrast between *"100K followers (1% engaged)"* and *"10K followers (30% engaged)"*. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`fake follower`, `bought follower`, `engagement pod`, `inflated audience`, `audience credibility` and `follower audit` returned zero hits repo-wide**, and `FTC`, `disclosure` and `material connection` returned **zero hits in this agent** — the one agent in the repo that owns compensated creator relationships said nothing about the disclosure law that governs them. Shipped as a new **"Verifying an Audience Before You Pay for It"** section, three rewritten rules, three success metrics and a source footer. The organising argument is the asymmetry `paid-media-sponsorship-syndication-buyer` Rule 1 names for outlets, sharpened: **an outlet sells a slot and an under-delivered slot can be made good; a creator sells credibility, and there is no make-good for having borrowed the wrong person's.** Around it: **read the median and its spread, never the mean**, from the last 20-30 posts *of the type you are actually buying*, because a mean is set by an account's best two posts and the mean is the figure that gets quoted to you; **grade the comments rather than counting them** — the job titles under the replies are the closest thing to an audience export you will get, an audience of aspirants is a real audience that is not a pipeline, and the same accounts arriving within minutes of every post is the observable signature of an arrangement LinkedIn's own [Professional Community Policies](https://www.linkedin.com/legal/professional-community-policies) prohibit ("don't agree with others ahead of time to like or re-share each other's content", read 2026-08-27); **read the sponsored-post history, not the organic feed**, for cadence and category adjacency; and the correction almost no program makes — **the number you priced against was earned by posts nobody paid for**, so a fee negotiated against an organic median buys a disclosed advertisement that will not reach it, and pricing moves to the creator's *previous sponsored* posts where they exist. Two primary-source facts do the legal work, both read 2026-08-27: the FTC's review rule makes it an unfair or deceptive practice to "[p]urchase or procure fake indicators of social media influence that they knew or should have known to be fake" ([16 CFR § 465.8(b)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/16/465.8)), with those indicators defined to cover followers, subscribers, views, likes, shares and comments "generated by bots, purported individual accounts not associated with a real individual … or hijacked accounts" ([§ 465.1](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/16/465.1)) — the *knew or should have known* standard being precisely why the diligence has to be a dated file rather than a recollection, with the question of how far the provision reaches routed to `ops-legal-compliance`; and the Endorsement Guides put the liability on the brand, not the creator — "Advertisers are subject to liability for misleading or unsubstantiated statements made through endorsements or for failing to disclose unexpected material connections between themselves and their endorsers," with a standing direction to **monitor their endorsers' compliance** ([16 CFR § 255.1(d)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/16/255.1)) — which turns disclosure into three operational steps, the third of which (open the live post, in the view a reader sees, and file a dated screenshot) is the one that goes missing. A final boundary makes vetting a creator different from auditing an outlet: **you are compiling a file on a named individual**, so the record stays to published, professionally relevant, campaign-necessary evidence, never infers a protected or sensitive characteristic, and leaves lawful basis and retention to `ops-legal-compliance`. Ideas-only, written from scratch, MIT-credited to [ScrapeCreators/social-media-research-skills](https://github.com/ScrapeCreators/social-media-research-skills) (1,785★ — outlier-against-own-baseline, multi-dimension prospect scoring) and [rdsolod-ui/insta-parcel-skill](https://github.com/rdsolod-ui/insta-parcel-skill) (source-strength hierarchy, disallowed-inference boundary); both are consumer-creator research over a paid social-data API, and the B2B reframing, the sponsored-versus-organic pricing correction and the FTC grounding are ours. **The change removes three fabricated benchmarks and asserts no engagement-rate, overlap, reach or pricing figure of its own.** Lint 2/2, both copies diff-identical, 544 internal links / 0 broken.
- **The Content Newsletter Curator's scorecard stops inventing benchmarks — the densest remaining fabricated-metric cluster in the content agents is retired** (both dual-located copies of [`content/content-newsletter-curator.md`](content/content-newsletter-curator.md); counts unchanged at 73 agents / 19 skills). The Success Metrics section asserted unsourced round numbers as fact — list growth *"5-10% monthly"*, open rate *"25-35%"* (with *"25-30%"* / *"35-45%"* sub-ranges), CTR *"4-8%"*, unsubscribe *"0.1-0.3% healthy / above 0.5%"*, conversion *"1-3%"* (*"2-5%"* / *"1-2%"* by segment), lead share *"15-25%"*, lifetime/expansion *"5-10%"* / *"10-15%"*, re-engagement *"15-25%"*, revenue influence *"8-12%"*, segmentation lift *"20-30%"* / *"25-35%"*, content-mix *"3-5x"* / *"2-3x"* — the same anti-fabrication defect cleared from `content-copywriter` (2026-08-25), `content-video-script-writer` (2026-08-26) and `content-case-study-producer` (2026-08-26). Each is rewritten to the definition / baseline-from-your-own-history pattern with **no fixed figure asserted**: rates are read against this newsletter's own trend and reported with the count behind them; segmentation and customer-engagement claims now demand a **control or matched cohort** (with the caution that engagement correlates with account health, so the causal claim needs the control); revenue influence is framed as an artifact of the attribution model, not a benchmark to defend. The **open-rate contamination discipline** added 2026-08-14 (name the instrument, filtered vs. unfiltered, directional-only) is preserved but the *"25-35%"* assertion behind it is dropped. The duplicated *"0.1-0.3% / 0.5%"* benchmark in Critical Rule 8 was re-based to your own per-send baseline in the same pass so the figure is grepped to zero. Explicitly-illustrative example copy (send-time A/B examples like *"8am vs. 12pm"*, the *"200-400 words"* length guide, cadence heuristics) was deliberately left intact. Experiment-trust rules cross-referenced to `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`, subject-line validity to `email-copywriter`, contamination mechanism to `email-deliverability-specialist` Rule 9. The change **removes** fabricated figures and asserts none. Lint 2/2, both copies diff-identical, old strings grepped to zero.

Each is rewritten to the definition / coverage / baseline-from-your-own-history pattern with **no fixed figure asserted**: the accelerator, win-rate and testimonial-lift claims now demand a **control or matched cohort** (with an explicit caution that case-study exposure correlates with deal engagement, so the causal claim needs the control); volume and CPL are read against the program's own prior gated assets; portfolio and participation become coverage signals set to the sales motion's own need, not a standard. - **The Content Copywriter's scorecard stops inventing benchmarks — eight fabricated conversion figures replaced with measure-against-your-own-baseline metrics** (both dual-located copies of [`content/content-copywriter.md`](content/content-copywriter.md); counts unchanged at 72 agents / 19 skills). The entire Success Metrics section asserted unsourced round numbers as fact — *"8-15% conversion target (organic 8-12%, paid 10-18%, email 12-25%)"*, *"3-5% email CTR baseline, targeting 20-30% lift"*, *"Primary CTA generates 30-50% of all page actions"*, *"Benefit-focused headlines outperform feature-focused by 15-30%"*, social proof *"20-35%"*, BOFU *"50%+ higher"*, consistency *"10-15%"* — the exact anti-fabrication defect the PMM agents were cleaned of on 2026-08-24/25, now reaching the content discipline. The **"A/B test win rate: 60%+ of copy tests generate a statistically significant winner"** metric was doubly wrong — a fabricated figure *and* the same *"60-70% of tests win"* quota the repo retired from `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer` on 2026-08-10 because a test forced to produce a winner invites peeking; it is now an **A/B test *verdict* rate** (valid, powered, one-variable tests resolving to a real winner or a real null). Experiment-trust rules are cross-referenced to `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`, subject-line validity to `email-copywriter`, and cross-surface coherence to `pmm-messaging-architect`. The change **removes** fabricated figures and asserts none. Lint 2/2, both copies diff-identical, 0 broken links.
- **The Competitive Intelligence agent's research-ethics citation is refreshed to the ICC/Esomar Code's 2025 fifth edition — the previous citation had silently gone stale on every article number** (both dual-located copies of [`product-marketing/pmm-competitive-intelligence.md`](product-marketing/pmm-competitive-intelligence.md); counts unchanged at 72 agents / 19 skills). The *Three Records, Three Questions* section grounds "the rep who lost the deal must not conduct the interview" in the profession's own standard, and quoted the **2016 edition** with an in-line note that a 2025 fifth edition existed but was gated behind a language-selection form and had not been read. It has now been located as a clean primary PDF on ESOMAR's own standards host ([standards.esomar.org/…/icc-esomar-code-2025.pdf](https://standards.esomar.org/assets/documents/icc-esomar-code-2025.pdf), © ICC/Esomar 2025, "Updated as of July 2025," HTTP 200) and read in full — and **every cited article had renumbered or moved**, so the citation was pointing at a superseded edition: the distinction-of-activities duty moved from **Art. 1(c)→1(d)** (now grounded explicitly in public confidence) and **Art. 1(d)→1(e)**; the identify-and-state-purpose duty stayed at **Art. 4(a)** but was reworded, with the participant's refusal right relocated to **Art. 1(a)** ("right to decline participation"); the old **Art. 6(a)** non-research-purpose-disclosure duty folded into **Art. 1(e)** (2025 Art. 6(a) is now a different provision, on client data transfer); and the parallel non-researcher obligation moved from **"Article 9 of the consolidated ICC marketing code" → "Article 7 of the ICC Advertising and Marketing Communications Code,"** its wording expanded to name consumer reviews and social-media postings. Both copies rewritten with all quotes verbatim from the 2025 edition, the URL and edition descriptor updated, and the "not read" caveat dropped. The change updates a citation and asserts no new claim or figure. Closes backlog line 112. Lint 2/2, both copies diff-identical, 0 broken links.
- **The Link Building Strategist learns the half of organic visibility that happens on other people's pages** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-link-building-strategist.md`](seo/seo-link-building-strategist.md); no count change). On a B2B SaaS company's highest-intent queries — `best [category] software`, `[category] tools`, `[competitor] alternatives` — the results are review-platform category pages, software directories and publisher roundups. `seo-content-optimizer` already rules those SERPs structurally unwinnable with a page you host and stops there; the AEO/GEO playbook lists keeping G2 / Capterra / TrustRadius entries current as "table stakes" and names no owner. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`listicle`, `best software`, `directory submission`, `vendor profile` and `third-party page` returned zero hits repo-wide**, `roundup` resolved only to HARO expert-quote roundups and to affiliate roundups named as a SERP obstacle, and `nofollow` existed solely as an indexing directive on `seo-technical-auditor` — never as a link-qualification attribute, with `rel="sponsored"` absent entirely. Shipped as **Critical Rule 10**, a **"Ranking Inside Pages You Don't Own"** section, a **Third-Party Shortlist Presence Map** deliverable, and two success metrics. The organising idea: the shortlist on those pages is handed to a buyer with or without you, and the page has an editor — so **the correction is the highest-yield ask and the least attempted one** (an evidence diff, not a pitch, on the entries you already have), entries are graded **absent / present-and-stale / present-and-current** with unknown never rounding to current, and the work is reported as *presence coverage* rather than folded into the link count, where a nofollowed high-intent placement scores zero and a purchased slot would score as earned authority. Two policy facts do the heavy lifting, both quoted from primary sources read 2026-08-25: Google's spam policies name *"Low-quality directory or bookmark site links"* as link spam and state the exemption plainly — bought links are fine *"as long as they are qualified with a `rel="nofollow"` or `rel="sponsored"` attribute value"* ([Google Search spam policies](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/essentials/spam-policies)) — which makes a sponsored roundup slot `paid-media-sponsorship-syndication-buyer`'s buy that must never be logged as an earned link; and G2 publishes that review weight decays on a schedule, *"roughly ... -3% per month or -30% per year,"* with market presence built from *"15 metrics"* including company size, web presence and vendor age ([G2 research guidelines](https://research.g2.com/research-guidelines)) — so a one-off review drive is a depreciating asset (the program itself stays with `pmm-customer-advocacy`) and a promised grid position is a promise about someone else's algorithm. Ideas-only, MIT-credited to four independent collections licence-verified this run ([LeadMagic/gtm-skills](https://github.com/LeadMagic/gtm-skills), [coreyhaines31/marketingskills](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills), [FradSer/dotclaude](https://github.com/FradSer/dotclaude), [teachskillofskills-ai/DigitalMarketingPro-techshu](https://github.com/teachskillofskills-ai/DigitalMarketingPro-techshu)). **No coverage, placement-rate, review-volume or grid-position benchmark is asserted** — the first inventory is the baseline. - **The win/loss program gets its missing instrument — three evidence records, three different questions, and a sample that is allowed to say "not readable yet"** ([`product-marketing/pmm-competitive-intelligence.md`](product-marketing/pmm-competitive-intelligence.md), both dual-located copies). New **Critical Rule 10** and a **Three Records, Three Questions** section supply it: the CRM close-reason field is a *census* that measures how reps classify losses and never why buyers left; a recorded sales call shows *when* the deal turned, not why, because the seller was in the room; only a post-decision interview by someone with nothing to sell can answer *why*. **Win rate is a census statistic the interview sample cannot measure**, rep-selected and buyer-stated reasons are never summed, and every per-competitor or per-segment claim carries its own cell count — because 20 interviews split across won/lost and 3-5 competitors averages two to three per cell. - **AEO/GEO freshness pass — the playbook and the AI-search agent gain the strongest independent caveat yet on GEO effect sizes: a 2026 critical survey finds the causal gains are bounded to already-retrieved content** ([`guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md`](guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md) §2 + Sources + `Last reviewed` → 2026-08-23; the same bound added to the directional-caveat paragraph in both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md); counts unchanged at 71 agents / 19 skills). Both files lean on the KDD-2024 GEO effect sizes (quotations +40%, statistics +33%, …) — the agent turns them into a citability scoring rubric. A critical survey of GEO reviewing **45 studies from 2023–2026** ([Martinez, arXiv:2607.14035](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14035), July 2026) puts a hard bound on them: *"no reviewed technique shows a stable, longitudinal, cross-platform causal effect on organic discoverability,"* and the one effect that replicates is narrow — only **already-retrieved content can causally alter its own citation or use**, with commercial GEO tools showing *"low source overlap, substantial run-to-run variability, and persistent fidelity gaps."* This maps onto the playbook's own discovery-vs-citation split: the levers reliably improve how a page the engine *already pulled* gets quoted (the AEO half), but none is proven to make an engine *retrieve* you (the discovery half — correlational only). The playbook gains a flagged "Reality check" callout after the effect-size table; the agent's rubric caveat now states the score judges whether a *retrieved* passage earns the citation and does not promise retrieval. Verified against the primary source (arXiv abstract, read 2026-08-23), all quotes verbatim; no effect sizes changed (none were contradicted, only bounded). - **The Performance Analyst now checks the export is intact before it trusts the series — silent row-limit truncation, and placeholder values parsed as real numbers** (both dual-located copies of [`analytics/analytics-performance-analyst.md`](analytics/analytics-performance-analyst.md) gain a **"Read the export before you read the series"** subsection inside *Reading a Change Before Explaining It*, placed *upstream of* the existing comparability freeze; counts unchanged at 71 agents / 19 skills). The agent already froze comparability and respected cohort maturity, but both assume the two periods in hand are complete and correctly typed — and two silent failures upstream of that arrive looking like ordinary data. **Silent export truncation:** many tools cap an export at a fixed row count (a GA4 exploration, a CRM report, a saved-list export, a BI copy-paste) with nothing on the file to say so, so the rows are the first *N* under some sort order, not the period; the tells are mechanical (a suspiciously round count like 1,000/10,000/50,000, a total that stops rising as the date range widens, a "last" row that isn't the last day) and the check is one reconciliation of the export's row count and summed total against the tool's own headline figure for the identical filter before any period math. **Placeholder values parsed as numbers:** a field a calculation depends on often carries a sentinel where the value is missing (an empty cell or `0` coerced to zero, a `-1`/`9999`/`N/A` for "unknown," a default `1900-01-01`), and none of them error — a zero cost reads as infinite ROAS, a `9999`-day sales cycle drags every average, a `-1` "score not set" sums in as real; this is the section's own *absent is not zero* rule turned upstream, and the check is to look at a field's distribution for a spike at exactly 0/1/−1/9999 before it feeds a numerator or denominator. Grep-verified genuinely absent before writing — `truncat` existed only as the narrow GSC page-decay guard in `seo-content-optimizer`, every `placeholder` hit was about *copy* placeholders, and `9999`/`sentinel`/`magic number` returned nothing repo-wide. - **Conversion Rate Optimizer: the sample-size calculation is now allowed to conclude "do not run this test"** (both copies of [`analytics/analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer.md`](analytics/analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer.md) gain **Critical Rule 10**, a **"Before You Design the Test: Can This Funnel Power It?"** section, a **Test Feasibility Record** deliverable, and a **Feasibility Discipline** success metric; counts unchanged at 71 agents / 19 skills). Rule 2 already required a sample-size calculation and the 2026-08-10 trust layer already governed whether a *finished* test is readable — neither answered whether the funnel can produce the sample at all. Because required sample scales with the **inverse square** of the effect, a B2B page converting at 3% tested for a 20% relative lift needs ≈13,900 visitors per arm — roughly nine months at 3,000 visits a month (recomputed from the standard formula at Z = 1.96 / 0.84; illustrative inputs, not asserted as typical). The new argument is what nine months *means*: **the ceiling on test duration is comparability, not patience.** Every surface has a **comparability horizon** — the longest window it has historically gone unchanged in any way that moves the metric — and a test needing longer than that is not slow, it is unreadable, because the page, the offer, the pricing and the tracking will all move underneath it. Ships five reroutes ranked by the inverse-square property (test a bigger swing · move upstream · pool near-identical surfaces · move it to the email list · randomize the account, not the session), a sixth legitimate outcome — **decide without a test, logged as a decision and not a result** — and an explicit refusal of three moves the market lists as peers: lowering the confidence threshold from 95% to 90% (≈21% off the sample requirement, roughly double the false-winner rate), adding arms, and switching to Bayes because N is small. On that last one: **a posterior is not more data** — at small N it is substantially shaped by the prior, so declare the prior before the data and never report P(best) without **expected loss** beside it, which prices being wrong in the metric's own units and makes **practical equivalence** available as a terminal verdict that retires a question instead of re-opening it every quarter. Adaptive allocation gets two objections: a bandit optimizes the metric visible *now* while the B2B metric that decides (SQL, pipeline) lands weeks later, and an adaptive split disables the flat-split sample-ratio check, which must then compare observed exposures against the allocator's *intended* allocation per period or state SRM as unverifiable. Ideas-only, MIT-credited to [coreyhaines31/marketingskills](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills), [OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills](https://github.com/OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills), [sidchaudhary/gtm-skills](https://github.com/sidchaudhary/gtm-skills) and [thatrebeccarae/claude-marketing](https://github.com/thatrebeccarae/claude-marketing), all four licences verified via the GitHub license API 2026-08-21. **No conversion rate, win rate, or lift figure asserted as typical** — the worked figures are arithmetic from a stated formula, verified by recomputation.
- **The Keyword Researcher now maps the query landscape AI answer engines actually get asked — not just the typed keywords a volume tool can see** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-keyword-researcher.md`](seo/seo-keyword-researcher.md) — new **Critical Rule 9**, a **"The AI-Answer Query Landscape: Discover What Classic Volume Can't See"** section, an **AI-Answer Query Landscape Map** deliverable, and an **AI-query landscape coverage** success metric; frontmatter, `AGENTS_INDEX.md`, and the `seo-growth` skill table updated to name the capability). The repo's designated "maps the entire addressable search landscape" agent was silent on AI search — while `seo-ai-search-optimizer` scores citability and recognition **against a query set it assumes already exists** (its share-of-voice heatmap needs rows, its brand-question set and "5–10 core topics" need discovering), and `seo-programmatic-strategist` Rule 10 already declares "Keyword Researcher hands you the *pattern* and the entity set" — a reference that dead-ended because the Keyword Researcher never mentioned it. Two of the repo's signature patterns at once: *control named, instrument absent* (the AEO program measures a landscape nobody discovers) and a *dead-ending handoff*. Gap grep-verified: the Keyword Researcher had **zero** mentions of AI search, AEO, prompts, conversational queries, query fan-out, or entity sets. The correction gives it the discovery half and draws a clean seam: reframe head/commercial terms into the natural-language questions people ask assistants, build the **follow-up tree** (the buyer's *next* question is the valuable unit), account for Google's **query fan-out** (the prompt typed is not the query answered — one prompt explodes into many sub-questions, each resolved from a different passage; per the AEO/GEO Playbook), mine real phrasing (People Also Ask, community threads, sales-call transcripts, support tickets) rather than inventing it, and cluster by *the answer an engine would synthesize* rather than by lexical overlap. The load-bearing rule is **zero volume is not zero demand** — a conversational query classic tools score at zero may be the exact question a high-intent account asks the week before it shortlists, so it is flagged *unmeasured — no volume denominator*, never *low-priority* (Rule 1's value-over-volume carried to its limit; *unknown never rounds to zero*). The seam is explicit: discovery here; citability scoring, the recognition test, the heatmap, and citation claims stay with `seo-ai-search-optimizer`, and the template-able question-shape-plus-entity-set goes to `seo-programmatic-strategist`. Self-directed gap, no external source; the query-fan-out mechanism references the repo's own AEO/GEO Playbook (already cited to Google). **No search-volume, traffic, or demand figure asserted** — the new metric reports questions-observed-in-a-real-source versus questions-inferred, never a percentage. - **The Link Building Strategist learns the defensive half of the job — the inbound profile, and disavow as a gated last resort rather than monthly hygiene** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-link-building-strategist.md`](seo/seo-link-building-strategist.md) — new **Critical Rule 9**, a **"The Links You Didn't Earn: The Inbound Audit and the Disavow of Last Resort"** section, an **Inbound Profile Audit & Disavow Decision Record** deliverable, and a **Disavow discipline** success metric; plus a one-line reciprocal seam in both copies of [`seo/seo-technical-auditor.md`](seo/seo-technical-auditor.md)). The agent earned links in eight rules and never once addressed the links pointing *at* the site — the toxic profile inherited from a prior agency, the scraper/PBN noise, a competitor's "negative SEO." Gap grep-verified before writing: **`disavow`, `negative seo`, `backlink audit`, `toxic link`, and `link audit` returned zero repo-wide**, while the only `unnatural link` / `reconsideration` hits sat in `seo-technical-auditor`'s manual-action branch — which reads the binary and says "route to remediation plus a reconsideration request" to an owner that did not exist until now (the repo's recurring *dead-ending handoff* pattern). The correction is a posture, not a tool: because Google's SpamBrain **nullifies** spammy links algorithmically since the December 2022 link spam update — "the link credit that was previously generated is lost… this is not a manual action" — the standing action on an ugly inbound link is **do nothing and record why**, and the "toxic link score" every third-party tool sells is cargo-cult work. Disavow is gated on the one binary that matters: an *"Unnatural links to your site"* manual action, after which remediation follows Google's stated order — **remove first** (the root-cause fix, logged for the reviewer), **disavow only the remainder**, and **write the reconsideration request as a narrative, not a link dump** — under the tool's own warning that it "can potentially harm your site's performance" if it disavows links that are actually helping. The seam is completed reciprocally: `seo-technical-auditor` now names `seo-link-building-strategist` as the owner of the unnatural-links case. No external skill used; posture quoted from and cited to Google primary docs read 2026-08-19 — the [December 2022 link spam update](https://developers.google.com/search/blog/2022/12/december-22-link-spam-update), the [Disavow links Search Console Help](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/2648487), and the [Manual actions report](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/9044175). **No ranking, recovery-time, or link-count figure asserted.** Roster unchanged at 71 agents / 19 skills (no discoverability sweep owed). Ships four mechanics: **one matched audience/campaign per tier** (a blended list forfeits per-tier budget, frequency, and reporting and lets delivery concentrate off the strategic few; a tier under LinkedIn's 300-match floor routes to another channel rather than blends up to size); **match rate is coverage, not setup** (Meta's hash-and-match means a work-email-only B2B list matches a smaller share of itself, fixed by more identifiers per contact and preferring LinkedIn's company-to-company match for enumerated lists); **air cover buys reach, not conversions** (an open-pipeline audience can't produce the ~50 events/7 days a conversion ad set needs to exit Meta's learning phase, so it runs on a reach objective and is scored on penetration/progression, not a CPL it was never big enough to compute); and a **self-rotating engagement exclusion** for frequency (engaged accounts graduate into an exclusion so prospecting budget keeps refreshing onto accounts that haven't responded). - **The Newsletter Curator stops optimizing send time and engagement tiers on a proxy's clock** (both dual-located copies of [`content/content-newsletter-curator.md`](content/content-newsletter-curator.md) — new **Critical Rule 9**, a **"Reading the Reader Off a Proxy: Send Time and Engagement Tiers"** section, five re-based decision points across the Critical Rules / Deliverables / A/B calendar, and two corrected Success Metrics). Two loops built on opens are re-based: **send day/time** onto the CTA-click-that-resolves-to-a-session and the conversion the newsletter already tracks (with the Rule 9 latency read run first to size the proxy population); and the **engagement tier + inactivity sunset** onto silence across *all* human signals rather than opens — the sunset error is the damaging one, since a proxy keeps opening a dead address forever so an opens-based "no opens in 60 days" never fires for exactly the subscribers it must catch. The fifth and last-but-one downstream consumer of the machine-contaminated engagement signal, and the maximal-contamination one: cold B2B outbound mails people with no prior relationship, at their work addresses, inside the security-mature enterprises whose scanners fetch and detonate on 100% of a send — the anchor's "the better the logo, the dirtier the open" made literal. The send-time read is singularly broken because a scanner prefetches within seconds of delivery, so a discovered "best open time" is mostly the SDR's own send clock echoed back. The sharpest fix is to the **Response Rate metric**, which was defined as "percentage of *opens* that receive a reply" — a machine-inflated denominator, *and* internally impossible (a 1-5% cold reply rate of opens, opens being 8-15% of sends, would fall below the 0.5-2% meeting rate; you cannot book more meetings than replies), so the targets were always per-delivered; the denominator is corrected to **delivered** with every figure left untouched. - **The Copywriter stops crowning a subject line on opens — with a significance rule that isn't one** (both dual-located copies of [`email/email-copywriter.md`](email/email-copywriter.md) — new **Critical Rule 9**, a **"Testing Subject Lines on a Signal Machines Fake"** section, three re-based decision points, and two metric caveats). The fourth downstream consumer of the machine-contaminated engagement signal (after the 2026-08-13 deliverability, automation-engineer, and newsletter-strategist ships), and the one the deliverability agent's Rule 9 explicitly handed the subject-line copy decision to. Two errors stacked here and both are now corrected: the agent decided subject-line A/B winners on **open rate** — the most machine-contaminated signal, and the one the subject line's whole job is to move, making the copywriter the most exposed of any consumer — *and* it twice declared a winner on a fixed **"≥10% difference"**, which is not a significance test at all (significance is a function of sample size and variance, so a fixed gap crowns noise on small sends and misses real effects on large ones; a test sized against a contaminated open rate is additionally under-powered, so "no winner" is the expected outcome even when a real one exists). Re-based to decide on the least-fakeable signal the send can power (clicks or downstream conversion; opens only as a directional read with the instrument named), with the whole experiment-trust question — peeking, sample-ratio mismatch, the ship/no-difference/extend verdict — routed to [`analytics/analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer.md`](analytics/analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer.md)'s *Trust the Split Before the Winner* and the contamination mechanism/tiers referenced from [`email/email-deliverability-specialist.md`](email/email-deliverability-specialist.md) Rule 9 — neither re-derived, no new external claims or figures. The distinct contribution is this agent's alone: it is the only consumer running a formal significance test, so correcting the fixed-percent-gap statistics is owned by no other agent. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical, 0 broken links. Item marked **PARTLY WORKED 2026-08-13**; the two send-time reads (`content-newsletter-curator`, `sales-outbound-strategist`) remain, one per run.
- **The Newsletter Strategist stops tiering — and billing sponsors on — a signal machines generate** (both dual-located copies of [`email/email-newsletter-growth-strategist.md`](email/email-newsletter-growth-strategist.md) — new **Critical Rule 9**, a **"The Engagement Tiers Rank Subscribers on a Signal Machines Also Generate"** section, the engagement-segmentation tier block rebuilt on the evidence tiers, three re-based sponsor-facing decision points, a corrected Rule 1, and two metric caveats). The third downstream consumer of the machine-contaminated engagement signal, following the 2026-08-13 deliverability ship (which owns the *list* consequence) and the same day's automation-engineer ship (the *routing* consequence), deferred then under one-change-per-run. This agent's blast radius is distinct in two ways. First, its "highly engaged: open rate 50%+" tier is where premium content and monetization attention go — and a mailbox a privacy proxy opens every send sits at the top of it forever, so tiering on opens pours investment into machines; the tiers are rebuilt on the four evidence tiers (**Confirmed human → Probable human → Unconfirmed → Silent**) defined and cited in `email-deliverability-specialist` Rule 9, with referrals and paid conversions — which this agent is unusually rich in — as top-tier confirmed-human signals, and removal re-keyed off *silence across every signal* rather than an unopened-count a proxy suppresses. Second, and owned by no other agent in the stack: it **quotes the number to a paying sponsor** — the pitch, the CPM-on-"1,000 readers" price, and the "how many opens" report all bill a third party against a proxy-inflated figure, so those are re-based to quote verified reach (clicks, UTM conversions) and to name the instrument rather than present a raw open rate as delivered human attention. Consuming agent: references the deliverability agent's tiers rather than re-deriving them, hands the subject-line under-powering question to `email-copywriter`, and keeps the scoring math with `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`. **No new external claims or figures asserted** — every mechanism and citation lives in the deliverability agent's Rule 9. Ideas-only, written from scratch; lint 2/2, copies diff-identical, 0 broken links.
- **The Automation Engineer stops letting a machine trip the MQL threshold** (both dual-located copies of [`email/email-automation-engineer.md`](email/email-automation-engineer.md) — new **Critical Rule 9**, an **"An Engagement Event Is an Input; a Score Is an Action"** section, a new **Scoring Signal Integrity** success metric, a caveat on the open-rate metric, and five re-based decision points in the scoring/trigger/nurture deliverables). The sibling of the 2026-08-13 deliverability ship, deliberately deferred then under one-change-per-run: that agent owns the *list* consequence of the machine-contaminated engagement signal (a dead address lingering); this agent owns the consequence Rule 9 there explicitly handed it — the *routing* one, where an open or a scanner-fired click doesn't just mismeasure but **fires machinery**: it adds a scoring point, flips a nurture branch, trips the MQL threshold, and puts a rep on the phone with someone whose only interaction was their employer's security appliance, then teaches the model its highest-scoring signal converts poorly. The agent scored `email open = 1 point, click = 3 points`, branched "opened 5 emails in 7 days → higher engagement path" and "opened 4+ of first 6 emails → deeper nurture," and keyed re-engagement off "4 weeks with no opens" — a leg broken the *other* way, since privacy proxies keep opens firing for long-dead contacts so an opens-only sunset almost never triggers. Re-bases each decision on the four evidence tiers (confirmed-human / probable-human / unconfirmed / silent) **defined and cited in the deliverability agent** — opens score zero, a bare click waits for a resolved first-party session, Confirmed-human events (reply, form, product login, demo/trial) carry the weight, and the MQL line is crossable only by Probable-human-or-above. Consuming agent: it references those tiers rather than re-deriving them, keeps the scoring-model math with `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`, and routes the single-provider open-collapse anomaly back to `email-deliverability-specialist`. **No new external claims or figures asserted** — every mechanism and citation lives in the deliverability agent's Rule 9. Ideas-only, written from scratch; lint 2/2, copies diff-identical, 0 broken links.
- **The Deliverability Specialist stops trusting the engagement signal it makes every list decision on** (new **Critical Rule 9** + a **"The Engagement Signal Is Machine-Contaminated: Reading Opens and Clicks Before Acting"** section + an **Engagement Signal Integrity Audit** deliverable + three rewritten/added success metrics, in both dual-located copies of [`email/email-deliverability-specialist.md`](email/email-deliverability-specialist.md)). `Mail Privacy Protection`, `MPP`, and `tracking pixel` returned **zero hits repo-wide**, yet the agent — 244 lines, never enhanced since the first release — selected IP-warming cohorts from "email openers from past 7 days," identified inactive contacts by "no opens/clicks in 180-365 days," defined its engagement metric as `(opens + clicks) / delivered`, and diagnosed a low engagement rate as a content problem. Two machine populations sit inside those numbers and push in opposite directions: privacy proxies fetch tracking pixels with no human involved, and — the **B2B inversion the consumer-shaped market advice misses** — corporate security gateways fetch and detonate links *before delivery*, so the usual "fall back to clicks" remedy is the more contaminated signal on exactly the enterprise accounts a B2B SaaS company most wants. Ships a four-tier evidence model (confirmed-human / probable-human / unconfirmed / silent) with the minimum tier each list decision may use, three own-data contamination measurements (client split, delivery-to-open latency, link-fanout shape), the instrument-declaration discipline (a bot-filtering toggle and a placement incident look identical in the chart), the warming-cohort and unsubscribe-`GET` consequences, and an explicit list of what is *still* clean (complaints, bounces, seed-list placement). Ideas-only, written from scratch; every mechanism quoted from and cited to a primary vendor or standards source read 2026-08-13 ([Apple](https://www.apple.com/legal/privacy/data/en/mail-privacy-protection/), [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/defender-office-365/safe-links-about), [HubSpot](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/marketing-email/understand-bot-filtering-in-marketing-email-analytics), [Klaviyo](https://help.klaviyo.com/hc/en-us/articles/22981852783899), [RFC 8058](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8058.html)). **No prevalence, inflation, or scanner-share figure asserted.** Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; 341 internal links, 0 broken.
- **AEO/GEO freshness pass — the playbook learns Bing now ships a native competitive share-of-voice metric** ([`guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md`](guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md) §1 Copilot/Bing bullet + checklist #15 + Sources + `Last reviewed` → 2026-08-13; the same fact added to the "Engine-specific reality (2026)" Bing/Copilot bullet in both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md)). Microsoft expanded the Bing Webmaster Tools **AI Performance report on 2026-06-16 (preview)** with four views the playbook had missed — it described only the four original Feb-2026 metrics and cited only the Feb preview. The material one is **Citation Share**: *the percentage of citations attributed to your site out of all citations shown across all sites for the same grounding query* — a **native competitive share-of-voice number**, not an absolute count, which is exactly what the agent's own "citation share vs. 3–5 competitors" success goal and its AI-Share-of-Voice heatmap (Copilot column) had you computing by hand. The other three (**Intents** — grounding-query classification; **Topics** — thematic clustering; **Compare** — period-over-period overlay) are noted with Citation Share flagged as the one to watch for B2B. Verified against the **primary source** ([blogs.bing.com, "New AI Visibility Insights in Bing Webmaster Tools," June 16 2026](https://blogs.bing.com/search/June-2026/New-AI-Visibility-Insights-in-Bing-Webmaster-Tools-Intents-Topics-Citation-Share-Compare), read 2026-08-13), with the Citation Share definition quoted verbatim; all four flagged as **preview**. No effect sizes or GEO figures touched (none changed). Lint 2/2, agent copies diff-identical.
- **The Customer Advocacy Manager learns that a customer's yes is scoped and it expires** (both dual-located copies of [`product-marketing/pmm-customer-advocacy.md`](product-marketing/pmm-customer-advocacy.md) — new **Critical Rule 10**, a **"Rights Are Scoped and They Expire: The Advocacy Consent Record"** section, an **Advocacy Rights & Consent Record** deliverable, and a **Rights Coverage and Freshness** success metric). The sibling of the 2026-08-12 solicitation ship, deliberately deferred then under one-change-per-run: that section governs *how often* you ask, this governs *what a customer agreed to and for how long*. Gap grep-verified — `logo rights`, `quote approval`, `approval to publish`, and `expiring consent` returned **zero** hits outside bookkeeping; `usage rights` existed only on `social-influencer-partnerships` (a different object — influencer content licensing), and the single adjacent line is `content-case-study-producer`'s per-asset "permission to publish," which covers one asset at one moment. The section makes advocacy consent a first-class, scoped, expiring record: captured **per element × per surface** (name, title, logo, quoted sentence, quantified metric, headshot, filmed testimonial × website / sales deck / paid social / out-of-home / press / analyst / investor materials), because "you can use our story" is not "you can put our CFO's face in a paid ad." Four disciplines are ours: an **approved metric is a dated claim that expires** (a "cut onboarding 60%" approved one year is not consent to keep asserting it three years and two reorgs later — every metric carries a re-verification date, then re-verify or retire); **a repositioning re-frames the story they approved → re-consent, don't re-purpose** (approval attaches to the frame, so moving an approved quote under a new headline is a new ask routed through Rule 9's relationship owner, not an internal edit); the **stored named-versus-anonymized decision** (regulated/competitor/stealth customers whose legal team will never approve attribution are anonymized references, not lost ones — the record states which identifying elements are withheld and stops a later requester from "upgrading" a masked story); and the **three-records/three-owners seam** — this agent owns the standing rights record (scope/surface/term/re-consent), `content-case-study-producer` owns per-asset publication approval (the point-in-time gate this ledger aggregates), and `ops-legal-compliance` owns the contact-level marketing-consent record (may we contact this person — a different object from publication rights), with the record living in the CRM/advocacy platform, not in `templates/brand-context.md` and not as a new repo artifact. **No legal provision is asserted** — every grant, dispute, or jurisdictional question defers to `ops-legal-compliance`. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the "consent is legal, not optional" thesis surfaced 2026-08-12 by [lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth](https://github.com/lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth) (MIT, verified live — states the principle, does not define the mechanics) and the legal/logo approval gate in [LeadMagic/gtm-skills](https://github.com/LeadMagic/gtm-skills) (MIT, verified live); the per-element×per-surface scoping, expiring-metric review date, re-consent-on-reframe, stored anonymization decision, and the seam are ours, and are not in the sources. No burnout, decline, or rights-dispute figure asserted. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical. Closes the sibling backlog item to the 2026-08-12 solicitation ship.
- **The Customer Advocacy Manager learns the one review-solicitation practice most likely to break the law by accident** (both dual-located copies of [`product-marketing/pmm-customer-advocacy.md`](product-marketing/pmm-customer-advocacy.md) — an upgraded **Rule 7**, a new **"Soliciting Reviews Without Gating the Ask"** section, a checked **G2/Capterra Review Strategy** deliverable, and a **Solicitation Integrity** success metric). Rule 7 said "comply with FTC review guidelines" and stopped — the repo's signature *control named, instrument absent* pattern; the 2026-08-12 capacity ship added the *compensation* half of compliance (Rule 3, the G2 $100 paragraph) but left the *solicitation* half unowned. Gap grep-verified: `16 CFR`, `Part 465`, `fake review`, `review gating`, `review suppression`, `Endorsement Guide`, and `selective solicitation` returned **zero** hits across the agent tree. The section names the market's default practice — **review gating**, screening the public-review ask behind an NPS/CSAT score so only "promoters" are asked — and takes the honest posture the FTC's own record supports: the rule does **not** ban it, but the FTC states on the record it "could violate the FTC Act," so it is treated as a **named exposure routed to `ops-legal-compliance`, not a settled ban**. Three disciplines: **timing vs. screening** (timing the ask to a lifecycle high point is Rule 4 and is fine; screening *who* by predicted rating is the exposure); the **incentive bright line** (§465.4 — an incentive "conditioned expressly or by implication on" a review "expressing a particular sentiment" is a plain violation by the rule's own text, no balancing required); and **no rating-based suppression** (§465.7(b) — a display that reads as representative can't drop the low-rated entries, with the equally-applied carve-out for spam/fake/off-topic/PII/defamatory; §465.7(a) bars threats and knowingly-false accusations to pull a review; contacting an unhappy reviewer to fix the problem is permitted, paying them to delete it is not). Seam mirrors the certify-vs-verdict boundary — the advocacy program owns *how you ask*, `ops-legal-compliance` owns the legal determination. **Every provision verified against primary sources on 2026-08-12** — §465.4/§465.7 regulatory text from the [official CFR](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CFR-2025-title16-vol1/pdf/CFR-2025-title16-vol1-sec465-6.pdf), and the selective-solicitation / incentive-takedown / **October 21, 2024** effective-date statements from the [FTC business-guidance Q&A](https://www.ftc.gov/business-guidance/resources/consumer-reviews-testimonials-rule-questions-answers). The gating-is-unlawful reading is **flagged as unsettled, not asserted**; the incentive and suppression provisions are quoted as the bright lines they are. Ideas-only; surfaced by the review-solicitation discipline in `the-nam-shub/e5-real-skills` (**no license asserted — no content reused**); the timing-vs-screening framing, the bright-line-vs-gray-area split, and the seam are ours. No enforcement or penalty figure asserted. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **The Customer Advocacy Manager learns that its roster is not its capacity** (both dual-located copies of [`product-marketing/pmm-customer-advocacy.md`](product-marketing/pmm-customer-advocacy.md) — new **Critical Rule 9**, a **"Budgeting the Ask: Advocate Capacity and Depletion"** section, an **Advocate Capacity Plan & Ask Ledger** deliverable, four rewritten/added success metrics, and a corrected Rule 3). A reference program does not run out of customers; it runs out of the four people who keep saying yes — and this agent (**71 lines, never enhanced since the first pass, the smallest PMM agent**) modeled advocacy as recruitment and inventory from end to end. Gap grep-verified: `advocate fatigue`, `over-request`, `request cap`, `cap requests`, `depletable`, `reference fatigue`, and `shared resource` returned **zero** hits repo-wide; `burnout` resolved only to *internal team capacity* in the project-management agents; `customer success manager` appeared nowhere. **The finding that made it worth the run is in our own metrics:** "40-60 reference calls per quarter" against "5-10 actively available" advocates is **4-12 asks per person per quarter**, several times any published practitioner cap — the agent stated the roster-consuming quota as a *goal*, and it is now named in-file as the worked example of the failure. Ships the **capacity arithmetic** (capacity is the sum of individuals' weighted ask budgets, computed before a volume target is published); the **per-person rather than per-account ledger** (the account is referenceable, a named human takes the call, and an account-level roster structurally hides that three requesters landed on one inbox); **ask weighting** (a logo permission, a 30-minute call, a filmed case study and a keynote are different costs, so a cap counted in *asks* invites substituting heavy for light — and the real time cost belongs inside the ask); the **champion-job-change over-count** (the advocate is a person, so when they leave the account stays a customer and the roster keeps counting a reference that walked, with the seam to `sales-outbound-strategist`'s job-change triggers drawn so the same event is not double-used); **route-through-the-relationship-owner with a recorded veto** (Rule 4 times recruitment on a program-side calendar; this is relationship-side veto on live account state); **recorded states rather than silences** — available / unavailable-with-return-date / suppressed-with-reason / retired-with-cause, because an advocate quietly dropped still inflates the roster, the repo's standing **unknown never rounds to available** applied to a person; the **latency tell** (depletion presents as slower replies and rescheduling, not refusal); the **recipient's-policy framing of compensation** (enterprise, public-sector, financial-services and healthcare gift rules make a cash-equivalent card a problem *for them* they are rarely free to explain, so the offer is a menu built to route around the individual-gift question); and the **two-direction read** (ask concentration as fragility, recruited-and-never-asked as its opposite, both off one ledger). **Rule 3 corrected in passing:** it prescribed a flat cash schedule one rule away from Rule 7's "never incentivize reviews," with no line between compensating a *reference call* and compensating a *public review*. Ideas-only, written from scratch; thesis surfaced independently by three sources read 2026-08-12 — [lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth](https://github.com/lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth) (MIT, "advocates are not a renewable resource"), [LeadMagic/gtm-skills](https://github.com/LeadMagic/gtm-skills) (MIT, over-used references as a named pitfall plus last-ask-date and calls-YTD as CRM state), and [the-nam-shub/e5-real-skills](https://github.com/the-nam-shub/e5-real-skills) (**unlicensed — ideas only, no content reused**, staggered platform asks). **The sources' per-quarter cap numbers were deliberately not adopted** — practitioner defaults with no measurement behind them; the file instructs setting and recording the cap instead. G2's incentive policy quoted and cited to [G2's Community Guidelines](https://legal.g2.com/community-guidelines) (read 2026-08-12); the gift-policy reasoning labeled in-file as our judgment, not a measured finding. No burnout rate, decline rate, or advocacy-attributed revenue figure asserted. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **The PPC Strategist learns that an automated bid strategy is only as good as the signal feeding it** (both dual-located copies of [`paid-media/paid-media-ppc-strategist.md`](paid-media/paid-media-ppc-strategist.md) — new **Critical Rule 10**, a **"Signal before strategy: what your conversion volume lets you bid"** subsection under *Operating a Live Account*, an extended **Bidding Strategy Framework** deliverable, and a signal-fit-bidding success metric). Sits *upstream* of today's learning-phase ship: that section governs reading a live automated strategy; this one governs *choosing* it and setting its starting target before launch. Three grep-verified gaps, all distinct from the learning-phase material — the agent mentioned conversion volume only for learning-phase *exit* (line 81), never as the **pre-launch floor that decides which strategy you can even pick**; it derived tCPA from LTV (the affordable *ceiling*) but never anchored the **starting** target to what the account has actually *achieved*; and "portfolio bid strategy" existed in the repo only as the budget optimizer's budget-pooling *hazard*, never as the *bidding* mechanic of pooling thin campaigns' signal. Ships the reconciliation that keeps the agent's LTV identity intact — **LTV is the ceiling, trailing-achieved performance is the starting point; you start at achievable and step toward the ceiling after each learning exit** — plus the **B2B inversion** (a demo-request campaign can take a quarter to clear a floor a consumer campaign clears in a day, so the floor is the binding constraint, not a checkbox), a **pool-the-signal-not-the-budget** seam with the budget optimizer (a portfolio bid strategy and a shared budget are different objects), and a handoff to the higher-funnel-event move the learning-phase section already owns. Google figures quoted from primary docs read 2026-08-11 — [About Target CPA bidding](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/6268632) ("at least 30 conversions" recommended for evaluation; a target set "too low ... may cause you to forgo clicks ... resulting in fewer total conversions") and Google's [Target ROAS requirements](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/6268637) ("at least 15 conversions in the past 30 days" on Search and Shopping); the 30-conversion figure flagged as an evaluation recommendation, not a hard gate, and all volume thresholds treated as directional. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the strategy-by-volume shape, achievable-not-aspirational start, and pool-thin-campaigns move surfaced by [aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills](https://github.com/aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills) (Apache-2.0), which labels its own thresholds rules of thumb — no text adapted. The B2B inversion, the LTV-ceiling-vs-achievable-start reconciliation, and the portfolio-signal-vs-shared-budget seam are ours. No CPA, ROAS, or conversion-volume figure of our own asserted. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical. Closes backlog item 120(b).
- **The PPC Strategist learns that its own fix is what makes the number unreadable** (both dual-located copies of [`paid-media/paid-media-ppc-strategist.md`](paid-media/paid-media-ppc-strategist.md) — new **Critical Rule 9**, a **"The learning phase: when the number isn't real yet"** subsection under *Operating a Live Account*, and a learning-state success metric). Today's budget-optimizer pacing ship left a **"Learning reset"** caveat and the creative strategist has long carried a **"learning period"** caveat, but **no agent owned the state itself** — grep-verified `learning limited`, `learning status`, and `conversion cycle` returned **zero** hits repo-wide. The PPC Strategist is the owner because it holds the spend-change gate, the authorization mechanism for the exact edits (bid-strategy switches, target/budget changes) that re-enter learning, and its *one variable, one verification window* rule already needs a reason its clock cannot start at the moment of the change. Ships the platform mechanics cited to primary docs (Google's three "Learning" triggers and "up to 3 weeks or 1-2 conversion cycles"; Meta's ~50-optimization-events-per-week exit and its significant-edit list) and **three disciplines that are ours**: **the recursion** — the move that closes a pacing or CAC gap is itself a significant edit, so you cannot repair the number and read the repair in the same window, and that measurement blackout is part of the cost of the change; **the doom loop** — reacting to a bad in-learning read with another edit resets learning again, so no material edit and no verdict until the window closes; and the **B2B conversion-volume inversion** — Meta's ~50-events/week exit is a consumer-volume criterion a B2B ad set optimized on demo requests may never reach ("Learning limited" indefinitely), so set the verification window to the real conversion cycle and, where the primary event is too rare, optimize on a higher-funnel event and validate down-funnel separately. Seams: authorization stays with the gate; the budget optimizer's pacing read and the creative strategist's test read both defer here for the definition of the state. Ideas-only, written from scratch; discipline (learning phase as a first-class state that gates action) surfaced by [aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills](https://github.com/aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills) (Apache-2.0), no text adapted. Google facts quoted from two primary docs read 2026-08-11; Meta figures taken from Meta Help Center text (its pages render as JavaScript to an automated fetch). No spend, CAC, or learning-duration figure of our own asserted. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **The Budget Optimizer learns that a plan is not a spend** (both dual-located copies of [`paid-media/paid-media-budget-optimizer.md`](paid-media/paid-media-budget-optimizer.md) — new **Critical Rule 9**, a **"Planned Is Not Delivered: Reading Pacing Before the Curve"** section, a **Budget Delivery & Pacing Report** deliverable, and three rewritten success metrics).
