The agent's entire model — Rule 2's empirical spend-vs-return curves, its saturation thresholds, Rule 3's scenarios — is fit on *delivered* spend, and it had no way to ask whether the plan it modeled was ever executed: grep-verified `underdeliver`, `over-deliver`, `budget cap`, `shared budget`, `burn rate`, `flighting`, `learning phase`, and `spend delivery` returned **zero** hits repo-wide, and `pacing` in the paid sense existed only as a frequency-capping line in `paid-media-programmatic-buyer` plus two blurb bullets in `paid-media-ops/SKILL.md` — the discipline named in a metrics list and owned by no agent. The organizing idea is ours: **saturation and delivery constraint are the same picture in a spend-vs-return chart** — money a channel cannot absorb produces no extra return either — so a saturation threshold observed at a spend level the channel never exceeded is *the top of the observed range*, and under-delivery rounds to unknown-above-here, never to saturated. Ships the **B2B inversion** (the binding constraint is usually the audience, not the budget, so a channel at 70% of plan has a reach ceiling and needs reallocation, not a bid increase); the **average-not-a-cap** table (Google 2×/30.4×, LinkedIn +50%, Meta +25%) with *a single heavy day is not an incident* and *a ceiling is not a plan*; **fix the target curve before reading the number** (even/front/back-loaded, percent-to-pace, projected landing over completed days only, low confidence under ~5 elapsed days, every figure badged delivered/stated/projected); a **six-driver table** where the remedies do not overlap and *unknown never rounds to saturated*; **underspend as an unplanned reallocation** (expired / pooled by a shared budget to the broadest campaign that can absorb it / period-end catch-up buying the worst inventory at its worst price); and **fixing pacing without destroying the measurement** (step it, mark the discontinuity), with authorization deferred to `paid-media-ppc-strategist`'s spend-change gate, flight-level DSP pacing to `paid-media-programmatic-buyer`, and the incrementality question to `paid-media-attribution-analyst`. Ideas-only, written from scratch; discipline surfaced by [aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills](https://github.com/aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills) (Apache-2.0), [logly/mureo](https://github.com/logly/mureo) (Apache-2.0), and [scumunna/programmatic-skills](https://github.com/scumunna/programmatic-skills) (MIT). All platform behavior quoted from Google Ads, LinkedIn, and Meta primary docs read 2026-08-11. Two fabricated-precision metrics removed in passing (">85% scenario forecasting accuracy", "<10% variance vs. modeled results"). Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **The Technical SEO Auditor learns to diagnose a sitewide traffic drop before auditing it** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-technical-auditor.md`](seo/seo-technical-auditor.md) — new **Critical Rule 11**, a **"The Whole Site Dropped: Name the Class Before You Audit"** section, a **Sitewide Traffic-Drop Diagnosis** deliverable, and a **sitewide-drop class-before-fix** metric). When organic traffic falls across a whole site, the two most SEO-native causes — a Google **algorithm/core update** and a **manual action or security issue** — were owned by **nobody**: grep-verified `manual action`, `core update`, and `algorithm update` returned **zero** hits repo-wide, `seo-content-optimizer` explicitly routes site-level causes *away* ("if the whole property fell by roughly the same amount, the page is not the story"), and `analytics-performance-analyst` owns the *method* but as a marketing generalist that never names an algorithm update or a manual action. The enhancement resolves the rampstackco `seo-traffic-diagnosis` item honestly: its five-layer **method** (confirm-real → localize → page → technical → external) is a **non-gap** — deferred to `analytics-performance-analyst` and `seo-content-optimizer`, not rebuilt — while the **SEO-native cause taxonomy** it runs over is the real gap. Ships a **localization table** where the segment names the owner (the branded-vs-non-branded cut that separates a brand/outage/manual-action event from an SEO ranking problem); the **two report-read causes** — the Manual Actions and Security Issues reports checked *first* because they are the one cause with a binary answer, remedy = reconsideration request not a content refresh; and the algorithm-update read held honest by *timing-is-a-hypothesis* (rule out a same-window deploy first) and *there-is-no-switch-to-flip* (the content owners' slow work, recovery on the next update); a **deploy-is-the-prime-suspect** read for a sudden cliff; a fixed **certainty-and-cost ordering** that puts the over-called external causes last; and a **two-classes-both-fit** guardrail. Ideas-only, written from scratch; frame surfaced by the `seo-traffic-diagnosis` skill in [rampstackco/claude-skills](https://github.com/rampstackco/claude-skills) (MIT). Manual-action, security-issue, and core-update behavior cited to Google primary docs read 2026-08-11 (Manual Actions report; Security Issues report; Google Search's core updates; the dated Search Status Dashboard). No traffic-loss, recovery-time, or ranking figure asserted. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **The Technical SEO Auditor learns to run a site migration — the highest-risk technical SEO event** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-technical-auditor.md`](seo/seo-technical-auditor.md) — new **Critical Rule 10**, an **"A Migration Moves Every Signal at Once"** section, a **Site Migration Runbook** deliverable, and a migration-signal-retention metric). `analytics-performance-analyst` already **routes** "site migration, redirect, canonical, or template change" *to* this agent, and `seo-content-optimizer` / `seo-ai-search-optimizer` name migrations as events they route away — yet the auditor had **zero** migration content (grep-verified: `migration`/`redirect map`/`replatform` appeared only incidentally, never as a capability), so the inbound handoff dead-ended. Ships the discipline a URL-changing move demands: a **pre-cutover baseline** (crawl, top landing pages/queries, sitemap, robots, schema) as the only line between an expected reprocessing dip and a real regression; the **one-to-one 301 redirect map** with orphans as the quality measure; the **one-hop rule** (Google follows up to 10 hops — a survival limit, not a design budget); **move-all-at-once + release the staging `noindex`/robots block** (the §3 belt-and-braces trap at its most expensive); a **don't-fold-a-rewrite-into-the-move** rule so a traffic move stays attributable; the **weeks-to-reprocess / redirects-stay-a-year** windows from Google's own numbers; and a **move-type blast-radius table**. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the checklist scaffold surfaced by [rampstackco/claude-skills](https://github.com/rampstackco/claude-skills) (MIT), whose uncited "30–70% traffic loss" figure was **not** adopted — no migration loss/recovery percentage is asserted. Every redirect behavior, timeframe, and procedure cited to Google primary docs read 2026-08-10 (Redirects and Google Search; Site moves with URL changes; HTTP status codes and network errors). Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **The Technical SEO Auditor learns that indexing is a decision, not a delivery** (both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-technical-auditor.md`](seo/seo-technical-auditor.md) — new **Critical Rule 9**, an **"Indexed Is a Decision, Not a Delivery"** section, an **Index Disposition Audit** deliverable, and a rewritten indexation metric). The agent's every lever governed crawl *eligibility*; it had no branch for the state Google documents as `Crawled - currently not indexed` — fetched cleanly and declined — against which robots, sitemap, canonical, and crawl-budget fixes are all no-ops. Gap grep-verified: `not indexed`, `Page indexing`, `URL Inspection`, `X-Robots-Tag`, `nofollow`, `410`, and `Removals` were **zero** repo-wide, while a deliverable promised "soft 404 diagnosis" and a metric promised "95%+ of target pages indexed" with no instrument for either. Ships a **four-disposition model** (intended exclusion / broken signal / deferred crawl / declined) so an audit stops reporting one defect total and driving healthy exclusions toward zero; a **route-don't-fix** handoff of the declined bucket to `seo-content-optimizer` and `content-blog-strategist` (the return leg of a handoff that previously only ran one way); the **robots.txt-cancels-noindex trap** — stacking a `Disallow` on a `noindex` removes the only directive that was working — plus `X-Robots-Tag` for the gated PDFs that have no `<head>`; an **intended-exclusion map** declared before the audit; a removal-mechanism table; and **unknown never rounds to indexed**. Ideas-only, written from scratch; surfaced by [kostja94/marketing-skills](https://github.com/kostja94/marketing-skills) (MIT), whose 404-vs-410 distinction was **not** adopted because Google's own docs say all `4xx` except `429` are treated the same. All states, directives, and status-code behavior cited to Google primary documentation read 2026-08-10.
- **Core Web Vitals corrected repo-wide: FID → INP.** `INP` returned **zero** hits repo-wide while `FID`/`First Input Delay` appeared in five places — the repo taught a Core Web Vital Google **retired on 2024-09-09** (INP replaced it 2024-03-12) and prescribed a `<100ms` threshold nobody measures. Fixed in [`seo/seo-technical-auditor.md`](seo/seo-technical-auditor.md) (both copies, with a dated note), [`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/seo-growth/SKILL.md`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/seo-growth/SKILL.md), and [`strategy/playbooks/phase-0-discovery.md`](strategy/playbooks/phase-0-discovery.md) + its twin. In the same pass, Rule 6's **Mobile-Friendly Test** — sunset by Google on 2023-12-01 with its API and the Mobile Usability report — became the URL Inspection tool's rendered-HTML view, and "GSC Index Coverage report" became its current name, the **Page indexing** report.
- **AEO/GEO freshness pass — the playbook learns how Google now *shows* a citation** ([`guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md`](guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md) §1 + Sources + `Last reviewed` → 2026-08-09; the same engine-behavior fact appended to the "Engine-specific reality (2026)" Google bullet in both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md)). Google shipped attribution changes to AI Overviews / AI Mode on **2026-05-06** that the 2026-07-28 review missed. Two are directly material and were absent from the playbook: (1) **inline citations now sit right next to the specific sentence they support** — so a clean, self-contained, extractable passage is what earns the visible link (reinforces §3); and (2) **when the cited source is a discussion or social post, the link now shows the creator's name/handle and the community name, not just the domain** — which gives the existing **Reddit ≈ 6× G2** signal (§2/§4/checklist #12) a concrete mechanism: the account you post under and the subreddit you post in are surfaced to the buyer, so authentic participation under a real identity beats an anonymous brand drop. The three weaker UX features in the same announcement (hover previews, next-step suggestions, subscription highlighting) were deliberately **not** added — low actionability for a citation playbook. Cited to the primary source ([blog.google, "How AI Mode and AI Overviews help you explore the web"](https://blog.google/products-and-platforms/products/search/explore-web-generative-ai-search/), read 2026-08-09), verified against Search Engine Land; no GEO effect size touched (the "+27.8/25.9/24.9%" figures circulating are the KDD 2024 paper's *average* metric, not a new study — the playbook already cites that paper's headline numbers). Lint 2/2, agent copies diff-identical.

### Added
- **New discipline: Account-Based Marketing — the target account list finally has an owner** (new [`abm/abm-account-based-strategist.md`](abm/abm-account-based-strategist.md), dual-located in the new [`abm-ops`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/abm-ops/SKILL.md) skill; roster **69 → 70 agents**, **18 → 19 skills**, **16 → 17 disciplines**). The repo already shipped a [Monthly ABM loop](loops/monthly-abm-loop.md) and a [worked ABM campaign workflow](examples/workflow-abm-campaign.md), and `ops-legal-compliance` already named "the ABM program" as a channel that must query the suppression record — but **no agent owned the program itself**. Grep-verified before writing: `target account list`, `account tier`, `buyer enablement` and `mutual action plan` returned **zero**; `ABM` and `account-based` resolved only to *consumers* of a list somebody else was assumed to have produced — `paid-media-social-ads-specialist` (ABM ad targeting), `sales-outbound-strategist` (account-based sequences), `events-field-marketing-strategist` (per-event target accounts) — and the ABM loop routed account scoring to `pmm-competitive-intelligence`, a competitor-research agent, because there was nobody else. The new agent owns what all of them consume: the list and its selection evidence, the **capacity-and-coverage model** that sizes it (accounts a paired seller can genuinely work × sellers paired × per-tier touch density — cut the list, never thin the touches), the **1:1 / 1:few / 1:many** tier model with per-tier service levels, the **signals-to-actions matrix** (two corroborating signal families and a declared freshness window before any high-effort play), the per-tier **orchestration contract**, the **sales-pairing agreement** (no named seller, no program — that is the go/no-go answer, not a scheduling problem), and an **account scoreboard that is never denominated in MQLs** — coverage, penetration, pipeline from the list, and win-rate/cycle-length deltas against a *published* comparison set, under a credit rule where ABM takes assist credit rather than a second copy of pipeline another channel already claimed. Two things are ours against every surveyed source: **the account list is also a media asset with a documented floor** — LinkedIn requires ≥300 rows and ≥300 matched member accounts for a company list to run in an active ad set ([LinkedIn Marketing Solutions Help](https://www.linkedin.com/help/lms/answer/a423102), read 2026-08-17), so a 15-account Tier 1 tier cannot be a standalone paid audience and a touch plan assuming air cover across every tier has a silent hole in it; and an explicit **boundary rule** naming the eight agents that own everything downstream, so the program publishes the account set and the contract and never executes the touches. Ideas-only, written from scratch, credited in-file to [gtmagents/gtm-agents](https://github.com/gtmagents/gtm-agents) (Apache-2.0), [LeadMagic/gtm-skills](https://github.com/LeadMagic/gtm-skills), [lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth](https://github.com/lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth) and [modest-curator478/claude-skills](https://github.com/modest-curator478/claude-skills) (MIT) — four independent sources shipping ABM as a first-class role, which is why the gap was treated as real. The 1:1/1:few/1:many taxonomy is attributed to ITSMA / [Momentum ITSMA](https://momentumabm.com/the-pioneers-of-account-based-marketing) (read 2026-08-17). **No coverage, penetration, win-rate, or spend-per-account benchmark is asserted.** Lint 70/70, copies diff-identical, 0 broken internal links, both manifests parse; counts updated in the README (incl. badges), `AGENTS_INDEX.md`, `llms.txt`, `CITATION.cff`, both manifests, the suite router, the CATALYST orchestrator and framework docs, `integrations/`, `loops/`, `strategy/` and `CONTRIBUTING.md`, and the live GitHub About description.
- **Content Blog Strategist learns that its volume target is a ceiling, not a quota** ([`content/content-blog-strategist.md`](content/content-blog-strategist.md), both dual-located copies) — the agent's flagship Rule 7 prescribes "12 pillar posts + 240 cluster posts over 18 months" and frames velocity as an unconditional good ("strategic consistency compounds"), with **no quality floor** — the exact input Google's **scaled content abuse** policy was written to catch, and this agent (82 lines, **never enhanced** until now) had no notion of it. Gap grep-verified: `scaled content`, `content abuse`, `thin content`, `quality dilution`, and `helpful content` returned **zero** hits across any *content* agent, and the scaled-content-abuse policy lived in the repo in exactly **one place** — a single AEO-warning line in [`guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md`](guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md) — a guide the blog strategist never reads. The repo's signature "control asserted, instrument absent" pattern: a flagship rule commits to scaled production with no gate. Adds **Critical Rule 9**, a **"The Plan's Volume Is a Ceiling, Not a Quota"** section, a **Publish / Hold / Cut Gate** deliverable, and a **value-not-volume** success metric. The spine: the 240-post figure is a *capacity* estimate, not a quota — a cluster post built to occupy a keyword rather than answer a real question can't earn its ranking anyway, so cut it *before* it's written. The gate is a reader ("would be glad it exists even if it never ranked"), not a keyword. **Seam drawn** to `seo-content-optimizer`, which owns thin/cannibalizing pages *after* they exist (decay triage, consolidation, the sub-300-word flag) — this rule owns the decision *not to create the page in the first place*, the matched production-side counterpart. **This closes the (c) slice of the [rampstackco/claude-skills](https://github.com/rampstackco/claude-skills) watch item by resolving its two named ideas as non-gaps** (refresh/decay already owned by `seo-content-optimizer`; pillar architecture already this agent's own Rules 2/4/8) and shipping the grep-verified adjacent gap instead. Ideas-only, written from scratch; scaled-content-abuse and people-first wording quoted from and cited to Google primary docs read 2026-08-10 ([Spam policies](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/essentials/spam-policies), [Creating helpful content](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/fundamentals/creating-helpful-content)). The retired Helpful Content System's "site-wide drag" language is flagged as **no longer documented** — treated as a risk to design against, not a mechanism asserted. No ranking, suppression, or recovery figure asserted. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **Conversion Rate Optimizer learns that a significant test can still be lying** ([`analytics/analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer.md`](analytics/analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer.md), both dual-located copies) — the agent had the *power* half of experiment discipline (Rule 2: calculate sample size, reach 95%) and **none of the trust half** — the checks that decide whether to believe a result that is powered and significant. Gap grep-verified: `sample ratio mismatch`, `SRM`, `optional stopping`, `novelty effect`, and `primacy effect` returned **zero** hits repo-wide; `guardrail metric` existed only on the Twitter agent and `peeking` only on the paid strategist plus a one-line skill-wrapper mention. Worse, Rule 2's "reach 95% before declaring a winner" actively invites **peeking**. Adds **Critical Rule 9**, a **"Trust the Split Before the Winner: When a Significant Result Is Still an Artifact"** section, an **Experiment Trust Report** deliverable, a Rule 2 pointer, and a rewritten success metric. The spine: power asks *could I detect it*, trust asks *did the machinery produce the number I'm reading* — and the second is answered **before** you look at the winner. **Sample ratio mismatch** is the first gate (chi-squared goodness-of-fit on observed vs. intended allocation, strict `p < 0.001`); an SRM whose cause you can't find is a **discard, not an adjust**, because a randomizer you can't trust to split evenly you can't trust to have split *representatively*. Then **peeking** (a stopping rule fixed before the data, or a sequential design built to permit monitoring — not stopping the day the line first crosses 95%), **generalized guardrail metrics** (Rule 4's lead-quality guard extended to unsubscribe/complaint rate, page-load and error rate), and the **novelty/primacy** durability read (segment new vs. returning; a first-week lift among returning users is reaction-to-change that washes out). Resolves into a gated **ship / investigate / extend / stop / revert** verdict with *invalid, rerun* in front of it when the SRM gate fails. Ours: the B2B small-N inversion (the SRM check is itself underpowered at B2B volumes, so inspect the assignment mechanism directly), the discard-not-adjust rule, the guardrail generalization, and the seams with `paid-media-creative-strategist` (power for paid tests) and `analytics-performance-analyst` (observational anomalies). One success metric fixed in passing: the old "60-70% of tests show positive results" made *winning* a quota (the exact anti-pattern peeking exploits) — now the number to hold high is the share of completed tests that pass the trust gate and produce a defensible decision, with a 60-70% *win* rate flagged as itself a symptom of peeking or under-powering. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the checklist surfaced by the `ab-test-analysis` skill in [phuryn/pm-skills](https://github.com/phuryn/pm-skills) (MIT, 25k★). SRM as chi-squared goodness-of-fit and its cause taxonomy cited to Kohavi, Tang & Xu, *Trustworthy Online Controlled Experiments* (Cambridge, 2020) and Fabijan et al. (KDD 2019). No conversion-lift or error-rate figures asserted. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **Twitter/X Strategist learns the difference between a bad day and a pile-on** ([`social/social-twitter-strategist.md`](social/social-twitter-strategist.md), both dual-located copies) — the agent already *named* "crisis communication and negative feedback response protocols" as a deliverable, but supplied no method, and Rule 8 governs only *steady-state* moderation (respond to criticism, don't ratio-fight, block only abuse). Nothing told it what changes when a cluster of criticism accelerates into a coordinated public ratio. Adds **Critical Rule 10**, a new **"When Criticism Becomes a Pile-On: Managing a Public Ratio"** method section, an upgraded crisis deliverable, and a **Crisis Response Discipline** success metric. The moves: **triage first** (most "crises" are ordinary criticism, and over-escalating manufactures one); **classify the event type** because the response differs — own a real failure plainly, but *correct misinformation with evidence rather than apologizing for what didn't happen* (an apology ratifies a false premise); **acknowledge fast and centralize** in one pinned timestamped post; **never scrub legitimate criticism** (the Streisand effect turns a complaint into the story); **pause the scheduled queue** so an autopilot promo doesn't drop into the fire; and **the agent drafts, a human approves and posts**. Seam drawn to `social-community-builder`, which owns crisis comms in your *owned* spaces (Discord/Slack/forum) — this owns the *public-platform* ratio where you don't control the room. Ideas-only, adapted from the `crisis-and-moderation` skill in [social-media-skills/skills](https://github.com/social-media-skills/skills) (MIT); no figures asserted.
- **Product Launch Manager learns to read the room it is launching from** ([`product-marketing/pmm-launch-manager.md`](product-marketing/pmm-launch-manager.md), both dual-located copies) — the pre-mortem shipped 2026-08-08 routes every standard failure narrative to the agent that owns the test for it. **One narrative had nowhere to route: your own company.** A launch can be killed from inside as surely as by the market, and nothing in this repo modelled that — `sales-deal-strategist` maps the *customer's* buying committee (economic buyer, blocker, champion), `pm-campaign-coordinator`'s stakeholder map is a *communications* plan (role, frequency, escalation path), and `pm-client-success-manager`'s risk register covers *agency↔client* risk. None reads an internal stakeholder's **stance toward the launch itself**. Adds a new routing-table row, a passage giving the launch manager ownership of the internal read, an extension to the **Launch Pre-Mortem & Risk Register** deliverable, and an **Internal Alignment Read** success metric. The move it makes: **a readiness signature is what hides the silent doubter**, since a sign-off certifies the work is done and never that its owner wants the launch to win — so each decision-critical internal stakeholder gets a stance (supporter / neutral / blocker / silent doubter) reached not by re-collecting sign-offs but by asking, function by function, whether its owner would **fund it from their own budget, staff it from their own headcount, and defend it unprompted to their own boss**. A yes on the checklist and a no to those three is the veto, found while there is still room to move it. An all-supporters map is logged as a failed exercise, not as green, and the read is deliberately **not** reported as a percent-aligned figure. Internal-political-map framing adapted ideas-only from `stakeholder-maps` in [stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills](https://github.com/stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills) (MIT, verified live); the fund-staff-defend test, the stance taxonomy, the sign-off-versus-alignment distinction, and the agent seams are ours.
- **Product Launch Manager learns to run the retrospective it was told to run** ([`product-marketing/pmm-launch-manager.md`](product-marketing/pmm-launch-manager.md), both dual-located copies) — Rule 8 ended "conduct post-launch retrospective within 2 weeks" and supplied **no method**; the pre-mortem shipped the same day had already promised the risk register would be "re-opened at the post-launch retrospective," a promise nothing yet discharged. Gap grep-verified: `retrospective` appeared repo-wide only as a *scheduling* bullet across the project-management agents (`pm-marketing-ops-scrum-master` sprint retros, `pm-campaign-coordinator` post-campaign retros) and the phase playbooks — never as a technique on the launch owner. Adds an updated **Rule 8** (now points to the method and splits the horizon), an **"After It Ships: The Launch Retrospective"** section, a **Post-Launch Retrospective & Decision Log** deliverable, and a **Retrospective Decision Yield** success metric. The section keeps **two ledgers separate** — *what happened* (which metrics moved, which tripwires fired and whether the prepared response worked, which predicted risks materialized, and the real failures the register named nowhere) and *what we would decide differently* (each a written decision change with a named owner and a landing place) — and holds that a retrospective producing only the first is a **debrief, not a retrospective**. Ours: the **two-pass structure** (execution pass within 2 weeks, market-outcome pass deferred to the pre-mortem's one-sales-cycle horizon) that resolves the 2-week-vs-deal-clock tension; the **register-as-spine** discharge of Rule 9's re-open (blocking risks, expiring accepted trade-offs, the unnamed-failures column); the **judge-the-decision-not-the-outcome / survivorship / outcome-bias** discipline; and the explicit **seam** placing this as launch-specific *method* on the launch owner, layered on the PM agents that own retrospective *cadence*, not a second scheduler. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the two-ledger split and the debrief-vs-retrospective rule credited to the `retro` skill in [stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills](https://github.com/stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills) (MIT, verified live 2026-08-08). No launch-outcome, decision-yield, or calibration figure asserted; the new metric says not to set a target rate and not to manufacture a decision change to clear the gate. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **Product Launch Manager learns to assume the launch already failed** ([`product-marketing/pmm-launch-manager.md`](product-marketing/pmm-launch-manager.md), both dual-located copies) — Rules 2, 3, and 4 were a complete apparatus for *schedule and completion* risk (a 30% timeline buffer, a signed readiness checklist, weekly dependency reviews), and **none of them asked whether the launch would work**; the file's only postponement trigger fired on an unchecked box. Gap grep-verified: `pre-mortem`, `premortem`, `prospective hindsight`, `red team`, `falsif`, and `tripwire` returned **zero** hits repo-wide, `risk register` existed only as agency-delivery and compliance risk, and `postpone` appeared only in this agent's own Rule 3. Adds **Critical Rule 9**, an **"Assume It Already Failed: The Launch Pre-Mortem"** section, a **Launch Pre-Mortem & Risk Register** deliverable, and two success metrics — **the first enhancement this agent has ever received**. The section's spine is that every blocking risk needs a named person, an observable that would prove it real, and an answer to *when that observable resolves*: pre-launch-testable risks are unfinished tasks with a date, post-launch-only ones are tripwires whose response is written in advance, and a register that is entirely post-launch reads rigorous while changing no decision. Also ours: run it **before** readiness sign-off (after sign-off a blocker becomes a self-reversal) and never facilitated by whoever owns the date; a **one-sales-cycle** horizon rather than launch week, because B2B launch failures resolve on the deal clock; accepted trade-offs that record the condition they were accepted under, because acceptance expires; an instrument routing table pointing six standard failure narratives at the agents that already own the test; and the rule that zero blocking risks is a finding about the room, not a clean launch. Technique attributed to Gary Klein ([HBR, September 2007](https://hbr.org/2007/09/performing-a-project-premortem), read 2026-08-08); **the widely-repeated "~30% better" prospective-hindsight figure is deliberately not asserted** and is flagged in-file as contested against its own source. Ideas-only, written from scratch; structuring learned from [stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills](https://github.com/stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills) (MIT) and independently corroborated by [matteotitta/genesys-skills](https://github.com/matteotitta/genesys-skills) (MIT). One metric fixed in passing: the Launch Readiness Score is now labeled a completion measure, not a confidence signal.
- **Twitter/X Strategist learns to read a winner off organic social** ([`social/social-twitter-strategist.md`](social/social-twitter-strategist.md), both dual-located copies) — Rule 2 already told the agent to "test 3–5 thread variations monthly" and its deliverables promised "engagement data showing which variations perform best," but it supplied **no method**: no controls, no decision rule, no acknowledgement that organic social can't reach statistical significance, and Success Metrics that read winners straight off single-post performance. Gap grep-verified: `paired repetition`, `decision rule`, `holdout`, `underpowered`, and `minimum detectable` returned **zero** hits across `social/`. Adds **Critical Rule 9** ("A Winner Needs a Rule and a Repeat"), a **"Reading a Winner Off Organic Social: Testing Without Significance"** section, and a **Test Discipline (proven, not lucky)** success metric. The section draws the seam explicitly: `paid-media-creative-strategist` owns the *powered* paid test (a budget lever buys sample → compute an MDE, four-outcome verdict), while this is the method for the *structurally-unpowerable* organic case — small samples with the algorithm in the middle, so rigor comes from **one variable, a decision rule written before publishing, matched controls over 1–4 weeks, and 3–5 paired repetitions**, not a p-value; the two agree on what a winner is (neither promotes a single favourable read), and a format proven organically gets re-tested under the paid discipline if budget is later put behind it. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the "controls-and-repetition instead of p-values" spine adapted from the `experimentation-and-ab-testing` skill in [social-media-skills/skills](https://github.com/social-media-skills/skills) (MIT). The ~20% materiality threshold is flagged **directional** and self-calibrated to an account's own variance, not a benchmark; **no engagement or lift figure is asserted**; the organic-vs-powered seam and the calibrate-to-your-own-variance rule are ours. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **LinkedIn Organic Strategist learns to design a post's first hour** ([`social/social-linkedin-strategist.md`](social/social-linkedin-strategist.md), both dual-located copies) — the agent's **first enhancement** (85 lines, untouched since the first pass). Its whole thesis is "algorithm-native organic strategies" (Rule 1: Algorithm Compliance First; Rule 4: Engagement Reciprocity), yet it governed engagement only as an outbound *reciprocity budget* — commenting on **other people's** posts — and owned no distribution mechanics for **its own** posts in the window that decides their reach. Gap grep-verified: `Notify Employees`, `first-hour`, `golden hour`, `link in comment`, `dark post`, and `Thought Leader Ad` returned **zero** hits repo-wide (the two `first hour` hits are unrelated launch-checklist bullets in a strategy playbook). Adds **Critical Rule 9** ("Distribution Is Designed, Not Hoped"), a **"The First Hour: Designing a Post's Distribution"** section, and two paired success metrics. The section covers: the **early-engagement window** framed as a testable pattern on your own account rather than a law (line up substantive first-hour replies, publish on your analytics' timing, and *confirm* the window predicts reach for you before building rituals); the native **Notify Employees** tool as the single most legitimate first-hour lever a page owns, with its three governing constraints each written as a discipline — LinkedIn's documented **once-per-24-hours** cap (reserve it for notify-worthy posts), the **opt-out cost** (employees can opt out anytime, permanently shrinking the reachable pool and surfacing as declining Page analytics — over-notifying spends a non-renewable resource), and the **relevance-model / delivery-lag** caveat (not every employee is notified, and processing takes hours, so it is not a broadcast you control); **link placement** as a *contested* practitioner belief (LinkedIn has publicly stated it does not penalize outbound links) resolved by A/B testing on your own account rather than folklore; and a **paid line** handing the amplified versions — *Thought Leader Ads* and dark/unpublished posts — across the seam to `paid-media-social-ads-specialist` rather than absorbing paid scope. New metrics: **First-Hour Signal (proven, not assumed)** and **Employee-Notification Restraint** (opt-out rate as the guardrail). Ideas-only, written from scratch; surfaced by the `linkedin-company-pages` skill in [social-media-skills/skills](https://github.com/social-media-skills/skills) (MIT). The Notify Employees cadence, opt-out, relevance-model, and delivery-timing facts are cited to [LinkedIn Pages — Use Employee Notifications](https://www.linkedin.com/help/linkedin/answer/a549372) (read 2026-08-07); the first-hour and link-in-comments effects are flagged **contested/practitioner-reported** and framed as own-account hypotheses; the source's specific multiplier figures (4×/7×/−60× and similar) were **not** adopted, as they trace to vendor blogs rather than primary sources. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **Programmatic Media Buyer learns to audit supply quality** ([`paid-media/paid-media-programmatic-buyer.md`](paid-media/paid-media-programmatic-buyer.md), both dual-located copies) — the agent's **first enhancement** (61 lines, untouched since the first pass). Rule 6 mandated "premium publisher list, content category allowlists, fraud detection, and brand safety verification" as one control and supplied no method for any of it. Those are **three different questions** — was my ad beside bad content (brand safety), was it served to a human (invalid traffic), and was the domain worth buying through a chain worth paying in a slot anyone could see (supply quality) — and only the third one drains a B2B display budget. Gap grep-verified: `ads.txt`, `sellers.json`, `supply chain`, `supply path`, `Active View`, `measurable impression`, `log-level`, `domain report`, `GIVT`, `SIVT`, `pre-bid`, `verification vendor`, `arbitrage`, `inclusion list`, `invalid traffic`, `click fraud`, and `made for advertising` all returned **zero** hits repo-wide. Adds **Critical Rule 9**, a **"Buying an Audience, Receiving an Impression: Auditing Supply Quality"** section, and a **Supply Quality & Domain Disposition Audit** deliverable. The section teaches that the platform already filters and credits back the fraud the old metric told you to chase (Google removes invalid traffic pre-bid and post-serve, and the "Invalid clicks" column reports traffic *already detected and not charged*); that made-for-advertising inventory is not fraud but real impressions on domains that exist to convert ad spend into revenue, identified by **manual judgment on combined indicators** rather than any single ratio, and that the homepage is not the tell; that **viewable rate is denominated in measurable impressions, not delivered ones**, so a placement you mostly cannot see into can report a flattering viewable rate — read the pair, and let **unmeasurable round to unknown, never to viewable**; that the domain report is sorted by **spend, not impressions**, with four dispositions written *before* it is opened and the spend-concentration curve read as a finding in itself; that `ads.txt`, `sellers.json`, and the OpenRTB SupplyChain object answer two questions a buyer can actually act on (is the seller authorised, and how many fee-taking nodes sit between you and the publisher); that log-level access is a **contract term, not a setting**, so absent it the honest report says *unverified*; and that blocking domains one at a time is a losing race against supply that regenerates faster than the list grows — the structural fix is an **inclusion list**, whose cost in reach and CPM is the right B2B trade because reach was never the binding constraint. Also fixes **two unmeasurable success metrics**: `<2% fraudulent impression rate` (which measured the platform's filter, not the buy) and `85%+ of impressions on premium publishers` (a percentage against an undefined tier). Facts cited to [DV360 invalid-traffic filtering](https://support.google.com/displayvideo/answer/6076504), [Google Ads invalid traffic](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/11182074), [IAB Tech Lab on identifying MFA](https://iabtechlab.com/using-openrtb-signals-to-identify-made-for-advertising/), [Google Active View viewability](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/7029393), [ads.txt](https://iabtechlab.com/ads-txt/), and [sellers.json / SupplyChain Object](https://iabtechlab.com/sellers-json/) (read 2026-08-07). **No MFA waste percentage is asserted** — published estimates vary widely by study, year, and SSP; measure your own domain report.
- **Competitive Intelligence Specialist learns to read competitors' live advertising** ([`product-marketing/pmm-competitive-intelligence.md`](product-marketing/pmm-competitive-intelligence.md), both dual-located copies) — resolves the backlog's open half of the `olson-adam/ad-mastermind` item (competitor-ad field scans vs. `pmm-competitive-intelligence`). The agent's Rule 1 enumerates its independent CI sources — product research, G2/Capterra reviews, pricing pages, hiring/LinkedIn, SEC filings, social listening — and **omits advertising entirely**; its update cadence is quarterly (Rule 2) and its triggers fire on funding/hiring/launch *announcements* (Rule 6), all lagging or press-shaped. A competitor's live advertising is the one signal that shows, for free and in public, what they are spending budget to say *right now*. Gap grep-verified before writing: `ad library`, `ad-transparency`, `transparency center`, `creative velocity`, `working material`, and `phrase-frequency` returned **zero** hits across `product-marketing/`, `paid-media/`, and `plugins/`; the Creative Strategist has no competitor-ad, inspiration, or swipe coverage, so there is no seam conflict. Adds **Critical Rule 9** and a **"Reading Competitors' Live Advertising"** section pointing at the three official, free, public ad-transparency libraries (verified 2026-08-07): the [LinkedIn Ad Library](https://www.linkedin.com/ad-library) (ads since 1 June 2023, visible one year after last impression, searchable by company/keyword/country/date, EU ads showing impression ranges and targeting), the [Google Ads Transparency Center](https://adstransparency.google.com/) (search by advertiser or website across Search/Display/Gmail/YouTube, with paying entity, location, and dates), and the [Meta Ad Library](https://www.facebook.com/ads/library/) (a Page's currently-running Facebook/Instagram ads, appearing within 24h of first impression — with spend/impression/targeting detail and multi-year retention limited to social-issue/electoral ads and, under the EU DSA, EU-targeted ads). What to read (the funded message/offer/CTA as a stronger strategy signal than homepage copy; launch/ICP/geo expansion often visible in ads before the press release, with an appeared-vs-announced date gap logged to measure the lead time; **creative volume as a state, not a snapshot**) and what not to infer (**presence is a claim, not a result** — you cannot read a competitor's performance from the outside, so ad presence feeds positioning intel while ad effectiveness stays unknowable). Two hard boundaries: competitor ad creative is **copyrighted working material** — summarize category-level patterns, never redistribute a competitor's ad verbatim into a battle card, deck, or public "teardown" (an IP problem under Rule 4 and the Legal Compliance Officer's remit, and a strategy one); and **absence in a library is unknown, not a finding** (a competitor may run on a channel with no public library, or the library may lag). Seam stated in-file: reading competitors' ads to understand their *strategy* is CI and lives here; testing your own creative is the Creative Strategist's — a competitor's ad is a market hypothesis, never a template to copy. Ships a **Competitor Ad-Activity Scan** deliverable and an **Advertising Signal Coverage** success metric. Ideas-only; the public-ad-libraries-as-CI-source method and the working-material discipline credited to [olson-adam/ad-mastermind](https://github.com/olson-adam/ad-mastermind) (MIT, re-verified 2026-08-07), whose own approach uses a paid Apify scraper (**not adopted** — the official first-party libraries are free and authoritative); the three official-library framing, the presence-is-not-performance and absence-is-unknown rules, the creative-volume-as-state read, the appeared-vs-announced lead-time metric, and the Creative-Strategist seam are ours. All platform facts cited to primary sources (LinkedIn Ad Library Help, Google Ads Transparency Center Help, Meta Ad Library) read 2026-08-07; **no spend, impression, or effectiveness figure asserted** for any competitor. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **Quality Assurance Manager learns to prove its scorer actually discriminated** ([`client-ops/ops-quality-assurance.md`](client-ops/ops-quality-assurance.md), both dual-located copies) — resolves the backlog's `olson-adam/ad-mastermind` discrimination-control item after re-checking the source held up (still MIT, now carrying documented field-scan and gauntlet runs that log where their own blinding failed rather than hiding it — the "seen to survive contact" bar the 2026-08-03 note set). The Reference Class section (shipped 2026-07-31) names "**a generous scorer is useless**" as its operative discipline but supplies no way to *detect* one: a reviewer — or, increasingly, an AI-assisted scoring pass — can drift into grading a whole set of headline/subject/concept variants uniformly **strong**, and the copy-scoring pass had no catch for it. Gap grep-verified: `decoy`, `negative anchor`, `discrimination control`, and `blind judg*` returned **zero** hits repo-wide. Adds a **"The Discrimination Control: Proving the Scorer Actually Discriminated"** section: when scoring a *set* judged together, seed one deliberately weak **negative-anchor decoy** (benefit-free and generic, or a known past under-performer), judge the set blind with the decoy unmarked, and **read the decoy's placement first** — if it lands at/near the bottom the round's rankings are trustworthy, but if it grades strong or outranks a real candidate the **round is invalid** (the scorer was flattering, not evaluating) and its scores are discarded, not salvaged. Plus an honest-decoy construction rule (plausibly in-format and genuinely weak — not a joke line, not a strong line in disguise; when scoring against Tier-1 first-party data, a documented past under-performer is the best decoy), a log-the-control-pass-or-fail discipline mirroring the source's own `blinding_failed` transparency, and a guardrail that **the decoy proves discrimination, not quality** (a passed control ratifies the *ranking*, never the winner — absolute quality is still the reference class's job — and set-scoring only, since a single deliverable in isolation has no ranking to invalidate). Wholly on this agent — it owns copy scoring outright, so no cross-agent seam. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the seed-a-decoy-and-void-the-round mechanic credited to [olson-adam/ad-mastermind](https://github.com/olson-adam/ad-mastermind) (MIT, re-verified 2026-08-06), which applies it to scoring ad concepts against real market campaigns; the set-size boundary, the honest-decoy construction rule, and the proves-discrimination-not-quality guardrail are ours. No detection-accuracy or scoring figures asserted. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **AI Search Optimizer learns to measure the traffic AI answers actually send** ([`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md), both dual-located copies; one-sentence cross-reference added to [`analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md`](analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md), both copies) — the agent's funnel is stated as `reachable → recognized → quotable`, and its Success Metrics already promise to "attribute 5-10% of monthly qualified traffic to AI answer engine referrals" — a fourth stage (**arrival**) it committed to a number for but had **no method** for. Gap grep-verified: `chatgpt.com`, `utm_source=chatgpt`, and `AI referral` returned **zero** hits in agent content (the `referrer` hits are newsletter mechanics; the `referral traffic` hits are all `seo-link-building-strategist` on link quality). Adds a **"Measuring Arrival: The Traffic AI Answers Actually Send"** section whose spine is that a near-zero AI-referral number is usually a **measurement artifact, not a finding**: native assistant apps, copy-pasted links, and in-assistant browsers arrive with no document referrer and fall into GA4 **Direct** ("Source exactly matches `(direct)` AND Medium is one of `(not set)`, `(none)`" — quoted from Google, read 2026-08-06), so the observed number is a **floor, not a total**, and the standing *unknown never rounds to observed* discipline says to rule out the instrument before calling it a finding. Also: build an **AI channel group** on the referrer domains you *can* see (`chatgpt.com`, `perplexity.ai`, `gemini.google.com`, …); UTM only on links you **actually control** (you can't tag a link an assistant composes, so a controlled-link UTM is a floor inside the floor); and **judge the segment by conversion/pipeline, never by raw session count**, because the volume is understated by an unknown amount. **Seam decided in-file** (the certify-vs-verdict shape used for the 2026-08-03 CPL reconciliation): the section lives on the AEO agent — it completes that agent's own funnel and backs its own unbacked *Citation traffic attribution* metric — while the GA4 **construction** (the custom channel group, the referrer-domain match list, the observed/modeled/missing decomposition) is handed to `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`, whose established measurement-layer craft it is, and which gains a one-sentence reciprocal pointer in its GA4-audit check 6. Interpretation on one agent, construction on the other, navigable from both sides. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the gap is credited to the `ai-referral-analytics` skill in [`jstanx/aeo-toolkit`](https://github.com/jstanx/aeo-toolkit) (MIT, verified 2026-08-06); the floor-not-total framing, the controlled-links-only UTM rule, the judge-the-segment discipline, and the construction/interpretation seam are ours. GA4's Direct definition cited to [Google's channel definitions](https://support.google.com/analytics/answer/9756891); **no figure is asserted for the share of AI traffic that arrives without a referrer** — it is unmeasured by nature, which is the section's whole point. Lint 4/4, both copies of each agent diff-identical.
- **AI Search Optimizer learns that recognition is settled off your own site** ([`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md), both dual-located copies) — Critical Rule 2 asserted that "AI systems heavily weight entity signals for citation decisions" and then sent the reader entirely to their own JSON-LD; the Entity Markup Implementation Plan was on-site only. Markup is a *claim you make about yourself*, while recognition is a *conclusion the engine reaches* from how consistently the rest of the web describes the same company — so the agent owned only the half of the entity a company can write itself. A new **"Recognition Before Citation: The Entity Lives Off Your Site"** section, **Critical Rule 10**, and an **Entity Recognition Audit & Reconciliation Plan** deliverable close it: `sameAs` as a one-place identity join rather than a link tactic; a B2B SaaS corroboration set (review platforms, company databases, the LinkedIn page, Wikidata, developer surfaces, press) diffed row by row against one canonical fact record; **fact drift as a dated-event problem** — the wrong description of a company is almost never invented, it is *the last true thing still being repeated*, so the reconciliation sweep attaches to the raise / rename / acquisition / leadership announcement rather than to a hygiene calendar; disambiguation for name collisions and the two-entities-in-the-wild period after a rebrand; **Wikidata and Wikipedia separated as different bars with different risks**, including a hard guardrail against self-authored or undisclosed Wikipedia editing; the knowledge panel read as an instrument you can claim and suggest edits to, never author; and a **recognition test built on brand questions graded on the description**, distinct from the topic-question citability heatmap, where *recognized-and-wrong* is triaged ahead of *unknown* and a hedging engine counts as unknown rather than a soft yes. Two unmeasurable success metrics were fixed in passing — an "Entity Authority score" no entity-extraction API publishes, and "top 3 entity rankings" which are not a thing — and a brand-question recognition metric added. Ideas-only; surfaced by [`jstanx/aeo-toolkit`](https://github.com/jstanx/aeo-toolkit) and independently by [`Thibaultbm/claude-seo-geo`](https://github.com/Thibaultbm/claude-seo-geo) (both MIT), written from scratch; every fact cited to a primary source (schema.org, Google's Organization structured-data and knowledge-panel documentation, Wikidata:Notability, Wikipedia:Paid-contribution disclosure) read 2026-08-06, with **no citation or ranking effect asserted for any action**.
- **Performance Analyst learns to report a stock metric's change as flows, not a net** ([`analytics/analytics-performance-analyst.md`](analytics/analytics-performance-analyst.md), both dual-located copies) — resolves the backlog's "`growth-accounting`'s movement identity" item (and completes a change the prior run left half-written, with three references pointing at a section that did not exist). A "flat" customer base or MRR is almost always a large inflow and a large outflow that net near zero, and the net erases the question that matters — is the bucket filling faster than it leaks. A new **"Movement Accounting: A Flat Number Is Two Flows"** section plus **Critical Rule 10** supply the discipline: decompose period-over-period movement into the additive identity **Closing = Opening + New + Resurrected + Expansion − Contraction − Churned**, reconciling *exactly* to the observed net with any residual left visible rather than smoothed into the nearest bucket. Four layers beyond the source identity: the **count-vs-value distinction** (expansion and contraction exist only on value metrics — a logo is one logo whether it upgrades or not — so a customer *count* moves only on new/resurrected/churned while its *revenue* moves on all five); **five boundary decisions** (entity, identity, reactivation window, maturity, value) each framed as an instrument change that reclassifies flows with *zero* customer behavior, cross-linked to the series-break register from the anomaly section; the **maturity bias on fresh-period outflow** (cancellations effective at term end, involuntary/dunning churn still inside its retry cycle, and late-booked downgrades make the newest period understate outflow *by construction* — the movement-metric twin of the cohort-maturity phantom downtrend); and the **voluntary-vs-involuntary churn split** plus **contraction-as-leading-indicator-of-churn**, two reads the net conceals. Adds a **Period Movement Waterfall** deliverable (whole-base opening→closing loop for one period, paired with the Cohort Analysis Report's single-vintage-forward view) and a **Movement Reconciliation** success metric. The SaaS quick ratio — (New + Expansion) / (Contraction + Churn) — is named but presented as a derived read, **not a target**. Ideas-only; the additive identity credited to the `growth-accounting` skill in [krillinai/growth-skills](https://github.com/krillinai/growth-skills) (MIT); the four layers above are ours; no ratio thresholds, churn benchmarks, or other figures asserted (a healthy movement profile is a property of a specific account). Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **Performance Analyst learns to rule out the instrument before naming a cause** ([`analytics/analytics-performance-analyst.md`](analytics/analytics-performance-analyst.md), both dual-located copies) — Rule 7 sent the analyst to investigate a metric that "significantly diverges" and offered four candidate causes (campaign change, external event, technical issue, market shift), three of which answer *why the world moved* — before anything establishes that it moved at all. It also never defined *significantly*. A new **"Reading a Change Before Explaining It"** section plus **Critical Rule 9** supply the method: five declared verdicts with `measurement change` ranked ahead of any behavioral cause and `expected variation` / `unresolved` as legitimate closes; a comparability freeze before any period-over-period claim; a dated **series-break register** whose routing table names, for each instrument change this repo already governs — consent posture, GA4 config, the lead-scoring model, the field layer, attribution windows, LinkedIn delivery settings — the metric it moves with *zero* behavioral change and the agent to confirm it with; the B2B maturity argument (long outcome windows against a fixed calendar make the freshest column of every cohort report understated by construction, producing a permanent phantom downtrend); an absolute-count floor beneath every percentage threshold, because eight demos to five is a 38% collapse and an ordinary month; mix-versus-within-segment decomposition that reconciles to the parent total; and a mechanism stage that requires contradicting evidence and the smallest discriminating check. Adds an **Anomaly Investigation Record** deliverable and a **Verdict Quality** metric, and fixes two internal contradictions: the "95% of anomalies within 48 hours" SLA (impossible for long-window metrics, and an invitation to fabricate a cause on deadline) is now set per metric against its own maturity, and "segment analysis reveals 15-25% variation" no longer makes finding variation a quota against Rule 3's mandatory 5-7 segments. Ideas-only; five-verdict resolution, comparability freeze, and anti-fishing rule credited to `growth-anomaly-investigation` in [krillinai/growth-skills](https://github.com/krillinai/growth-skills) (MIT); no figures or benchmarks asserted.
- **Messaging Architect gets a *method* for Rule 5's messaging validation** ([`product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md`](product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md), both dual-located copies) — Rule 5 mandated testing messaging with sales teams, advisory boards, and prospects before rollout and "never launch messaging you haven't validated," then *named the audiences and stopped*: no definition of a signal, no way to know a test can even answer the question at B2B volumes, and no verdict for the case a named-account motion actually lives in — where the test is underpowered. Gap grep-verified: `underpowered`, `minimum detectable`, `stopping rule`, `pre-register`, `feasibility`, `composite signal`, `echo moment`, and `winner` all returned **zero** hits in the agent. Adds a **"Validating the Message: Diagnose, Gate, Then Judge on a Composite"** section in three ordered moves. **(1) Diagnose before you test** — score each asset on **five pillars** (ICP alignment, differentiation, clarity, emotion-as-*consequence*, cross-channel consistency), each weak/adequate/strong with the failing pillar routed to its real fix: a clarity failure is a rewrite not a test, a differentiation failure routes to `pmm-positioning-strategist`, and only a message clean on all five with a genuine open question earns a scarce live test (the messaging analog of the Creative Strategist's "test big levers first"). **(2) Gate before you test** — the messaging sibling of pre-registration, asked *before the test is designed*: targetability (are the responses coming from the buyer you're testing or from whoever the optimizer finds cheapest), qualified-volume (enough in-ICP interactions per variant to separate the arms — the Creative Strategist's inverse-square volume arithmetic applies unchanged), and measurement reach (does instrumentation see past the click); a volume failure drops you to the composite, it does not license promoting whichever arm leads. **(3) Judge on a composite** — the verdict is **validated / not validated / not yet**, explicitly **not** "statistically significant," on a *majority* of mixed quantitative (reply rate, conversion, scroll depth, demo-request — directional, never significance-tested unless the gate passed) and qualitative signals (5-second clarity, objection reduction, stated preference, sales believability, and **echo moments** — a prospect repeating your phrasing back unprompted). **Seam with `paid-media-creative-strategist` decided in-file so the two can't contradict on what "a winner" is:** the Creative Strategist governs whether a *powered paid A/B test* may declare a statistically-significant winner and refuses to round underpowered noise to one — that stays authoritative and is deferred to for any powered paid test; but B2B message validation is *almost always* underpowered as a statistical test (the normal case, not a failure), which is precisely the Creative Strategist's own final rung, *decide by structured judgment and label it as judgment* — this scorecard is that rung made into a method, and the qualitative signal is the instrument rather than a consolation prize. Ships a **Message Validation Plan & Scorecard** deliverable, a **Message Validation Integrity** success metric, and a pointer added to Rule 5. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the five-pillar audit, the pre-test feasibility gate, and the composite scorecard are credited to the `message-market-fit` skill in [Fearofsnakes/pmm-skillset](https://github.com/Fearofsnakes/pmm-skillset) (MIT, verified 2026-08-05); the seam resolution, the weak/adequate/strong grading, the triage-by-failing-pillar routing, the three gate checks, and the majority-rule/qualitative-outweighs guardrails are ours. No effect sizes or benchmark figures asserted; the volume arithmetic is inherited from the Creative Strategist, not re-derived. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **Messaging Architect gains the third variation axis: segment** ([`product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md`](product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md), both dual-located copies) — Rule 3 gave the agent two axes to vary messaging on, persona and buying moment, and omitted the one sales asks for constantly and answers worst: **market segment**. A VP of Engineering at a hospital system and a VP of Engineering at a fintech are the same persona at the same buying moment and are not moved by the same opening line. Gap grep-verified first: `vertical messaging`, `industry messaging`, and `swap test` returned **zero** hits repo-wide; every `vertical` hit was an ICP *targeting* dimension (`sales-outbound-strategist`: "industry verticals with highest fit"; `phase-1-strategy`: "Industries (vertical focus)") or a reference-account *tag* (`pmm-customer-advocacy`), and the only messaging mention was one clause inside a deliverable ("industry/vertical pages"). Adds a **"Messaging by Segment: Route the Entry Point, Don't Fork the House"** section and **Critical Rule 10**, built on the point that segment messaging is a **routing** problem, not an authoring one: the pillars, narrative, and proof architecture stay locked, and per segment you decide only *which pillar leads* and the *one-to-two lines* that reach that buyer — because the two things teams actually do are both expensive (one averaged message that describes no one; or a message house per vertical, where every fork drifts independently, every repositioning becomes an N-times job, and the surface-against-surface consistency audit inherits N× the pairs to check). Route each segment by **buying trigger** — what changed in their world that started the search *this quarter* — rather than standing pain, which explains category relevance and no urgency at all. Then five disciplines the idea is usually shipped without: **one segment axis per map, chosen for buyer recognizability** (vertical, size/stage, use case, system-of-record, regulatory regime — and if a buyer wouldn't use the axis to describe themselves unprompted, it is a CRM reporting dimension and the messaging will sound like one); **no invented triggers**, since a well-written fabricated trigger is indistinguishable from a real one on the page and everyone downstream will build a landing page on it — evidence or an inferred tag with a validation task, under the standing *inferred never rounds to verified* rule, and no inferred row goes live on a homepage or paid surface; **triggers are perishable while pillars are not** — a passed compliance deadline reads exactly as persuasive as it did when true, so every trigger carries a date and a review class (deadline / wave / structural) and the map is re-verified quarterly rather than semi-annually; **the base-rate counterfactual** — the segment's non-buyers lived through the same regulation and the same audit season, so a trigger earns its row only if it shows up disproportionately in accounts that actually entered a cycle, read against win/loss with `pmm-competitive-intelligence`; and **a segment entry point makes a credibility promise the proof library has to keep** — naming a vertical implies vertical evidence, and since `pmm-customer-advocacy` already tags reference accounts by vertical/industry this is a lookup, with unbacked rows either softening to a segment-*framed* claim on cross-industry proof or waiting while the missing reference is logged as a gap. Closes with two tests: the **swap test** (an entry point that still reads fine on another segment is a generic tagline occupying a row) and its inverse the **merge test** (two segments routing to the same pillar with interchangeable entry points are one segment — verticalizing is never free, so carry a row only where the trigger, lens, or must-have outcome genuinely differs). Ships a **Segment Entry-Point Routing Map** deliverable and a **`Segment Routing Integrity`** metric. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the routing method, trigger-first framing, one-pillar constraint, and swap test are credited in-file to the `vertical-messaging` skill in [matteotitta/genesys-skills](https://github.com/matteotitta/genesys-skills) (MIT); the two failure modes and their maintenance cost, the axis-recognizability test, trigger perishability, the base-rate counterfactual, the segment-proof check, and the merge test are ours. The source's numeric confidence thresholds (≥60% verified / ≤10% estimated rows) were **deliberately not adopted** — an arbitrary percentage bar a three-segment map cannot meaningfully meet. No effect sizes or benchmark figures asserted. Lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **Legal Compliance Officer learns that consent and suppression are one cross-channel record, not per-tool state** ([`client-ops/ops-legal-compliance.md`](client-ops/ops-legal-compliance.md), both dual-located copies) — resolves the backlog's "consent/suppression as a durable shared record" scope item. The agent already owned consent management and "track opt-outs and honor them" (Rule 3), but framed it per-regulation and per-channel; nothing repo-wide stated that an opt-out captured in one channel must propagate to **all** channels that can initiate contact. Gap grep-verified: no `cross-channel` suppression statement existed, and the only adjacent mention (`email-deliverability-specialist` line 140) framed cross-platform suppression sync narrowly as *"preventing duplicate sends"* — a deliverability concern on the wrong owner for the legal framing — while the Social Ads Specialist builds Meta/LinkedIn **custom audiences from customer email lists** with no opt-out-before-upload step (the concrete re-contact and CPRA-"share" risk). **Scope decided at the seam:** the record's system of record is the CRM/CDP (operational contact data, *not* `brand-context.md`, which holds brand config not contact lists), so what was missing is the *discipline*, which is advisory — placed on the consent owner as **Critical Rule 9**, a **"Consent Is One Record; Every Channel Reads It Before Contact"** section (per-tool consent fragmentation; GDPR Art. 21 / Art. 7(3) and CCPA/CPRA opt-out bind the business not the tool; ad-platform list uploads as a regulated "share" that decays on each audience refresh, with the service-provider caveat flagged for counsel), and a **Cross-Channel Suppression Coverage** success metric. Single owner, no channel-agent edits (the Legal Compliance Officer is the cross-cutting advisory owner). Ideas-only; regulation cited to the primary statutes, no fabricated figures; lint 2/2, copies diff-identical.
- **Attribution Analyst learns that a campaign has two costs-per-lead, and the gap between them is the finding** ([`paid-media/paid-media-attribution-analyst.md`](paid-media/paid-media-attribution-analyst.md), both dual-located copies; one-sentence cross-reference added to [`paid-media/paid-media-social-ads-specialist.md`](paid-media/paid-media-social-ads-specialist.md), both copies) — resolves the backlog's platform-CPL-vs-CRM-CPL reconciliation item at its seam. The agent already owned *object-level* reconciliation (the CRM Integration & Data Mapping deliverable: do platform conversions map to CRM leads/opportunities, 95%-mapping target) but had **zero** coverage of the derived *ratio* — `CPL` appeared nowhere in the file. Two disciplines were genuinely absent: (a) **platform-reported CPL and CRM-derived CPL for the same campaign routinely disagree** — the platform fires on events the CRM never receives (form abandons that still tripped the pixel, duplicates, bot/junk, cross-device double-counts), the attribution windows differ, and leads get disqualified after the conversion is booked — so the move is to reconcile per campaign and **report the gap itself with a decomposition, not the more flattering figure** (a campaign whose two CPLs diverge threefold is neither cheap nor expensive, it is *unmeasured*, and "unmeasured" is the finding); and (b) the **ordering rule** — audit that conversion tracking fires, maps, and is deduplicated *before* computing any waste/scale/pause verdict, because a verdict built on broken measurement is not wrong occasionally but wrong *by construction and confidently*. Adds a **"Two CPLs Disagree: Reconcile the Gap Before Anyone Spends Against It"** section, **Critical Rule 10**, and a **CPL reconciliation coverage** metric. **Seam decided in-file:** measurement reconciliation is the Attribution Analyst's (it certifies the number); the campaign-level scale/pause verdict stays with the channel specialists — so the Social Ads Specialist's Delivery-vs-Targeting Audit gains a one-sentence pointer routing its CPLs through the reconciliation before use. The ordering rule matters more now that two sibling analyses (the PPC Strategist's negative-keyword work and the Social Ads Specialist's delivery-vs-targeting audit) each produce a confident campaign number over whatever tracking error sits underneath. Ideas surfaced by `cpl-vs-crm-reconciliation` / `conversion-tracking-audit` in [mardab96/linkedin-ads-claude-skills](https://github.com/mardab96/linkedin-ads-claude-skills) (MIT), ideas-only, written from scratch; the gap-as-finding framing, the "unmeasured is the finding" rule, and the certify-vs-verdict seam are ours. **No divergence figure asserted** — reconcile your own account's gap rather than adopting anyone's number. Both dual-located copies of each agent are diff-identical; lint 4/4.
- **Social Ads Specialist learns that delivery is not targeting** ([`paid-media/paid-media-social-ads-specialist.md`](paid-media/paid-media-social-ads-specialist.md), both dual-located copies) — the agent's stated superpower is "targeting precision that reaches ideal customer profiles while eliminating waste," and Rules 4 and 8 govern who you *select* — but nothing anywhere checked whether delivery honored that selection, against **two LinkedIn settings that are enabled by default**: Audience Expansion ("automatically enabled on ad sets where it's available," serving members with attributes merely *similar* to your targets) and the LinkedIn Audience Network ("automatically enabled for new single image, carousel, document, and video ad sets," serving your ads on third-party apps and sites). Gap grep-verified first: `demographics report` returned **zero** hits repo-wide, `seniority` returned four — all in customer-research recruiting and ghostwriting cadence, **none in any paid agent** — and every `audience expansion` hit meant lookalike growth, never the setting. Adds a **"Delivery Is Not Targeting: Auditing Who Actually Received the Budget"** section: the two defaults in LinkedIn's own words; the point that they **fail in opposite directions and need different evidence** (Expansion corrupts *who* → the demographics breakdown; LAN corrupts *where* → invisible there, visible only in the placement split, so reading one report to conclude about both is the real analytical error); the **ABM enumeration argument** (a matched account list is a finite enumeration, not a description — "similar members" cannot be in it, so Expansion on an ABM campaign abandons the list while still reporting under its name); an **ICP inclusion set written before the report is opened**, because classifying afterward reverse-engineers an ICP that matches delivery and always passes; four dispositions (on-ICP / leak / **review** / insufficient data) where off-ICP-but-*converting* slices are a signal the ICP is too narrow rather than something to exclude; and three limits that turn confident numbers wrong — the report is denominated in **impressions and clicks, not spend** (every budget-share figure is a labeled proxy), thin facets are **suppressed to protect member identity** and that floor hides *small* slices, so fragmented leakage reads as a clean report and *not shown never rounds to not delivered*, and some spill is structural, with near-zero off-ICP delivery usually meaning an over-narrowed audience rather than perfect targeting. Closes with running LAN like the programmatic buy it is (IAB-category exclusions, allow/block lists, verification profiles, its own CPL and lead-quality bar) — the controls our programmatic agent already mandates for DSP inventory but that were never wired to LinkedIn. Ships a **Delivery-vs-Targeting Audit** deliverable, an **On-ICP delivery share** metric, and **Critical Rule 9**. Ideas-only, written from scratch; surfaced by [mardab96/linkedin-ads-claude-skills](https://github.com/mardab96/linkedin-ads-claude-skills) (MIT), credited in-file, with the opposite-failure-modes framing, ABM enumeration argument, suppression consequence, and over-narrow inverse tell ours. All platform behavior cited to LinkedIn's own Marketing Solutions Help (read 2026-08-03); **no waste percentage asserted** — the figures circulating for LAN and Expansion trace to agency blogs, not to LinkedIn.
- **Brand context gains a correction-write-back discipline** ([`templates/brand-context.md`](templates/brand-context.md), both dual-located copies) — the file every agent reads was read-only-forever, carrying only a vague "review whenever positioning/pricing/ICP changes" footer and no way to *retire* a fact once it's wrong. Adds **Rule 6**: when you or the user find mid-task that something here is outdated or a cited proof point no longer holds (a churned reference customer, a withdrawn logo, a revised metric, a persona pain contradicted by field data), surface it with a `[CORRECT BRAND CONTEXT: …]` marker — reusing the existing `[NEEDS INPUT: …]` convention — rather than fixing it only in the one deliverable that caught it, so every other agent stops inheriting the stale fact. Adds a compact **§14 "Corrections & learnings"** dated table whose first job is withdrawn proof: a standing record that a customer, logo, or number must now *stop* appearing in public copy. Closes the evidence-integrity loop the file already half-owned (Rules 1–2 forbid *inventing* proof; nothing let it *retire* proof). Scoped from indranilbanerjee/digital-marketing-pro's Update-Back Rule (MIT), ideas-only; its Two-Views Model and cached-state machinery were evaluated and declined as presupposing a per-engagement deliverable lifecycle this library deliberately doesn't have.
- **Content Optimizer learns to find cannibalization the decay triage structurally can't see** ([`seo/seo-content-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-content-optimizer.md), both dual-located copies) — the agent's Consolidate disposition already *resolves* cannibalization, but only once a **decline** puts the cluster on the decay triage; the worst cannibalization never declines, because the pages were never good (three URLs alternating on one query for years, none winning, none falling far enough to trip a threshold). Adds a **"Finding Cannibalization the Decay Triage Never Sees"** section — a standing library audit, not a decline-triggered one: cluster existing pages from free on-page signals by subset containment / same-primary-keyword / semantic-intent overlap; grade severity as overlap type × ranking disparity (URLs *alternating* on the query = the true signature; a stable single winner = topical breadth, not cannibalization); resolve with a four-way disposition (merge / canonical / differentiate / no-action) that is often *not* removal; never merge on lexical overlap alone (shared words ≠ shared intent), and degrade gracefully from SERP-confirmed cases to a lexical-candidate worklist when no query data is available. Adds a **Cannibalization Audit** deliverable and a **Cannibalization coverage** metric. Closes the backlog's cannibalization-detection item; owner decided at the seam (detection is a library audit → Content Optimizer; the Blog Strategist keeps prospective prevention). Source `blog-cannibalization` in AgriciDaniel/claude-blog (MIT), ideas-only.
- **Marketing Ops Architect learns that a duplicated tag doubles the numbers it certifies — and where the container audit stops being its job** ([`analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md`](analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md), both dual-located copies) — resolves the backlog's tag-container-hygiene scope question. The agent's own thesis is that "if instrumentation is wrong, every downstream number inherits the error silently," yet its six-check GA4 audit never named the single most common way that happens: a duplicated GA4 config or event tag firing twice. Check 1 ("fires where it should and **only there**") covered wrong *placement*, not double *counting*, and nothing connected "a metric moved with no marketing cause" to "suspect the container first." Adds a **seventh check — "Tag integrity"** — folding in only the one measurement-invalidating failure (Google: *"if the same Google tag is configured two or more times on the same page, it may cause duplicate data or mixed settings"*), with the diagnostic tell borrowed from the consent section's banner-change confound (a sharp step with no site release, campaign change, or seasonality is an instrument change until proven otherwise) and a requirement that container publishes be annotated so trend continuity is checkable. The **scope decision is drawn explicitly in-file**: the *mechanical* half of container hygiene the source listed — orphaned tags, tag/trigger/variable naming conventions, weekly container diffs, unpublished-draft governance — is analytics-engineering / web-implementation work outside an advisory persona's remit and was **deliberately declined**; this agent reads the container's *effect on the numbers*, not the wrench. Dimension named by the paywalled, not-adopted `/gtm-audit` in [cognyai/claude-code-marketing-skills](https://github.com/cognyai/claude-code-marketing-skills) (dimension credited, no content adopted — same handling as its `/ga4-audit` and `/gtm-audit` consent dimensions); the duplicate-tag behavior quoted from [Google Analytics Help — Google tag management](https://support.google.com/analytics/answer/12329709), read 2026-08-02.
- **Content Optimizer learns to diagnose a decline before prescribing a refresh** ([`seo/seo-content-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-content-optimizer.md), both dual-located copies) — an agent whose entire identity is "80% of SEO wins come from improving existing content" had a **calendar-driven** trigger (Rule 7: quarterly review of the top 100 pages) and **no branch that does not end in an edit**. Consolidation was a single clause inside one deliverable; pruning did not exist. Repo-wide, `content decay`, `dropped out`, `deindex`, `content inventory`, and `historical optimization` all returned **zero** hits, `redirect`/`canonical` lived only in `seo-technical-auditor` (technical, not content-lifecycle), and `retire` only in the analytics agents (field and campaign retirement). Adds a **"Reading a Decline Before Prescribing a Fix"** section, **Critical Rule 9**, a `Content Decay Triage` deliverable, and two success metrics.
