Covers: setting the comparison up so the delta means something (equal-length periods, **identical filters**, a year-ago pull wherever seasonality is plausible, and "dropped out" only when row limits and filters matched — otherwise you are recommending the retirement of a page that is fine); a **click floor applied before any percentage**, because a 60% decline on a 12-click page is seven clicks and B2B libraries are full of pages at those volumes, so a decay report ranked by percentage is topped almost entirely by noise (the same *a percentage over too few events is not a signal* discipline `paid-media-creative-strategist` applies to creative tests); a **four-signature diagnosis grid** reading impressions and position together, because falling clicks is the symptom and the four combinations do not share a response — demand loss, ranking loss, **SERP interception**, and a deliberate intent re-alignment that looks like damage; **four dispositions, three of which are not a refresh** (refresh / consolidate / leave alone / retire), with "leave alone" recorded *with its date* as an output rather than a gap, and retirement decided by what points at the URL — external links, internal links, live campaigns, sales collateral — never by word count; an **impostor check** run before any recommendation is written (property/tracking change, migration, indexation, seasonality, and the site-wide case where *if the whole property fell by the same amount, the page is not the story*); and an **asymmetry guardrail** — merging is a morning's work and unmerging is a quarter's, so anything that removes a URL inherits the repo's spend-change discipline: baseline, small attributable batches, a held rollback, and measurement of both directions. The interception row is the one that punishes a reflexive refresh and the one that became common in 2026: the page is winning the ranking and losing the click, so "make it more thorough and better structured" makes it a *more liftable* answer — that page routes to `seo-ai-search-optimizer`, whose job is being the cited source rather than the clicked one. On confirming it: Google's generative AI performance report in Search Console covers AI Overviews and AI Mode but reports **impressions only** — no clicks, no position, no queries — and is rolling out to a subset of properties, its data a subset of what already sits in the Web search type of the main Performance report. So AI-surface *exposure* is confirmable and AI-surface *click loss* is not; the section permits "shown inside AI features while position held and clicks fell" and forbids "AI Overviews took N clicks." Discipline surfaced by the [AgriciDaniel/claude-blog](https://github.com/AgriciDaniel/claude-blog) `blog-decay` skill (MIT) and independently corroborated by [rampstackco/claude-skills](https://github.com/rampstackco/claude-skills) (MIT) and [inhouseseo/superseo-skills](https://github.com/inhouseseo/superseo-skills) (Apache-2.0) — ideas only, written from scratch, and the source's own 20/40/60% severity tiers deliberately **not** adopted, since a percentage threshold with no absolute floor is actively wrong at B2B volumes. The click floor, the diagnosis grid, the AI-intercept row and its inference boundary, the site-wide-cause check, and the asymmetry guardrail are ours. Search Console behavior cited to [Google's Generative AI performance report (Search)](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/16984139) help page, read 2026-08-02, with a re-check note since metrics and availability change.
- **Reddit Specialist learns that a good answer can be the sentence an AI engine quotes** ([`social/social-reddit-specialist.md`](social/social-reddit-specialist.md), both dual-located copies) — the agent owned Reddit entirely through a community-credibility and traffic lens (every Critical Rule, deliverable, and success metric was about karma, upvotes, MQLs, and UTM traffic) and never mentioned AI engines, while the AEO/GEO Playbook and `seo-ai-search-optimizer` knew Reddit ≈ 6× G2 for AI citations but only advised "participate authentically" — so *AEO-driven thread targeting* was owned by neither. Resolves the seam as a section on the Reddit Specialist (a new prioritization lens on an activity it already performs, not a new persona). Adds an **"AEO-Aware Thread Targeting"** section, **Critical Rule 9**, and an `AI-Answer Presence (AEO)` success metric. Covers: the two different targeting questions (where buyers gather vs. which threads engines actually lift); a method for finding cited threads by querying the engines your buyers' questions logged-out and reversing from Perplexity's exposed sources; evergreen decision threads vs. decaying news threads; structuring an answer to be *liftable* not just upvoted (self-contained lead claim, named specifics over "it depends"); and guardrails keeping the 90/10 rule, no-astroturfing, human-posts-only, and citation-as-signal-not-target intact, with the per-engine strategy handed back to `seo-ai-search-optimizer`. Discipline surfaced by the open-source [onvoyage-ai/gtm-engineer-skills](https://github.com/onvoyage-ai/gtm-engineer-skills) `reddit-opportunity-research` (MIT) — ideas only, written from scratch; the 6× G2 and per-engine figures are directional and sourced in the AEO/GEO Playbook, and the [Reddit–Google content-licensing deal](https://www.tomsguide.com/ai/google-strikes-dollar60m-deal-with-reddit-for-ai-training-data-what-you-need-to-know) (Feb 2024, ~$60M/yr, terms since contested) is cited as the mechanism putting Reddit answers into Google's AI surfaces.
- **Marketing Ops Architect learns that consent decides which numbers are real** ([`analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md`](analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md), both dual-located copies) — the six-check GA4 instrumentation audit added on 2026-07-27 checks what happens to data *after* it arrives, and rested on an unaudited assumption that it arrived at all. Adds a **"Consent Before Collection: Separating Observed Data from Modeled and Missing"** section, **Critical Rule 9**, and a `Consent Coverage Transparency` success metric. Consent is the one failure in the measurement layer that is invisible by construction — a broken UTM leaves an *Unassigned* row, a mis-scoped dimension leaves an `(other)` bucket, but a consent gap leaves no error, no row, and no warning, just a smaller correctly-formatted number. Covers: the four consent signals and which two are measurement vs. advertising permissions; **basic vs. advanced implementation** as the choice that decides whether refused traffic is estimable or simply gone; the **B2B threshold trap** — GA4 models behavioral data only above ~1,000 denied events/day *and* ~1,000 consenting users/day, consumer-site volumes that a named-account B2B motion sits one to two orders of magnitude below, so the standing default is advanced mode's implementation cost with basic mode's coverage; an **observed / modeled / missing** decomposition where *unknown never rounds to observed* and "missing" is never read as "didn't happen"; three failure modes (**banner-change confound**, **ratios degrading worse than counts**, **global average hiding the regional hole**); the advertising half, where EEA consent gaps shrink Customer Match and retargeting audiences and get misdiagnosed as sync bugs; and an explicit boundary handing lawfulness to `ops-legal-compliance` and counsel. Dimension surfaced by the paywalled (**not adopted**) `/gtm-audit` in [cognyai/claude-code-marketing-skills](https://github.com/cognyai/claude-code-marketing-skills); written from scratch, all facts cited to Google's Tag Platform / Analytics Help / Google Ads Help documentation read 2026-08-01, with the B2B threshold conclusion labeled as our reasoning over Google's published requirements rather than a published figure.
- **Messaging Architect gets a *method* for its message-consistency audit** ([`product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md`](product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md), both dual-located copies) — Rule 6 already mandated a quarterly coherence audit across website, sales materials, product UI, email, case studies, ads, and content, and a `Message Consistency Score` metric graded it, but the rule *named the surfaces and stopped*: no method for what to compare, no priority order, no handling of the case that actually breaks consistency. Adds a **"Auditing Message Consistency: Surface Against Surface"** section built on the insight that the unit of a consistency audit is the *pair, not the page* — a set of surfaces can each be internally clean, on-brand, and correctly scored by `ops-quality-assurance` against the brand standard, and still tell three different stories. Three things the mandate was missing: (1) a **method** — read each high-priority pair of surfaces side by side on **four axes** (category claimed, primary value led with, audience addressed, proof cited), each a *substance* mismatch that only appears across surfaces, not a grammar or brand-voice problem; (2) **prioritize by visibility × stakes**, so a homepage-vs-pricing-page contradiction seen by every evaluator outranks a stale one-pager one rep emails twice a quarter; (3) a dedicated **post-repositioning straggler sweep** of the low-traffic corners that carry the *old* category and value after the loud surfaces get rewritten (footer boilerplate, About blurb, old blog CTAs, email signatures, canned sequences, help-center intros, social bios, datasheets), treated as part of the repositioning rather than a follow-up. Explicitly seamed against its two neighbours: `ops-quality-assurance` scores each surface against the brand standard *in isolation*; the Customer Language Bank diff compares copy to *buyers' words*; this method is the only one of the three that reads surfaces *against each other*. Rule 6 gains a pointer, a **Message Consistency Audit** deliverable is added, and the `Message Consistency Score` metric is upgraded to agreement on all four axes read surface-against-surface. Ideas-only, credited to the `message-consistency-check` skill in [`pmalliance/product-marketing-skills`](https://github.com/pmalliance/product-marketing-skills) (MIT); the four axes, the visibility × stakes triage, and the straggler sweep are our framing. No effect sizes or benchmark figures asserted.
- **Messaging Architect sources its vocabulary from customers before writing any** ([`product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md`](product-marketing/pmm-messaging-architect.md)) — Rule 1 sent you to customer research for what buyers *believe matters* and Rule 4 governed *proof*, but nothing anywhere owned where the **words** come from. Repo-wide, `buyer's own words`, `review mining`, `language bank`, and `word cloud` all returned zero hits, and every `their own words` hit was about *internal teams articulating positioning*. That is a hole in the agent's own logic, whose Identity already asserts that great messaging doesn't come from internal product knowledge — a message can be strategically correct and lexically foreign, and a buyer who doesn't recognize their own problem in your words never gets far enough to evaluate the argument. Adds a **"Sourcing the Vocabulary: Building a Customer Language Bank"** section and a **Critical Rule 9** (source the vocabulary before writing the message). Build the bank from every corpus where buyers describe their world unprompted — reviews, support tickets, call transcripts, churn reasons, survey free-text, community discussion — capturing verbatim phrasing, corpus, date, and speaker role, then **rank by frequency across the set**, because one customer's phrasing is an anecdote and the same construction from thirty is a finding, and a dozen hand-picked quotes cannot tell you which of two phrasings is the market's default. Then three disciplines the idea is usually shipped without: **weight each corpus by its bias before pooling** — public reviews are solicited and often incentivized (G2 permits incentives up to **$100 USD** and **labels confirmed incentivized reviews**, so segment on that tag rather than merging), support tickets are rich in problem vocabulary and nearly silent on value vocabulary, churn skews to the aggrieved, community is the least guarded but skews to practitioners, and **sales calls are the circular corpus** where the buyer adapts to the rep's framing and echoes your own terms back, so a phrase you already use counts as *unconfirmed* when it appears there; report frequency **per corpus as well as overall**, so a term ranking first only because one corpus is over-sampled is visible as an artifact of collection rather than a fact about the market. **Partition by committee role, not just frequency** — in B2B the reviewer is usually the *user* and the signer is the *economic buyer*, describing the same situation in different registers (the user says the workflow is clunky and eats twenty minutes a day; the CFO says the team is carrying headcount it shouldn't need), so review-mined language aimed at a homepage is user vocabulary pointed at a buyer; where a role is absent from every corpus, name the gap and go interview rather than borrow the user's words and hope. And **diff the bank against live copy with a verdict per gap** — **adopt** (their term is clearer and costs nothing strategically; most gaps), **bridge** (your term is strategically necessary, so lead with theirs and teach yours — a positioning decision executed in copy, coordinated with `pmm-positioning-strategist`), or **refuse** (**frequency is evidence, not a mandate**: a competitor's category name that frames you as their alternative, or a commodity label that erases your differentiation, can be the market's most frequent word and still lose you the argument before it starts — route these to positioning as findings, never to copy as instructions). Ships with a **Customer Language Bank and Copy Diff** deliverable and a **`Vocabulary Grounding`** success metric, plus a **never-manufacture-the-voice** guardrail: no composite quotes, no smoothing several real phrasings into one tidy line, no attributing a phrase to a persona because it sounds like them — a synthesized customer voice is a fabricated proof point under the same rule as an invented statistic, and paraphrase is fine if labeled as paraphrase. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the language-bank discipline credited in-file to [`pmalliance/product-marketing-skills`](https://github.com/pmalliance/product-marketing-skills) (MIT — the Product Marketing Alliance's own skill set), with the corpus-bias weighting, the buyer-vs-user partition, and the adopt/bridge/refuse rule marked as ours and not in the source. G2's incentive cap and labeling cited to [G2's Community Guidelines](https://legal.g2.com/community-guidelines), read 2026-07-31; the per-corpus skew characterizations are labeled methodological judgments, **not** measured figures — no percentages asserted. Both dual-located copies updated.
- **Quality Assurance Manager names its reference class before scoring copy** ([`client-ops/ops-quality-assurance.md`](client-ops/ops-quality-assurance.md)) — the Four U's rubric shipped earlier grades copy against *generic best practice*, the weakest bar available: a line can be rubric-perfect and still lose to the plainer line this brand's own audience already responded to. Repo-wide nothing tied a copy score to a data source (`reference class`, `score against`, `historical performance data` all returned zero hits in `client-ops/`). Adds a **"The Reference Class: What You're Scoring Against"** section built on a **three-tier data gate**, always labeling which tier the score used: (1) **this brand's own historical performance** — open/reply/click/conversion rates for *this* brand from `brand-context.md`'s "Proof we can actually cite" data or figures the user supplies, so an "adequate"-graded line that matches the pattern of this list's best sends beats a rubric-perfect line with no track record; (2) a **named, dated public benchmark** — cited source and date, never an unsourced "industry average" (an invented benchmark is a fabricated metric); (3) **generic principles only**, as an explicit last resort that must say so. The operative discipline: **the tier label is the deliverable, not a footnote** — "scored strong against this account's last 12 nurture sends (Tier 1)" and "scored strong on generic principles (Tier 3)" are different claims a reviewer must be able to tell apart, and every fix points at the data point behind it. Plus a **staleness trigger** (re-pull first-party data quarterly and after any material audience/offer/volume change; re-verify a benchmark's date and replace it when a newer edition publishes; a score against stale data is flagged, not trusted) and a **never-manufacture-the-comparison** guardrail tying back to the repo's no-fabricated-metrics rule. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the score-against-real-data discipline credited in-file to the `post-scorer` skill in `charlie947/social-media-skills` (MIT), re-aimed at B2B (the rest of that repo is creator-economy shaped and doesn't transfer). **No benchmark figures asserted** — the tiers describe where a number must come from, never what it is. Both dual-located copies updated.
- **Creative Strategist checks statistical power before declaring a verdict** ([`paid-media/paid-media-creative-strategist.md`](paid-media/paid-media-creative-strategist.md)) — the agent was testing-obsessed and rigour-shaped, but rested on an assumption that quietly fails in B2B: that the test can answer the question. Repo-wide, 95%-significance testing was prescribed in nine places while `underpowered` and `minimum detectable` appeared **zero** times — nothing handled the case a B2B SaaS account actually lives in, where the test cannot be powered at all. The agent's own success metrics were self-contradictory, promising "40%+ lift with 95% statistical confidence within 60 days" *and* "3-5 independent creative tests monthly," which at B2B conversion volumes are mutually exclusive. Adds a **"Power Before Verdict: Testing at B2B Volumes"** section and a **Critical Rule 9** (never run a test the account cannot power). It opens with the arithmetic — required sample scales with roughly the inverse square of the effect, so halving the MDE quadruples the traffic — and with LinkedIn's own conceded floor: **$3,000 lifetime budget per ad set** for lead-gen tests ($6,000 for one two-arm comparison), **14 days minimum / 21 recommended / 90 maximum**, and **p ≤ 0.1** treated as acceptable significance rather than the 95% the CRO literature assumes. Then: **pre-register** the MDE, stopping rule, and the assumptions inside the sample-size calculation *before* spend, because a decision rule written after the results is a story with numbers in it; **pick a metric at an altitude you can power** (CTR powers in days, cost-per-SQL frequently never — and a CTR winner is evidence about attention, not pipeline, since in B2B the creative that maximizes clicks reliably pulls the wrong job titles); rank tests by **effect size the change can plausibly produce** (offer > audience > concept > format > hook > visual > CTA wording), where the bottom of the list isn't merely low-leverage but *arithmetically untestable* — a CTA word swap that genuinely moves things 3% returns "no difference" every time in an account that can only detect 40%; **four outcomes, not two** (winner / loser / **inconclusive-underpowered**, read honestly as *no difference above the MDE you powered for* and never a quiet promotion of whichever arm leads / **invalid**, discarded and rerun rather than interpreted), with underpowered noise never rounding to a winner — the same *unknown-is-its-own-state* discipline the PPC strategist applies to account health; a six-option ladder for **when you can't power it** (raise the effect size, move up the funnel, pool across campaigns and quarters, holdout at account level, decide by structured judgment *labeled as judgment*, or don't test and spend the budget on reach); **concurrency and peeking** (parallel tests split finite traffic and lower each other's power; stopping early on a favourable read inflates false positives); and **log the MDE next to every result**, because "we could not detect a difference smaller than 35%" is reusable a year later and "no significant difference" is not. Rewrites the two contradictory success metrics and adds a `Test validity rate`. Ideas-only, written from scratch; pre-registration discipline credited in-file to `AgriciDaniel/claude-ads` (MIT), the variable-impact ordering to `borghei/Claude-Skills` (**MIT + Commons Clause** — a restrictive non-free condition, so ideas-only with the license flagged and no text reused). All figures cited to LinkedIn's own A/B testing documentation, read 2026-07-30. Both dual-located copies updated.
- **Marketing Ops Architect governs the field layer: ownership in the name, and a deprecation path** ([`analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md`](analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md)) — Critical Rule #3 assigned every field an owner in the data dictionary, but a dictionary is a lookup nobody opens at the moment they build a report or wire a sync, and it says nothing about how to *retire* a field. Repo-wide there was no field-prefix convention (`prefix` appeared in zero agents) and no field-deprecation lifecycle (the "deprecat" hits were unrelated — MMM tooling and email announcement templates). Adds a **"Governing the Field Layer"** section in two parts. **Ownership in the name:** prefix each custom field's API name with its owning function (`mkt_`/`sales_`/`cs_`/`fin_`/`ops_`, and a distinct `sys_` for integration-written fields no human should hand-edit), so ownership is legible at the point of use and the classic marketing-"Region"-vs-sales-"Region" collision is split into `mkt_region`/`sales_region` at creation instead of discovered in a broken report — with two constraints that make or break it (set the prefix *at creation*, because renaming an API name is itself a breaking change; and keep it a signpost *on top of* the dictionary's owner field, never a replacement). **A deprecation lifecycle, not a delete key:** because most CRMs let you delete a field without first telling you what depends on it — silently breaking every report, workflow, sync, and scoring rule that referenced it — fields get a four-step path out (mark deprecated with a `zz_deprecated_` prefix + dictionary status/date/owner → stop the writes → map dependents and hold a grace period → archive values, then remove), folded into the same quarterly audit as the data-quality review and logged as a versioned change (Rule #7). Closes by distinguishing deprecating a *field* (schema) from suppressing *records* (the fit/engagement "suppress from scoring" disposition), and by refusing to collapse the lifecycle into one step — the gap between "stop using this" and "this no longer exists" is exactly where dependents get found and moved. Ideas-only, written from scratch; surfaced by the `revops-data-governance` skill in `NEON-Rutger/B2B-revops-skills` (MIT), credited in-file. CRM behavior stated vendor-neutrally on purpose — field-deletion dependency handling and soft-delete windows differ across Salesforce/HubSpot and change over time, so the section says to verify the specific platform before removing any field in production. Both dual-located copies updated.
- **Lifecycle Architect gains an opt-down ladder as an alternative to the hard unsubscribe** ([`email/email-lifecycle-architect.md`](email/email-lifecycle-architect.md)) — the agent optimized frequency at the sender's discretion and mentioned a preference center in passing, but offered the subscriber only the binary all-or-nothing unsubscribe lever; repo-wide there was no opt-down ladder and "sunset" existed only in a *social* agent. Adds a **"The Opt-Down Ladder: A Structured Alternative to the Hard Unsubscribe"** section built as descending rungs, each **mapped to the concrete rule the ESP must enforce** (the operative discipline: a preference you *collect* but don't *honor* is worse than none — it earns the spam complaint you were avoiding): **frequency step-down** (weekly→monthly as an enforced send-cap segment, not a profile field no audience reads), **stream scoping** (per-topic subscription groups — HubSpot subscription types / Mailchimp groups / SFMC publication lists — keeping operational streams while marketing ones stay individually revocable), **pause/snooze** (dated suppression with a reactivation that actually fires), and **sunset** (a managed exit co-owned with the deliverability specialist, since mailing unengaged contacts depresses inbox placement for the whole list). Closes with a guardrail that the ladder **never obscures, delays, or gates the real unsubscribe** — a *pause* is a preference, not a legal opt-out, so a paused contact who clicks unsubscribe is still a full immediate opt-out — and a note that the choice must be enforced where every sending system reads it (the durable cross-tool consent record left as a separate backlog scope decision). Ideas-only, written from scratch; deliverability/compliance facts cited to primary sources (Gmail/Yahoo one-click unsubscribe processed within two days per [Google's sender guidelines](https://support.google.com/mail/answer/81126), CAN-SPAM's 10-business-day rule, GDPR Art. 7(3)), read 2026-07-30; pattern credited in-file to `aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills` (Apache-2.0), no text reused. Both dual-located copies updated.
- **AI Search Optimizer audits crawler access before it audits citability** ([`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md)) — the agent carried a sourced GEO field guide and a 0–100 citability rubric, both of which silently assumed the engine can fetch the page. Nothing in the repo checked that assumption: `GPTBot` appeared **nowhere**, and robots.txt showed up only in generic crawl-budget lines with no AI-agent awareness. Adds an **"Access Before Citation: Auditing AI Crawler Reachability"** section and a **Critical Rule 9** (never audit citability before auditing access). It separates the three jobs an "AI bot" can have — **training**, **retrieval indexing**, and **user-triggered fetch** — in a per-operator table, because blocking them costs completely different things: a *training* opt-out is a licensing decision, a *retrieval* block is a visibility decision, and the common failure is making the second by accident while intending the first. Names the three misreads that cost citations: blocking `Google-Extended` does **not** remove you from AI Overviews (Google states it doesn't affect Search inclusion or ranking; `noindex`/`nosnippet`/`max-snippet`/`data-nosnippet` are what do); blocking `GPTBot` does **not** hide you from ChatGPT search (that index is `OAI-SearchBot`); and robots.txt is **not** access control for user-triggered fetchers (OpenAI notes robots.txt may not apply to `ChatGPT-User`; Perplexity documents that `Perplexity-User` generally ignores it — anything that must not be fetched belongs behind auth). Then it audits three layers where access actually dies — **robots.txt** (most-specific-group matching, so a permissive wildcard rescues nothing), **the edge** (WAF/CDN bot rules returning 403 while robots.txt says allow — including Cloudflare's Search/Agent/Training classification and its 15 Sep 2026 defaults), and **render** (assume no JavaScript execution unless the operator documents otherwise; a capsule that only exists after hydration doesn't exist) — and closes by demanding **empirical verification from logs, not intent**: per-agent request counts, status-code mix and last-seen, reported as reachable / blocked / **never-seen**, with never-seen never rounding up to reachable and user agents spoof-checked against published IP ranges. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the surfacing sources credited in-file (MIT) with their own bot tiering explicitly **not** adopted where it contradicts vendor docs. Every agent behavior cited to its operator's own documentation, read 2026-07-30.
- **Marketing Ops Architect scores fit and engagement as two axes, not one number** ([`analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md`](analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md)) — the repo prescribed single-axis lead scoring (a 0–100 score with a 50+ handoff threshold; "MQL at 20+ points" in the CATALYST foundation playbook), listing engagement, demographic, firmographic and behavioral dimensions as inputs *summed into one number*. That shape lets engagement compensate for fit: a perfect-ICP VP who read two pages and a free-tier student who opened forty emails land on the same score, so sales works both, rejects one, and stops trusting the model — the actual mechanism behind "your leads are garbage." Adds a **"Scoring Fit and Engagement as Two Axes, Not One Number"** section: the gate is **AND, never SUM** (`Fit ≥ F AND Engagement ≥ E`, with both thresholds back-derived from your own closed-won history rather than adopted from anyone's published defaults); a **four-quadrant disposition table** giving each combination a different owner — high-fit/low-engagement is a *marketing* problem to nurture, low-fit/low-engagement is suppressed from scoring, and low-fit/high-engagement is **investigated, not routed**, via a monthly cluster review, because forty accounts in the same unexpected industry is an ICP finding rather than forty bad leads; **decay engagement, never decay fit** (fit is a property of the account — it changes on enrichment refresh and CRM field change, not on the calendar; a score that falls while nothing changed is what permanently discredits a model with reps); **recycled vs. disqualified** reason codes so a declined MQL lands somewhere reportable and feeds the model instead of vanishing; and **treat the model like production** — version it, never edit it live, tag scored records with the version that scored them, and judge a challenger on acceptance and conversion rather than score distribution. Ships with a **`Fit/Engagement Routing Precision`** success metric. Ideas-only, written from scratch; surfaced via `NEON-Rutger/B2B-revops-skills` (MIT), credited in-file, and that source's own MQL-acceptance improvement figures deliberately **not** reproduced. Platform behavior quoted from and cited to Adobe Experience League (Marketo's separate `Demographic Score` / `Behavior Score` programs) and HubSpot's Knowledge Base (its three-property fit/engagement/total model), read 2026-07-30; all thresholds and cadences flagged as calibration guidance, not benchmarks.
- **Discovery Coach learns transparency selling: disclose to disarm** ([`sales/sales-discovery-coach.md`](sales/sales-discovery-coach.md)) — the Coach was strong on what to *ask* (SPIN/MEDDPICC/Challenger et al.) but silent on what to proactively *volunteer*, and "transparency" existed repo-wide only as pricing/reporting transparency, never the selling methodology. Adds a **"Selling by Disclosure (Transparency Selling)"** section: volunteer your genuine limitations before the buyer pries them out (the trust-building twin of disqualification rigor, not a contradiction of it), because a spotless pitch reads as something hidden and buyers already assume imperfection. Five mechanics — **volunteer the flaw first**, **imperfection beats perfection** (ratings peak *slightly below* a perfect score — the Spiegel Research Center figure flagged directional), **order negatives by primacy/recency**, **pre-empt the objection you know is coming**, and **make pricing legible early** (handed off to the Proposal Architect) — scoped to the highest-trust-deficit deals and explicitly *not* license to dump every weakness. Ideas-only, written from scratch; framework attributed to Todd Caponi, *The Transparency Sale* (2018); surfaced via `LeadMagic/gtm-skills` (MIT), credited in-file. No numeric business claims fabricated.
- **Deal Strategist measures thread coverage as a state, not a headcount** ([`sales/sales-deal-strategist.md`](sales/sales-deal-strategist.md)) — the agent already told you to build 3–5 threads, but the only metric grading that work counted *stakeholders*, which rewards the exact failure it should catch: five threads can still be single-threaded on the economic buyer. Adds a **"Thread Coverage as a State, Not a Count"** block that grades each decision-critical role (economic buyer, champion, technical/security evaluator, procurement, known blocker) on three states — **Covered** (a live *first-hand* thread, not "my champion says finance is fine"), **Corroborated** (two independent sources, so one departure can't collapse the read — where champion risk lives), and **Current** (engaged inside the recent-activity window; a quiet thread is lapsed) — and defines *multi-threaded* as every critical role passing all three, re-graded at each stage gate rather than filled in once at Stage 3. Ships with a **`Buying-Committee Coverage`** success metric and reframes the existing "Stakeholder Engagement Score" as a proxy only. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the surfacing source `LeadMagic/gtm-skills` credited in-file (MIT); the signal-play trigger family from the same source was evaluated and found already covered by `sales-outbound-strategist`, so it was not duplicated.
- **Deal Strategist learns the third loss mode: buyer indecision** ([`sales/sales-deal-strategist.md`](sales/sales-deal-strategist.md)) — the agent modelled a lost deal as either *a competitor won* or *the buyer preferred the status quo*, which misses the largest category in late-stage enterprise pipeline: the buyer who wanted to change, agreed you were right, and never signed. Adds a section separating **indecision** (a *risk* problem — the buyer is already convinced and afraid of being personally wrong) from **status-quo preference** (a *value* problem), because the two take opposite plays and the cost-of-inaction reflex actively backfires on the first. The operational rule: **never escalate urgency on a stalled deal until you've diagnosed which mode you're in.** Then the four JOLT moves, adapted for multi-threaded deals — **judge the indecision and locate it** (champion's, economic buyer's, or a late skeptic's; graded low/medium/high with its evidence, since the stage model has no exit for a deal stuck at 85%), **offer a recommendation instead of options** (another comparison matrix is more surface area to be wrong about), **limit the exploration** (a fourth reference call is not an answer to anxiety), and **take risk off the table** by restructuring the downside — narrowed first phase, land-and-expand entry below the approval threshold, defined off-ramp, references matched to risk profile rather than logo value — all inside deal-desk, legal, and pricing-floor limits. Two additions are ours: **run the play through the champion** rather than around them, since they carry the most personal risk; and **classify every no-decision loss** by mode with evidence, plus a matching `No-Decision Loss Mix` success metric, so the biggest loss bucket stops being undifferentiated. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the JOLT framework attributed to Matthew Dixon & Ted McKenna (*The JOLT Effect*, 2022), the surfacing source credited in-file (MIT); statistics cited to two sources with a read-date, and the 84% figure flagged as inconsistently framed between them — direction evidenced, magnitude directional.
- **Marketing Ops Architect gains a web-analytics instrumentation audit (GA4)** ([`analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md`](analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md)) — the agent was thorough on CRM/MAP data quality and silent on the measurement layer feeding every downstream acquisition and campaign number. Adds a **read-only-by-default GA4 instrumentation audit** across six checks, each graded pass/needs-work/broken with *unknown never rounding up to pass*: **key-event configuration** (right events marked, reconciled to the MQL definition), **custom dimensions & metrics** within the standard-property quota (50 event-scoped / 25 user-scoped / 10 item-scoped custom dimensions, 50 custom + 5 calculated metrics) and correctly scoped, **PII in event parameters** as a fail-loud P0 against Google's explicit prohibition (with the Data-redaction remedy), **attribution model & lookback window** read from Admin and matched to the B2B sales cycle rather than assumed, **`(not set)` / *Unassigned* traffic** quantified and traced to cause, and **UTM → channel-grouping alignment** (case-sensitive reserved mediums, custom channel groups for legitimate exceptions). Ships with a *Web-Analytics Instrumentation Audit* deliverable folded into the weekly data-quality review. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the audit dimensions were surfaced by a paywalled (not adopted) third-party skill and one MIT source, both credited in-file; all limits, policy wording, and behavior cited to Google's own GA4 documentation with a 2026-07-27 read-date.
- **Quality Assurance Manager gains a copy-scoring rubric and an AI-tell screen** ([`client-ops/ops-quality-assurance.md`](client-ops/ops-quality-assurance.md)) — the agent was thorough on process (checklists, severity, legal, launch gates) but had no way to grade the *words*, so "copy quality" meant little more than spell-check. Adds a **Four U's scoring pass** (Useful / Ultra-specific / Unique / Urgent — each graded weak/adequate/strong with a diagnostic question and a fix), gated so a customer-facing headline or lead that grades weak on Useful *or* Ultra-specific goes back regardless of the other two. Plus an **AI-tell / banned-word screen** in two buckets: hollow modifiers that fail Ultra-specific by construction (*cutting-edge, world-class, seamless, robust, synergy, unlock, elevate…* — replace with the fact they stand in for, or cut) and machine-cadence tells (opener clichés, the "not just X, but Y" reflex, *delve/tapestry/realm/harness*, em-dash strings, a tacked-on "In conclusion"). The screen is explicitly a **flag, not a verdict** — it triggers a rewrite for concreteness, never an auto-reject or an authorship accusation, and reports no "percent-AI" figure. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the Four U's attributed to Michael Masterson / AWAI, the screen idea credited in-file to two MIT sources; no detection-accuracy claims.
- **Positioning Strategist gains a named-framework toolkit and a portfolio-first discipline** ([`product-marketing/pmm-positioning-strategist.md`](product-marketing/pmm-positioning-strategist.md)) — turns "do positioning" into a diagnosis-then-lens method instead of one favourite model applied to everything. Five canonical frameworks, each with an explicit *when to reach for it*: **Dunford's *Obviously Awesome*** five components for a mis-framed product (deepening the framework the deliverables already name); **Moore's *Crossing the Chasm*** beachhead / whole-product / reference-base moves when adoption stalls before the pragmatist majority; **Jobs-to-be-Done** (functional/emotional/social dimensions + the four forces of a switch) for the demand-side truth behind a purchase; **StoryBrand SB7** (customer-as-hero, brand-as-guide) as a top-of-funnel clarity discipline used to express a position, not find one; and **Blue Ocean's ERRC grid** to draw a divergent value curve out of a feature-parity category. Plus a **portfolio-before-message** rule: resolve company category → product-to-spine relationships → per-product buyer/job differences *before* any single product's positioning statement, so a growing SaaS doesn't accumulate contradictory site-wide narratives. Ideas-only, credited in-file to two MIT sources; frameworks attributed to their authors; no numeric claims.
- **Social Ads Specialist gains a creative-fatigue rule and a Meta Event Match Quality audit** ([`paid-media/paid-media-social-ads-specialist.md`](paid-media/paid-media-social-ads-specialist.md)) — the two slow leaks that erode a paid-social account without showing up in a single day's cost-per-lead. Creative fatigue is read as a **coordinated decline** (frequency climbing, first-time-impression ratio falling, CPM drifting up against flat/falling CTR, CVR dipping while CTR holds) with directional B2B refresh triggers (frequency ≥ 2.5 cold, 4–5 retargeting) and a fix framed as a queue, not a rescue — refresh hook/angle, one variable at a time, and tell creative fatigue apart from audience exhaustion. The **Event Match Quality audit** makes EMQ (Meta's out-of-10 match score) its own line item: run CAPI *alongside* the Pixel, send more matched parameters hashed to Meta's spec (`em`/`ph`/name/geo/`external_id` SHA-256; `client_ip_address`/`client_user_agent`/`fbc`/`fbp` un-hashed), deduplicate on a shared `event_id`+`event_name` inside Meta's 48-hour window, and target EMQ ≥ 7 on the primary conversion before scaling — under a hashed, consent-gated, aggregates-only PII boundary matching the email and PPC gates. Ideas-only, credited in-file to one MIT source; EMQ definition, parameter/hashing rules, and deduplication behaviour each cited to Meta's developer docs with a read-date. Numeric thresholds flagged directional.
- **PPC Strategist gains an evidence-graded audit, a spend-change gate, and a search-term loop** ([`paid-media/paid-media-ppc-strategist.md`](paid-media/paid-media-ppc-strategist.md)) — structure for the one discipline where being wrong costs money by the hour. Audits now score every check in **four states** (`pass`/`fail`/`unknown`/`not applicable`, where unknown never rounds to pass) and report **health and evidence coverage separately**, with graded / provisional / insufficient bands — below the bar you publish the missing access, not a score. Live-account changes pass a **blast-radius gate**: read-only by default, contained edits logged, budget/bid/targeting changes needing a before/after diff and approval inside a *written* ceiling, and structural or account-level changes needing explicit per-change approval — plus verify-state-before-write, pause-don't-remove, one variable per verification window, and a named rollback. Negative keywords become a recurring **search-term loop**: n-gram rollup → intent buckets → independent double-classification with disagreements routed to humans → a cross-level conflict check (negatives don't match close variants) → deliberate level choice inside Google's documented limits → gated apply → measure spend *and* conversions, so over-negation can't pass as optimization. Ideas-only, credited in-file to three MIT sources; negative-keyword behaviour and limits cited to Google Ads Help with a read-date.
- **AI Search Optimizer gains a measurement backbone** ([`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md)) — turns "optimize for AI citations" into a repeatable audit → monitor → benchmark loop: a **0–100 passage-citability score** whose weights track the GEO study's measured levers (quotations, statistics, outbound citations, extractable structure, author schema, freshness, minus a keyword-stuffing penalty); **citation-regression tests** that diff each page against a stored baseline and alert when a capsule, cited stat, schema, or held citation degrades; and an **AI Share-of-Voice heatmap** (queries × engines) that exposes per-engine gaps a single blended number hides. Composite weights flagged as directional editorial judgement, not a validated model; includes an honest-measurement note (sample each query N times, report frequency, never record a citation you didn't observe). Ideas-only, credited in-file to three MIT sources; effect sizes cited to Aggarwal et al. (KDD 2024).
- **Skill Scout — a second recurring job** ([`maintenance/SKILL_SCOUT.md`](maintenance/SKILL_SCOUT.md) + [`maintenance/scout-ledger.md`](maintenance/scout-ledger.md)): monitors GitHub/web for high-value marketing skills, compares them to our inventory, and either enhances an existing agent or adds a missing one — under a hard, ideas-only licensing guardrail. First pass evaluated 53 open-source sources → 7 enhance / 3 add-proposals / 5 already-have.
- **Attribution analyst gains a Bayesian-MMM measurement backbone**, and **the discovery coach gains a sales-methodology taxonomy** (MEDDPICC/SPIN/Challenger/Gap/Sandler/Value), both adapted ideas-only from open-source skills and credited in-file.
- **Marketing Automation Engineer gains a pre-send safety gate** ([`email/email-automation-engineer.md`](email/email-automation-engineer.md)) — the one place in this repo where an agent's mistake is unrecoverable. Classifies every send by blast radius (contained / segment / broadcast) with an escalating approval bar, sets ESP and CRM connections to **read-only by default** (write scopes opt-in per task, send never implied), and adds a 9-item pre-send checklist: audience resolved to a number, suppressions applied, cross-flow collision check, merge-field and link rendering, one-click-unsubscribe and lawful-basis compliance surface, SPF/DKIM/DMARC alignment and complaint-rate headroom, multi-client seed review, a named kill switch, and a warm-up ramp. Fails loud with `[NEEDS INPUT: …]` rather than sending on an assumption. Ideas-only, credited in-file; bulk-sender thresholds cited to Google's published sender guidelines.
- **README hero demo** ([`assets/catalyst-demo.svg`](assets/catalyst-demo.svg)) — an animated SVG showing the CATALYST
  orchestrator take one product-launch brief, load `brand-context.md` as Step 0, select CATALYST-Sprint mode, and
  route the work to named specialists across nine disciplines. Self-contained (no scripts, no external fonts or
  requests, ~8 KB), honours `prefers-reduced-motion`, and degrades to a complete, readable frame where CSS
  animation doesn't run. Every agent named in it is a real agent in this repo, and it is labelled an illustration
  rather than a recorded session.
- **Weekly competitive-intel loop** ([`loops/weekly-competitive-intel-loop.md`](loops/weekly-competitive-intel-loop.md)) — a
  fast weekly change-detection loop, distinct from the quarterly deep-dive the `pmm-competitive-intelligence` agent
  runs on its own. Three parallel sweeps (public sources, search surface, AI-answer citations) diff against last
  week's snapshot, then a **triage gate** assigns `watch` / `notify` / `act` severity, and only `act` items reach
  messaging and battle cards. Carries a **competitor watchlist + snapshot** between runs, requires a source URL and
  read-date on every claim, requires a second source or field corroboration before acting, sets an explicit
  public-sources-only ethical boundary (no fake trials, no NDA-fishing), and treats a quiet week as a valid outcome
  rather than manufacturing a finding.
- **Monthly ABM loop** ([`loops/monthly-abm-loop.md`](loops/monthly-abm-loop.md)) — the recurring counterpart to the
  one-shot ABM workflow. Seven steps across account re-scoring, buying-committee coverage, tiered messaging, asset
  refresh, multi-channel sequencing, measurement, and next-cycle planning. Carries a persistent **account ledger**
  between runs, so each month starts from what the last month learned; includes suppression rules for accounts in
  live sales conversations and a fabrication guard on account research.
- **Brand context template** ([`templates/brand-context.md`](templates/brand-context.md)) — a fill-in file capturing ICP,
  positioning, messaging pillars, citable proof, voice, terminology, and compliance constraints. The CATALYST
  orchestrator now loads it as Step 0 and passes it to every specialist, so output is tailored to your company —
  and agents may only assert facts recorded there, emitting `[NEEDS INPUT: …]` markers instead of inventing
  customers, metrics, or certifications. Shipped with the plugin at `plugins/saas-marketing/templates/`.

### Changed
- **AEO/GEO freshness pass — two now-settled Google facts corrected** ([`guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md`](guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md), both copies of [`seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`](seo/seo-ai-search-optimizer.md)). (1) **FAQ rich results** were described as "being removed in 2026"; they are now *gone* — rich results stopped appearing May 7 2026, the report and Rich Results Test support were dropped in June, and **Search Console API support ends August 2026** (the imminent date was unstated). `FAQPage` markup stays valid and Google still reads it, so the keep-the-structure advice is unchanged. (2) The **GSC Search Generative AI performance report** was grouped with Bing's report under "track citations," but Google's report is **impressions-only** (visibility in AI Overviews/AI Mode — no clicks, CTR, or query data) whereas **Bing Webmaster Tools' AI Performance report** reports actual **citations**; checklist item 15 now distinguishes them. Both facts cited to Google's own docs (read 2026-07-28); playbook `Last reviewed` bumped to 2026-07-28.
- **All 12 category skills now load `brand-context.md` first**, not just the CATALYST orchestrator. Invoking a
  skill directly (e.g. `/saas-marketing:seo-growth`) applies the same brand tailoring and the same
  anti-fabrication boundary as going through the orchestrator.
- **The weekly content engine loop now has a Step 0 brand-context block** ([`loops/weekly-content-engine-loop.md`](loops/weekly-content-engine-loop.md)),
  matching the ABM and competitive-intel loops — all three loops now load brand context first. Its fabrication
  guard is aimed at the risk specific to this loop: it rewards cited statistics and direct quotations, which is
  exactly where an agent will invent a number, misattribute a quote, or cite a page that doesn't say what the
  sentence claims. Every statistic and quotation must now name its source, link to it, and date the underlying
  data, and two new checklist items enforce that before publish.
- **[`integrations/README.md`](integrations/README.md) rewritten against current vendor docs**, which are now cited
  in a `Sources` section with a `Last reviewed` date. Adds the Claude Code plugin install as the primary path,
  a verified `--tool` → destination table for `scripts/install.sh`, and per-tool notes on rules formats
  (Cursor requires `.mdc`; Windsurf's current location is `.devin/rules/`; Copilot needs `NAME.agent.md`).

### Fixed
- **The flagship CATALYST orchestrator skill contradicted itself on how many disciplines it spans** — its opening paragraph said "80 specialized agents across **12 functional categories**" while three later lines in the same file (the `## 17 Disciplines & 80 Specialist Agents` table, its `Total: 17 Disciplines × 80 Specialist Agents` line, and the Full-Annual mode's "all 17 disciplines") plus the skill's own `description:` frontmatter said **17**. "12" is a legacy count from before the roster grew to its current 17 category directories. Corrected the stale intro to "17 disciplines" in [`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/catalyst-orchestrator/SKILL.md`](plugins/saas-marketing/skills/catalyst-orchestrator/SKILL.md) (the copy that loads into context when the orchestrator runs) and in the CATALYST framework overviews [`strategy/catalyst-strategy.md`](strategy/catalyst-strategy.md) (both dual-located copies, kept diff-identical) and [`strategy/EXECUTIVE-BRIEF.md`](strategy/EXECUTIVE-BRIEF.md) (whose own "The CATALYST Framework" list already said "80 Specialized Agents across 17 disciplines"). Agent count (80), skill count (19), and every public surface (About, README badges, `AGENTS_INDEX.md`, `llms.txt`) were already correct and unchanged.
- **Phase 4 launch playbook told B2B teams to turn on the Meta Audience Network for "broader reach"** ([`strategy/playbooks/phase-4-launch.md`](strategy/playbooks/phase-4-launch.md), both dual-located copies). The Meta campaign-setup section read `Audience network: Include Messenger, Audience Network (broader reach)` — stale on two counts. Terminologically, Meta renamed "automatic placements" to **Advantage+ placements**, which already include Facebook, Instagram, Messenger, WhatsApp, and the Audience Network ([Meta for Business](https://www.facebook.com/business/ads/meta-advantage-plus/placements), [Help Center](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/196554084569964)); Audience Network isn't a toggle you "include" alongside Messenger. Substantively, the line told a launching team to deliberately turn on the Audience Network — Meta ads served in third-party apps and sites, largely mobile games and entertainment ([Help Center](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/987564874649426)) — for reach, the exact placement `paid-media-social-ads-specialist` now teaches teams to audit and hold to its own lead-quality bar (delivery ≠ targeting, shipped 2026-08-03). Rewritten to name the current default, flag the Audience Network as consumer-app inventory to vet rather than free reach, and point at the delivery-vs-targeting audit. Verified against Meta's own documentation (read 2026-08-03), not the LinkedIn sources the specialist cites; no waste percentage asserted.
- **`scripts/install.sh` installed GitHub Copilot agents to a location Copilot never reads.** It preferred
  `$HOME/.github/agents/` — but `.github/agents/` is repository-scoped, and the personal-scope directory is
  `~/.copilot/agents/`. Files also kept a plain `.md` extension instead of the required `NAME.agent.md`.
  Both corrected, and Copilot detection no longer keys off an unrelated `$HOME/.github` directory.
- **Stale and incorrect integration instructions.** `claude <file>` / `claude --context <file>` (not real
  invocations), `aider --model claude-opus` (not a valid alias — `opus` is), `/edit` described as output
  iteration (it opens an editor to compose a prompt), the dead `codeium.com/windsurf` URL, `windsurf.dev` in the
  install script's help text, and a hardcoded "Claude Code: up to 200k tokens" context claim.

_Tracked in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md). This project is actively maintained — see the roadmap for what's next._

## [1.1.0] — 2026-07-21

### Added
- **Installable Claude Code plugin + marketplace.** The 13 skills now install natively:
  `/plugin marketplace add shalintripathi/saas-marketing-agents` then
  `/plugin install saas-marketing@saas-marketing-agents`. No more copy-paste required.
- **AEO/GEO Playbook** ([`guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md`](guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md)) — a sourced, practitioner-grade
  guide to Answer Engine Optimization and Generative Engine Optimization for B2B SaaS, grounded in 2026
  Google/Bing guidance and the GEO research literature.
- **`llms.txt`** at the repo root for AI-crawler discoverability.
- **Marketing loops** ([`loops/`](loops/)) — recurring-cadence, agent-run workflow templates (starting with a weekly content engine loop).
- Repo hygiene: `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `CITATION.cff`, `ROADMAP.md`, and this `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Changed
- **Rewrote the README** for discoverability and clarity: positioning, badges, a one-screen "what it is,"
  a 30-second quick start, an agent index table, and a star call-to-action.
- **Refreshed the AI Search Optimizer agent** with current AEO/GEO tactics (GEO effect sizes, Bing AI Performance
  reporting, entity/author signals, content-freshness cadence).
- Relocated the skill bundles from `cowork/` into `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/` to form the installable plugin.
  The `./scripts/install.sh --tool cowork` path still works and copies from the new location.
- Marked the repository as Markdown (not Shell) for accurate GitHub language statistics.

### Fixed
- Broken agent path referenced in the README, CONTRIBUTING, and integrations guide
  (`content-marketing/content-blog-writer.md` → `content/content-blog-strategist.md`).
- Corrected the documented agent frontmatter schema to match the real fields (`name` / `description` / `color` / `emoji`).

## [1.0.0] — 2026-04-02

### Added
- Initial release: 59 B2B SaaS marketing agent personas across 12 categories, the CATALYST orchestration
  framework, example workflows (ABM, product launch, demand gen, content engine), an integrations guide,
  and CI linting for agent files.
